# UI Registry

Living document. Update after every reusable component or established visual pattern is built.

## Before Building

1. Search this registry.
2. Reuse an existing component or exact pattern where possible.
3. Follow `ui-tokens.md` and `ui-rules.md`.
4. Add the component here after implementation.

## Entry Format

```text
### ComponentName
- Path:
- Purpose:
- Variants:
- Key tokens/classes:
- Accessibility behavior:
- Used by:
- Notes:
```

## Components

### TechnicalStepper (FMP-TECH-01 inline → extracted/made clickable FMP-TECH-05E, delocked FMP-TECH-05F, routing centralized FMP-TECH-05J)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/technical-stepper.tsx`
- Purpose: the ONE shared 4-step Technical workflow indicator (Drawing Received → SD & Calculation Submission → Getting Approval → FD Issuance) — every step a real clickable `<Link>` to that stage's own page, including stages not yet reached (this is an internal RECAFCO system — every stage is viewable anytime; see `computeStageMode`).
- Variants: `mode` per step (via `computeStageMode`) drives 3 visual states — completed = green circle + checkmark icon; current = filled accent pill (`bg-accent-light` background) with a larger bold-accent circle/label; future = plain muted numbered circle, the SAME shape as any other step, just muted (FMP-TECH-05F removed the `Lock` icon FMP-TECH-05E had used here — an internal company workflow shouldn't look "forbidden"). No distinct "Returned/Reopened" visual — a reverted stage just reads as "current" again.
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-success`/`bg-accent`/`bg-surface-secondary` per state (same 3-color scheme every other stepper/badge in this module already uses); `Check` (lucide-react) only — no other icon per step.
- Accessibility behavior: `<ol>` of `<li>` each wrapping a real `<Link>` — ordered-list semantics plus genuine keyboard/screen-reader-navigable links (not `<span>`s pretending to be clickable). Each link carries a `title` attribute ("Stage Name — Completed/Current/Pending") so the state is available to assistive tech/tooltips without adding visible text that would bloat this compact bar (FMP-TECH-05F).
- Used by: all 4 Technical stage pages (Drawing Received, SD & Calculation Submission, Getting Approval, FD Issuance) and the workflow overview page (`jobs/[contractId]/page.tsx`).
- Notes: server-renderable (no `'use client'` needed — it's just links). Reads `computeStageMode()` and (FMP-TECH-05J) `stageHref()` from `_lib/technical-format.ts` — the same 2 helpers every stage form's footer now also uses for its own "Open `<Stage>`" navigation links, replacing a `STAGE_SLUGS` map that used to be declared separately in this file and in the overview page. The mode's 3rd value is still called `'locked'` internally in code (pure implementation detail) — every user-facing label now says "Pending", never "Locked".

### TechnicalChecklist (FMP-TECH-01, simplified FMP-TECH-05C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/drawing-received/_components/technical-checklist.tsx`
- Purpose: the Drawing Received screen's Workflow Panel "Checklist" sub-section — 4 items, each computed live from the real saved `TechnicalDrawing`/attachment rows (received date recorded / files uploaded / reference+revision captured / ready for next stage) — never a hardcoded "already complete" state.
- Variants: none.
- Key tokens/classes: `Check`/`Circle` (lucide-react) per item, `text-success`/`text-text-muted`. No longer renders its own outer card/heading as of FMP-TECH-05C — it's a bare `<ul>` now, nested inside the page's own combined "Workflow Panel" card.
- Accessibility behavior: plain `<ul>`, no special ARIA needed (static content, not interactive).
- Used by: Drawing Received screen — its only consumer (confirmed via search before simplifying it in FMP-TECH-05C).
- Notes: server-renderable. Reusable pattern for any future Technical stage screen that wants the same "checklist reflects real data" treatment (would need its own outer card if used standalone elsewhere, since this file no longer provides one).

### TechnicalActivityTimeline (FMP-TECH-01, extended FMP-TECH-05C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/drawing-received/_components/technical-activity-timeline.tsx`
- Purpose: real `TechnicalWorkflowActivity` rows rendered as a timeline (Drawing package received / Files uploaded / Details saved / Clarification requested / Step completed, etc.) — mirrors `IncidentActivity`'s own timeline shape/mechanism.
- Variants: `bare?: boolean` (FMP-TECH-05C, default `false`) — when true, skips the outer `rounded-lg border ... p-4` card and the "Activity Timeline" heading, for nesting inside Drawing Received's combined "Workflow Panel". Every other call site (SD & Calculation, Getting Approval, FD Issuance) doesn't pass it, so those 3 pages are unaffected. Empty state ("No activity recorded yet.") when the list is empty, in either variant.
- Key tokens/classes: small `bg-accent` dot marker per entry, `text-text-primary`/`text-text-muted` for event/actor+timestamp lines.
- Accessibility behavior: `<ol>` (chronological sequence).
- Used by: all 4 Technical stage screens (Drawing Received passes `bare` + a `.slice(0, 3)`'d activity list as of FMP-TECH-05B/05C; the other 3 pass the full list with no `bare`).
- Notes: server-renderable. `EVENT_LABELS` in this file is the one place technical-workflow event codes are translated to display text — extend it (not a new component) when a future stage adds new event codes.

### DrawingAttachmentsPanel (FMP-TECH-01, polished FMP-TECH-05B)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/drawing-received/_components/drawing-attachments-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: real file upload/list/delete for a Technical Drawing intake record — a deliberately simpler sibling of `IncidentEvidenceAttachments` (plain multi-file `<input>`, no camera/video staging picker — Technical Drawing attachments are documents/images/CAD files, not incident evidence).
- Variants: `canUpload`/`canManage` gate the upload control and each row's Delete action (uploader-or-`contracts.manage`, same ownership rule `IncidentAttachment` uses). Empty state (FMP-TECH-05B): when `canUpload`, a single compact dashed strip holds both the "No files uploaded yet." text and the upload control side by side, replacing the old stacked "text line above a separate full-width dashed box" (the ticket's own "overly large blank attachment box" complaint).
- Key tokens/classes: same list-row shape as `IncidentEvidenceAttachments` (`Paperclip` icon, filename/size/uploader/date line, `Download`/`X` actions).
- Accessibility behavior: file input is a visually-hidden (`sr-only`) native `<input type="file">` behind a styled `<label>`; errors rendered with `role="alert"`.
- Used by: Drawing Received screen.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Backed by a real local-disk storage service (`technical-attachment-storage.service.ts`, mirrors `IncidentAttachmentStorageService`) — not a placeholder/disabled state, since this codebase's existing attachment infrastructure could be safely reused.

### DrawingReceivedForm (FMP-TECH-01, redesigned FMP-TECH-05B, polished FMP-TECH-05C, simplified FMP-TECH-05D, mode-aware FMP-TECH-05E, de-floated FMP-TECH-05F, navigation-clarified FMP-TECH-05J, internal-scroll workspace FMP-TECH-05N, reverted to single page scroll FMP-TECH-05O)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/drawing-received/_components/drawing-received-form.tsx`
- Purpose: the Drawing Received screen's main form — Card 1 "Drawing Receipt Details", Card 2 "Drawing Information" + Card 3 "Attachments" (via `DrawingAttachmentsPanel`) side by side on desktop (FMP-TECH-05C), Card 4 "Remarks & Follow-up" — plus the footer's 2 distinct actions (Save Draft / Complete Drawing Receipt & Continue). Request Clarification was removed from this screen's UI in FMP-TECH-05D (the flow doesn't yet show who receives it/owns it/where it's tracked) — the backend action/endpoint is untouched, only this screen stopped calling it.
- Variants: takes a `mode: TechnicalStageMode` prop (FMP-TECH-05E, replacing the old `isCurrentStage: boolean`) and (FMP-TECH-05J) a `currentStage: TechnicalStage` prop — `disabled` is `!canWrite || mode !== 'active' || isPending`. `mode === 'completed'` shows a "This stage is completed. Showing the saved data below in read-only mode." banner and hides the Save Draft/Complete buttons; footer's right side instead shows an "Open `<Current Stage>`" link (FMP-TECH-05J) when the workflow has moved on. Drawing Received is the first stage so `mode` is never `'locked'` here (nothing precedes it) — it still types the prop as the full `TechnicalStageMode` union for consistency with the other 3 stage forms.
- Key tokens/classes: `SectionCard` (local to this file — `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-3.5 shadow-sm`, tightened `p-5` → `p-4` in FMP-TECH-05B → `p-3.5` in FMP-TECH-05C). Card 1's grid is `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3`; Card 2's inner grid is `sm:grid-cols-2`. Footer action bar reverted in FMP-TECH-05F from `sticky bottom-0` (with a `border-t-2 border-t-accent` top line + drop shadow, added FMP-TECH-05B/05C — it covered part of the form and read as an error strip) to a normal in-flow `border-t border-border pt-4` block, the exact same shape the other 3 stage forms already use: Left: Back to Workflow / Right: Save Draft, Complete (primary). `INPUT_CLS` gained `disabled:*` classes in FMP-TECH-05J (dimmed background/text/cursor) so a read-only/disabled field is visibly distinct, not just relying on native browser dimming.
- Accessibility behavior: every input has an associated `<label htmlFor>`; validation/success messages use `role="alert"`/`role="status"`.
- Used by: Drawing Received screen only, today.
- Notes: `'use client'`. One shared `<form>` (not separate forms) with `useTransition`-wrapped handlers, each building its own `FormData` snapshot from the same form ref and calling a different (unchanged) server action — chosen over `useActionState` (which binds to exactly one action) since this screen needs more than one server call from the same field state. `SectionCard`/`Field`/`INPUT_CLS` are local to this file, not yet extracted. No field, validation, or the 2 remaining actions' wiring changed across FMP-TECH-05B through 05J — only markup/spacing/grouping/mode-plumbing. FMP-TECH-05N briefly split the root into a `lg:`-scoped scrollable-cards region + a DOM-sibling pinned-footer region (stacking 2–3 independent scroll areas on one page); FMP-TECH-05O reverted that — back to one `space-y-5` wrapper and a single `<form>` whose action-button footer is its own last child again, exactly the FMP-TECH-05F shape.

### NeedsAttentionPanel (FMP-TECH-01C, polished FMP-TECH-05, Open Stage link FMP-TECH-05P)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/needs-attention-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: the Technical Dashboard's manager-facing "Needs Attention" list — 8 real conditions (overdue planned review, urgent priority, Drawing Received left uncompleted, unresolved clarification request, stuck too long at Getting Approval, overdue target/expected approval date, overdue FD Issue Date), each computed server-side by `TechnicalService.getDashboard()`; never a fabricated/sample warning.
- Variants: empty state now a success-tinted "on track" card with a `CheckCircle2` icon ("All Technical workflow items are currently on track.") instead of a plain gray box.
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-warning-light`/`border-warning/40` row, `AlertTriangle` icon, a per-item stage badge (using the item's own `stage` field and the shared `stageBadgeClasses()` helper), a small bordered "Open Stage" button (FMP-TECH-05P, was "Open" — relabeled for consistency with the jobs table's own primary action).
- Accessibility behavior: plain `<ul>`; each row's button has clear text ("Open Stage"), not a bare icon.
- Used by: Technical Dashboard (`/technical`).
- Notes: server-renderable. `REASON_LABELS` in this file is the one place attention-reason codes are translated to display text. `TechnicalAttentionItem.stage` was added to the API response shape in FMP-TECH-05 so this panel could show a stage badge without a second lookup — FMP-TECH-05P also uses it to build the "Open Stage" link's `href` directly via the shared `stageHref()` helper (was `/technical/jobs/:id`, the workflow overview — now goes straight to the stage that actually needs attention).

### RecentActivityPanel (FMP-TECH-01C, polished FMP-TECH-05, limited to 5 FMP-TECH-05P)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/recent-activity-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: the Technical Dashboard's cross-job activity feed — real `technical_workflow_activities` rows across every in-scope job order, newest first, each showing job/stage/actor/timestamp.
- Variants: empty state ("No Technical activity recorded yet."). Each entry's connector dot is tinted success/warning/neutral depending on whether the event represents a positive outcome (e.g. a stage completed), a warning-type event (rejection, clarification, changes requested), or a routine save.
- Key tokens/classes: a connected timeline (dot + vertical line between entries), matching the same visual language `TechnicalActivityTimeline` already uses on each stage's own screen — one row per activity across every job, with a stage badge (shared `stageBadgeClasses()` helper) and an "Open" link into that job's workflow overview.
- Accessibility behavior: plain `<ol>`.
- Used by: Technical Dashboard.
- Notes: server-renderable. Shares its own local `EVENT_LABELS` map with `TechnicalActivityTimeline`'s (duplicated, not imported — both files are small and independently readable). The component itself is unchanged in FMP-TECH-05P — the 5-item limit is applied by the PAGE (`page.tsx` passes `dashboard.recentActivities.slice(0, 5)`, backend still returns up to 10 unchanged), with a "Showing latest 5 activities" note rendered by the page when more exist (no "view all activity" page/modal exists to link to instead).

### StageProgressOverview (FMP-TECH-01C, polished FMP-TECH-05, total count FMP-TECH-05P)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/stage-progress-overview.tsx`
- Purpose: a compact numbered 4-step "how many jobs are at each stage" indicator for the Technical Dashboard — built from `stageBreakdown`, the exact same per-stage counts the KPI cards above it already show (no separate query, no invented distribution).
- Variants: empty state when `total === 0` ("No active job orders across the 4 Technical stages yet."), replacing the old always-rendered (and always-empty-looking) 0%-width bars.
- Key tokens/classes: a "Total active workflows" count line (FMP-TECH-05P, the same `total` the component already computed — no new data) sits above the 4 rows; each row shows a numbered circle badge, the stage label, count + share percentage, and a `h-1.5` bar (tightened from `h-2` in FMP-TECH-05P) tinted per-stage via the shared `stageBarClasses()` helper (same 4 colors the jobs table's stage badges use, from `_lib/technical-format.ts`).
- Accessibility behavior: plain elements with visible numeric labels (count/percentage shown as text, not conveyed by bar width alone).
- Used by: Technical Dashboard, in a 2-column row beside the new `NextActionPanel`.
- Notes: server-renderable.

### RefreshButton (FMP-TECH-01C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/refresh-button.tsx`
- Purpose: a plain "Refresh" action for the Technical Dashboard's header — calls `router.refresh()` to re-run the page's own Server Component data fetch; no new endpoint, no client-side state duplicated from the server response.
- Variants: none.
- Key tokens/classes: matches this app's standard secondary-button style (`border border-border bg-surface ... hover:bg-surface-secondary`); `Loader2`/`RefreshCw` (lucide-react) swap while pending.
- Accessibility behavior: `disabled` while a refresh is in flight (prevents duplicate triggers).
- Used by: Technical Dashboard.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Small and generic enough to reuse verbatim on any other Server Component page that wants a manual-refresh action.

### SdCalculationSubmissionForm (FMP-TECH-02, mode-aware FMP-TECH-05E, redesigned FMP-TECH-05H, navigation-clarified FMP-TECH-05J, made editable-anytime FMP-TECH-05K, all actions always shown FMP-TECH-05L, internal-scroll workspace FMP-TECH-05N, reverted to single page scroll FMP-TECH-05O)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/sd-calculation-submission/_components/sd-calculation-submission-form.tsx`
- Purpose: the SD & Calculation Submission screen's main form — Card 1 "Submission Core Details", Card 2 "Linked Drawing & Technical Scope", Card 3 "Attachments & Remarks" (attachments left, Remarks + a collapsed "Contact details" `<details>` disclosure right) — plus the footer's 3 distinct actions (Save Draft / Submit SD & Calculation / Complete & Move to Getting Approval). Request Clarification was removed from this screen's UI in FMP-TECH-05H (same reasoning as Drawing Received's own removal in FMP-TECH-05D) — the backend action/endpoint is untouched.
- Variants: takes `mode: TechnicalStageMode` (FMP-TECH-05E) and a `currentStage: TechnicalStage` prop (FMP-TECH-05J) — `disabled` is `!canWrite || mode === 'completed' || isPending` (FMP-TECH-05K, was `mode !== 'active'`): RECAFCO is an internal system, so a `'locked'` (not-yet-reached) stage is now just as editable as `'active'` — only a genuinely `'completed'` (already-passed) stage stays read-only. The FMP-TECH-05J top-of-page locked banner and its "Open Drawing Received" footer link were both removed in FMP-TECH-05K; in their place, a small right-aligned `text-xs text-text-muted` note sits directly above the footer, shown only when `mode === 'locked'` ("Completion will require Drawing Received to be completed."). Footer now shows all 3 actions (Save Draft, Submit SD & Calculation, Complete & Move to Getting Approval) together whenever `mode !== 'completed'` (FMP-TECH-05L — Submit was previously active-only; every one of the 3 is independently backend-gated regardless of what the UI shows, so hiding Submit added no real protection, only an inconsistency next to the always-shown Complete button). `mode === 'completed'` still shows "This stage is completed. Showing the saved data below in read-only mode." and an "Open `<Current Stage>`" link, unchanged from FMP-TECH-05J.
- Key tokens/classes: `SectionCard` (local, `p-3.5 shadow-sm space-y-3`, tightened from `p-5` in FMP-TECH-05H to match `DrawingReceivedForm`). Card 1's grid is `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3`; Card 2's grid is the same, with Scope/Description as a full-width textarea below it. Submitted By/Designation/Contact No/Email moved into a `<details>` "Contact details (optional)" block inside Card 3 — still real, unrenamed form fields (still submit via `FormData` when active, expanded or not). The "Related Drawing Received" field is a real `<select>` populated from `completedDrawings` (real completed `TechnicalDrawing` rows for this workflow only) — never free text, never another contract's records. `INPUT_CLS` keeps its `disabled:*` classes from FMP-TECH-05J (now only visible in the genuinely-`completed` case).
- Accessibility behavior: every input has an associated `<label htmlFor>`; validation/success messages use `role="alert"`/`role="status"`; `<details>`/`<summary>` is natively keyboard-operable with no extra ARIA needed.
- Used by: SD & Calculation Submission screen only, today.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Same "one shared form, several `useTransition`-wrapped handlers" mechanism as `DrawingReceivedForm` — 3 handlers here (was 4; Request Clarification's handler removed in FMP-TECH-05H, matching Drawing Received's own FMP-TECH-05D precedent). "Open `<Current Stage>`" (completed case only) uses the shared `stageHref()`/`STAGE_OPEN_LABELS` from `_lib/technical-format.ts` (FMP-TECH-05J). `SdAttachmentsPanel`'s `canUpload`/`canManage` relaxed from `isActive`/`mode !== 'locked'` to `mode !== 'completed'` in FMP-TECH-05K — upload/delete were never actually stage-gated server-side (`createSdAttachment`/`deleteSdAttachment` have no stage assertion), so this only removes a frontend-only over-restriction. The backend's own draft-save gate (`saveSdSubmissionDraft`) was relaxed in lockstep — see `technical.service.ts`'s `assertSdStageNotAlreadyPassed()` (FMP-TECH-05K). FMP-TECH-05N briefly applied the same internal-scroll-plus-pinned-footer split as `DrawingReceivedForm`; FMP-TECH-05O reverted it back to one normal flow.

### SdAttachmentsPanel (FMP-TECH-02, polished FMP-TECH-05H)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/sd-calculation-submission/_components/sd-attachments-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: real file upload/list/delete for an SD & Calculation Submission record — deliberately a near-duplicate of `DrawingAttachmentsPanel` (not a shared cross-stage import) to keep each stage screen's attachment UI independently readable/modifiable.
- Variants: `canUpload`/`canManage` gate the upload control and each row's Delete action (uploader-or-`contracts.manage`). Empty state (FMP-TECH-05H, matching `DrawingAttachmentsPanel`'s own FMP-TECH-05B pattern): when `canUpload`, one compact dashed strip holds "No SD/calculation files uploaded yet." + the upload control + a small accepted-file-types line, instead of a stacked text line + separate large dashed box.
- Key tokens/classes: identical list-row shape to `DrawingAttachmentsPanel`.
- Accessibility behavior: same as `DrawingAttachmentsPanel` — visually-hidden native file input behind a styled label; errors via `role="alert"`.
- Used by: SD & Calculation Submission screen.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Backed by the SAME `TechnicalAttachmentStorageService` Drawing Received uses (a new `technical_sd_submission_attachments` metadata table, no new storage code) — reuses the same allowed-type/size constants too.

### GettingApprovalForm (FMP-TECH-03, mode-aware FMP-TECH-05E, simplified copy FMP-TECH-05F, navigation-clarified FMP-TECH-05J, made editable-anytime FMP-TECH-05K, all actions always shown + Request Clarification removed FMP-TECH-05L, internal-scroll workspace FMP-TECH-05N, reverted to single page scroll FMP-TECH-05O)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/getting-approval/_components/getting-approval-form.tsx`
- Purpose: the Getting Approval screen's main form — Approval Information, Approval Attachments (via `ApprovalAttachmentsPanel`) — plus the footer's 4 distinct actions (Save Draft / Send Back for Changes / Approve & Move to FD Issuance / Reject). Request Clarification was removed from this screen's UI in FMP-TECH-05L (same reasoning Drawing Received/SD already used to remove theirs — the flow doesn't yet show who receives it/owns it/where it's tracked) — the backend action/endpoint (`requestApprovalClarificationAction` / `POST .../clarification`) is untouched, only this screen's UI wiring to it was removed.
- Variants: takes `mode: TechnicalStageMode` (FMP-TECH-05E, replacing `isCurrentStage: boolean`) and a `currentStage: TechnicalStage` prop (FMP-TECH-05J) — `disabled` is `!canWrite || mode === 'completed' || isPending` (FMP-TECH-05K, was `mode !== 'active'`) — same "editable in both active and not-yet-reached, read-only only once genuinely completed" rule as `SdCalculationSubmissionForm`. The FMP-TECH-05J locked banner and its "Open SD & Calculation" footer link were removed in FMP-TECH-05K; a small note above the footer replaces them when `mode === 'locked'` (FMP-TECH-05L wording: "Approval completion will require SD & Calculation submission to be completed."). Footer now shows all 4 remaining actions together whenever `mode !== 'completed'` (FMP-TECH-05L — Send Back for Changes/Approve/Reject were previously active-only; every one is independently backend-gated regardless of what the UI shows, and a raw `assertApprovalStageIsCurrent()` rejection sitting next to still-visible buttons was the exact "confusing warning" the ticket reported — reworded to read as ordinary guidance instead, see `technical.service.ts`). `mode === 'completed'` still shows "This stage is completed. Showing the saved data below in read-only mode." and an "Open FD Issuance" link, unchanged from FMP-TECH-05J.
- Key tokens/classes: identical local `SectionCard`/`Field`/`INPUT_CLS` pattern to the prior 2 stage forms. Reject's button triggers a native `window.confirm()` before calling its server action (the ticket's own "requires confirmation") — the only one of this app's Technical form actions to do so.
- Accessibility behavior: every input has an associated `<label htmlFor>`; validation/success messages use `role="alert"`/`role="status"`.
- Used by: Getting Approval screen only, today.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Same "one shared form, several `useTransition`-wrapped handlers" mechanism as the prior 2 stage forms — 4 handlers here (was 5; Request Clarification's handler removed in FMP-TECH-05L). "Open FD Issuance" (completed case only) uses the shared `stageHref()`/`STAGE_OPEN_LABELS` from `_lib/technical-format.ts` (FMP-TECH-05J). `ApprovalAttachmentsPanel`'s `canUpload`/`canManage` relaxed to `mode !== 'completed'` in FMP-TECH-05K (upload/delete were never stage-gated server-side). The backend's own draft-save gate (`saveApprovalDraft`) was relaxed in lockstep — see `assertApprovalStageNotAlreadyPassed()` in `technical.service.ts` (FMP-TECH-05K). FMP-TECH-05N briefly applied the same internal-scroll-plus-pinned-footer split as `DrawingReceivedForm`; FMP-TECH-05O reverted it back to one normal flow.

### ApprovalAttachmentsPanel (FMP-TECH-03)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/getting-approval/_components/approval-attachments-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: real file upload/list/delete for a Getting Approval record (approved drawing, consultant comments, marked-up drawing, approval letter/email, rejected/commented file — the ticket's own real-world examples, never hardcoded).
- Variants: `canUpload`/`canManage` gate the upload control and each row's Delete action.
- Key tokens/classes: identical list-row shape to `DrawingAttachmentsPanel`/`SdAttachmentsPanel`.
- Accessibility behavior: same as the other 2 attachment panels.
- Used by: Getting Approval screen.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Backed by the SAME `TechnicalAttachmentStorageService` the other 2 stages use (a new `technical_approval_attachments` metadata table only).

### FdIssuanceForm (FMP-TECH-04, mode-aware FMP-TECH-05E, simplified copy FMP-TECH-05F, navigation-clarified FMP-TECH-05J, made editable-anytime FMP-TECH-05K, all actions always shown FMP-TECH-05L, internal-scroll workspace FMP-TECH-05N, reverted to single page scroll FMP-TECH-05O)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/fd-issuance/_components/fd-issuance-form.tsx`
- Purpose: the FD Issuance screen's main form (Technical Stage 4, the final stage) — FD Issuance Information, Issued By, FD Attachments (via `FdAttachmentsPanel`), a Return/Reopen reason field — plus the footer's 4 distinct actions (Save Draft / Submit FD Issue / Return-Reopen / Issue FD & Complete Technical Workflow).
- Variants: takes `mode: TechnicalStageMode` (FMP-TECH-05E, replacing `isCurrentStage: boolean`) and a `currentStage: TechnicalStage` prop (FMP-TECH-05J) — `disabled` is `!canWrite || mode === 'completed' || isPending` (FMP-TECH-05K, was `mode !== 'active'`) — same rule as the other 2 stage forms. The FMP-TECH-05J locked banner and its "Open Getting Approval" footer link were removed in FMP-TECH-05K; a small note above the footer replaces them when `mode === 'locked'` (FMP-TECH-05L wording: "FD completion will require Getting Approval to be approved."). Footer now shows all 4 actions together whenever `mode !== 'completed'` (FMP-TECH-05L — Submit FD Issue/Return-Reopen were previously active-only; every one is independently backend-gated regardless of what the UI shows). `mode === 'completed'` (which for this final stage means `workflow.status === COMPLETED`, since `currentStage` never advances past `FD_ISSUANCE`) still shows the read-only banner alongside the page's own richer "Technical Workflow Completed" banner, unchanged from FMP-TECH-05J.
- Key tokens/classes: identical local `SectionCard`/`Field`/`INPUT_CLS` pattern to the prior 3 stage forms. Return/Reopen requires its own reason field to be filled AND triggers a native `window.confirm()` before calling its server action (this stage's action reverts the whole workflow to Getting Approval, the highest-consequence action in this form).
- Accessibility behavior: every input has an associated `<label htmlFor>`; validation/success messages use `role="alert"`/`role="status"`.
- Used by: FD Issuance screen only, today.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Same "one shared form, several `useTransition`-wrapped handlers" mechanism as the prior 3 stage forms — 4 handlers here. "Issue FD & Complete Technical Workflow" is the only completion action across all 4 Technical stages that does not advance `currentStage` (there is no later stage) — it marks the whole `TechnicalWorkflow` completed instead. `FdAttachmentsPanel`'s `canUpload`/`canManage` relaxed to `mode !== 'completed'` in FMP-TECH-05K (upload/delete were never stage-gated server-side). The backend's own draft-save gate (`saveFdIssuanceDraft`) was relaxed in lockstep — see `assertFdStageNotAlreadyCompleted()` in `technical.service.ts` (FMP-TECH-05K), which checks `workflow.status === COMPLETED` only (FD Issuance has no later stage to have "advanced past"). FMP-TECH-05N briefly applied the same internal-scroll-plus-pinned-footer split as `DrawingReceivedForm`; FMP-TECH-05O reverted it back to one normal flow, `handleReturnOrReopen()`'s `returnReason` lookup unaffected either way since that field has always stayed inside `<form>`.

### FdAttachmentsPanel (FMP-TECH-04)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/jobs/[contractId]/workflow/fd-issuance/_components/fd-attachments-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: real file upload/list/delete for an FD Issuance record (final drawing PDF, DWG file, document package, transmittal/issue letter — the ticket's own real-world examples, never hardcoded).
- Variants: `canUpload`/`canManage` gate the upload control and each row's Delete action.
- Key tokens/classes: identical list-row shape to `DrawingAttachmentsPanel`/`SdAttachmentsPanel`/`ApprovalAttachmentsPanel`.
- Accessibility behavior: same as the other 3 attachment panels.
- Used by: FD Issuance screen.
- Notes: `'use client'`. Backed by the SAME `TechnicalAttachmentStorageService` the other 3 stages use (a new `technical_fd_issuance_attachments` metadata table only). Completing this stage requires at least one attachment (same fallback rule as the SD stage's own completion).

### TechnicalKpiCard (FMP-TECH-05, compacted + 3-color accent scheme FMP-TECH-05P)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/technical-kpi-card.tsx`
- Purpose: a Technical-Dashboard-only KPI tile — icon chip + tint + colored top accent bar + count + label + short helper text (e.g. "Not yet started", "Awaiting approval decision") — polished replacement for the plain `MetricCard` usage the dashboard had before.
- Variants: `accent` — `neutral | info | secondary | warning | module | success | error` (the type still carries all 7 for flexibility, but FMP-TECH-05P's actual dashboard usage only assigns 3 of them — see `page.tsx`'s own `kpis` array comment: `neutral` for not-started/on-track, a single shared `info` tint for all 4 in-progress-stage cards, `success`/`error` reserved for the 2 cards meant to stand out). `hasData: false` shows a muted "Unavailable" chip and an em-dash value, matching `MetricCard`'s own `status: 'unavailable'` convention.
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-{accent}-light`/`text-{accent}` icon chip, `border-t-{accent} border-t-[3px]` card accent, `rounded-xl border border-border bg-surface shadow-sm`. FMP-TECH-05P: tightened to `p-3.5` (was `p-4`), icon chip `size-8` (was `size-9`), value `text-xl` (was `text-2xl`), and added `min-h-[6.5rem]` so all 7 cards in a row hold the same height even if one helper line wraps.
- Accessibility behavior: icon marked `aria-hidden`; no interactive semantics (not a link/button — see Notes).
- Used by: Technical Dashboard only, today.
- Notes: Server Component (no `'use client'`). Deliberately NOT the shared `MetricCard` component (used by 23+ other module dashboards app-wide) — a local near-duplicate keeps this dashboard's own visual polish from risking any other module's KPI tiles, same reasoning every per-stage attachment panel in this app already follows. Deliberately NOT wrapped in a `<Link>`: no real per-metric filtered view exists on this page, and clickable-looking cards that don't actually filter would be a fake affordance.

### NextActionPanel (FMP-TECH-05, reworded + priority-ordered FMP-TECH-05P)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/next-action-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: "Next Action Focus" — one manager-friendly sentence telling the viewer what to look at first on the Technical Dashboard, derived purely from data the dashboard already fetched.
- Variants: 5 states, each with its own accent, checked in priority order (FMP-TECH-05P, the ticket's own explicit list) — Needs Attention exists (warning, links to the most urgent job's workflow) → `stageBreakdown.GETTING_APPROVAL > 0` (info, "N job orders are waiting for an approval decision.") → `stageBreakdown.SD_CALCULATION_SUBMISSION > 0` (info, "...waiting for SD & Calculation submission.") → `stageBreakdown.DRAWING_RECEIVED > 0` (info, "...waiting for Drawing Received completion.") → a calm "No urgent Technical action currently." (success). Later stages are checked before earlier ones deliberately — a job order stuck closer to completion is usually more urgent than one just starting. FD Issuance is intentionally not part of this chain (the final stage isn't an actionable "focus" item the way the earlier 3 are).
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-{warning|info|success}-light` card with a matching border tint; `Target`/`CheckCircle2` icons from lucide-react.
- Accessibility behavior: plain text content, one real link in the Needs-Attention state.
- Used by: Technical Dashboard only, today.
- Notes: Server Component. No new query — reads `dashboard.needsAttention`/`dashboard.stageBreakdown`, both already part of the existing `GET /technical/dashboard` response. Never invents a recommendation beyond those real signals. FMP-TECH-05P replaced the old single "busiest stage in natural `TECHNICAL_STAGE_ORDER`" fallback (which checked Drawing Received first, the opposite priority) with the 3 explicit stage-specific checks above, and dropped the `TECHNICAL_STAGE_ORDER`/`TECHNICAL_STAGE_LABELS` imports accordingly.

- Used by: `(protected)/dashboard/loading.tsx`.

### Split-screen enterprise login layout (FMP-UI-17B, reverses FMP-UI-08B's centered-column decision on the user's own explicit instruction; supersedes FMP-UI-08's earlier, different split-screen attempt; polished in FMP-UI-17C; real photo background added in FMP-UI-18, made full-screen in FMP-UI-18B; logo size/position tweaked in FMP-UI-18C; glass card/footer/tag polish in FMP-UI-18D; final polish + watermark crop in FMP-UI-17D; card/footer readability fix in FMP-UI-17E; layout composition polish in FMP-UI-17F; card header "Welcome Back" redesign in FMP-UI-17G; left-panel typography increased in FMP-UI-17H; module chips made non-interactive in FMP-UI-17J)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` (layout only), plus 2 committed image assets: `apps/web/public/login-hero.jpg` (2400×1148 as of FMP-UI-17D, was 2400×1350) and `apps/web/public/login-hero-mobile.jpg` (1000×478, was 1000×563). `apps/web/src/app/login/_components/login-form.tsx` (`action`/`useActionState` wiring, password eye toggle, focus rings) is untouched across every one of FMP-UI-17B/17C/18/18B/18D/17D — same file, same behavior, since FMP-UI-08.
- History: this page was a single centered column (no side panel) from FMP-UI-08B through FMP-UI-13 — FMP-UI-08's ORIGINAL split-screen read "too close to a generic enterprise/maintenance-system login" at the time, and FMP-UI-13 even said explicitly "do not return to left/right split-screen layout." **FMP-UI-17B deliberately reverses that**, on the user's own explicit request — a from-scratch rebuild, CSS-only. **FMP-UI-17C** polished that same split-screen further (still CSS-only). **FMP-UI-18** replaced the CSS-only LEFT-PANEL-ONLY background with a REAL RECAFCO photo the user supplied ("Desktop Lock Image.jpeg" — the actual precast yard at night, with the real RECAFCO signage/"50 years" display). **FMP-UI-18B** — on direct user feedback after seeing a screenshot ("add background image for the entire screen not for just one side") — moved that same photo from a left-panel-only treatment to a single shared layer covering the WHOLE page, both panels, every breakpoint.
- Structure: outer `<div className="relative min-h-screen bg-background lg:grid lg:grid-cols-[5fr_6fr]">` — the ~45/55 two-column split still only activates at `lg`; below that, the right panel remains the sole visible content column. Unchanged in shape since FMP-UI-17B; `relative` added in FMP-UI-18B so the fixed background (below) and its `z-10` siblings establish a clean stacking context.
- **Shared full-screen background** (**FMP-UI-18B, new** — the layer both panels now sit on top of): `<div className="fixed inset-0 z-0 overflow-hidden">` containing 2 swapped `<img>`s — `hidden lg:block` → `login-hero.jpg` (desktop), `lg:hidden` → `login-hero-mobile.jpg` (mobile/tablet, so phones don't download the larger file) — both `object-cover object-[60%_40%]` (a gentler crop bias than FMP-UI-18's `75%_45%`, since the photo now spans the FULL viewport width instead of a narrow 45% panel, so both the crane on the left and the RECAFCO signage right-of-center stay reasonably in view). **FMP-UI-17D** re-cropped BOTH source assets (via `sharp`'s `.extract()`, dropping the bottom 15% of the original photo's height BEFORE resizing) to remove a "www.recafco.com" watermark baked into the source photo itself — this was never a text element the page rendered, so the only reliable fix was cropping the source, not an `object-position` tweak (which can't guarantee the watermark stays out of frame at every viewport size). Then the navy gradient overlay: `linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(23,32,51,0.92) 0%, rgba(36,48,71,0.82) 55%, rgba(47,61,89,0.68) 100%)` — the SAME `--color-nav`/`-hover`/`-active` RGBs the very first solid CSS wash used, as a high-but-not-total-alpha TINT rather than a solid fill. **FMP-UI-17D added a SECOND overlay layer** on top of this one: `linear-gradient(90deg, transparent 0%, transparent 40%, rgba(23,32,51,0.55) 70%, rgba(23,32,51,0.8) 100%)` — the first (diagonal) gradient alone left the RIGHT side of the page (where the login card sits) with the LEAST darkening of the whole background, backwards from what a calm card backdrop needs; this second, purely left-to-right layer is transparent through the left ~40% (so the left panel's own look is completely untouched) and ramps to a strong flat navy by the right edge, specifically calming the busy photo detail directly behind the card. **FMP-UI-17E** strengthened that second layer once more (`0.55`/`0.8`→`0.65`/`0.88`) to work together with the card's own higher opacity (see Right panel below) for an even calmer zone directly behind the form. Both overlay layers together are the "with opacity" treatment: a raw dimmed photo with no color tint would fight every text element's contrast far more than one dimmed by a consistent brand-colored wash. Being `fixed` (not `absolute`), the whole background layer never scrolls away and always covers the full viewport regardless of page height.
- **Left panel** (`hidden lg:block`, `relative z-10`, `overflow-hidden`): no longer has its OWN copy of the photo/overlay (provided by the shared layer now) — just its own scoped decorative touches on top: the 2 corner registration/crop-mark brackets (`border-white/15`, a genuine blueprint/drafting annotation motif kept from FMP-UI-17C) and the 1 red brand glow (`rgba(198,40,40,0.16)`). Content wrapper history: `px-10 py-16` (FMP-UI-17C) → `px-10 pt-10 pb-20 justify-center` (FMP-UI-18C, still vertically CENTERED, just with asymmetric padding biasing it upward) → `px-10 pt-12 pb-12 lg:pt-14`, NO `justify-center` (FMP-UI-17F — top-anchored instead of centered, per direct user feedback that the logo read as "lower-left" rather than a true top-left brand mark on tall viewports) → **same top-anchored structure, group gap eased `gap-8`→`gap-6` (FMP-UI-17H, current)** — to help offset the taller title (below) without pushing the tags too far down on shorter viewports. Holds: an enlarged logo chip (**FMP-UI-18C**: `p-3.5`→`p-4`, image `h-16`→`h-20`), 2-line title ("RECAFCO Factory" / "Management Platform") — size history: `text-4xl xl:text-5xl` (FMP-UI-13-era) → `text-3xl xl:text-4xl` (FMP-UI-17D, "reduce dominance") → **`text-4xl xl:text-5xl 2xl:text-6xl` (FMP-UI-17H, current)** — sized back up once the login card itself became a stronger, more premium element (FMP-UI-17G) and the branding side needed to match it; scaled across 3 responsive steps rather than jumping to the top size everywhere, since this `<h1>` only ever renders inside `hidden lg:block` — its narrowest real width is exactly the `lg` breakpoint (~460px available after padding), where `text-5xl`/`text-6xl` risked wrapping "Management Platform" onto a 3rd line; line-height tightened again (`leading-[1.08]`→`leading-[1.05]`); gap from the logo unchanged (`mt-5`, FMP-UI-17F). Tagline ("Factory Operations System"): `text-xs font-semibold text-white/60` (FMP-UI-17C-era) → **`text-sm font-medium text-white/70` (FMP-UI-17H)**, gap from title bumped `mt-3`→`mt-4`. Supporting sentence: `max-w-md text-sm` (pre-17D) → `max-w-sm text-sm` (FMP-UI-17D, "not too wide") → **`max-w-lg text-base` (FMP-UI-17H, ~512px — the closest standard Tailwind utility to a requested ~520px) with entirely new copy** ("Secure access for contracts, technical approvals, erection workflows, production, safety and maintenance."). 8 module tags: one `flex-wrap` row (pre-18D, reflowed unpredictably, "Tasks" sometimes stranded alone) → 2 EXPLICIT rows of 4 (FMP-UI-18D, `[MODULE_TAGS.slice(0, 4), MODULE_TAGS.slice(4)]`, always Contracts/Technical/Erection/QA-QC then Storage Yard/Safety/Maintenance/Tasks) → chip styling refined (`h-9`, `border-white/12`, `bg-white/6`, FMP-UI-17D) → sized up once more (`h-9`→`h-10`, `px-3.5`→`px-4`, row gap `gap-2.5`→`gap-3`, row spacing `space-y-2.5`→`space-y-3`, FMP-UI-17H) to stay proportional to the larger title/description — still exactly 2 rows of 4, never crowded → **FMP-UI-17J (current)**: these chips were always plain `<span>`s with no `onClick`/`href`/`tabIndex` — never actually interactive — but `transition-colors hover:border-white/25 hover:bg-white/12 hover:text-white` made them visually LOOK clickable on hover; all 3 hover classes and `transition-colors` removed entirely (no hover state left), `cursor-default` added explicitly, base contrast eased one notch (`text-white/80`→`text-white/70`, `border-white/12`→`border-white/10`), and a small uppercase "System Modules" label (`text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-widest text-white/45`) added above the 2 rows.
- **Right panel** (`relative z-10`, **FMP-UI-18B**: `bg-background` removed): the shared photo now shows through the space around the login card here too. Its centered content wrapper (`w-full max-w-md`) gained `pt-8 lg:pt-12` in **FMP-UI-17F** — extra top padding inside the still-`justify-center` parent, so the mobile-branding/card/footer group centers slightly BELOW true dead-center rather than exactly in the middle, per "move the sign-in card slightly lower so it feels vertically balanced." The sign-in card's background opacity/blur history: `bg-surface` (FMP-UI-13/17B/17C, fully opaque) → `bg-surface/90 backdrop-blur-xl` (FMP-UI-18D, frosted-glass so it reads as floating WITHIN the photo scene) → `bg-surface/95 backdrop-blur-lg` (FMP-UI-17D, "should not feel transparent") → **`bg-surface/98 backdrop-blur-sm` (FMP-UI-17E, current)** — near-solid with only a whisper of blur, settled on after direct user feedback that the FMP-UI-17D version still let too much of the busy photo bleed through, undermining the contrast guarantees of the semantic text tokens (`text-text-primary`/`-secondary`) used inside the card — those tokens were always theme-correct; the card's own insufficient opacity was the actual bug. Card header history: icon+heading in one left-aligned `flex` row (`items-start`, FMP-UI-17C) → same row, `items-center` for cleaner alignment (FMP-UI-17D) → **redesigned entirely into a centered column (FMP-UI-17G, current)** — a large `size-16 sm:size-20 rounded-full bg-accent-light text-accent` circular badge holding `ShieldUser` (was a small `size-12 rounded-2xl` square badge holding `ShieldCheck`), then "Welcome Back" (was "Secure Sign in"), then "Secure access to RECAFCO Factory Operations System." (was "Use your RECAFCO account to access factory operations."), then the unchanged "Authorized Company Access" badge — all in one `flex flex-col items-center text-center` group, per direct user request to take structural inspiration from a reference login card (large circular icon, "Welcome Back," centered) while keeping RECAFCO's own red/navy identity, explicitly not the reference's blue HRMS styling. Footer history: plain `text-white/80`/`text-white/60` on the photo (FMP-UI-18B) → wrapped in a `rounded-2xl bg-black/25 backdrop-blur-sm` "pill" (FMP-UI-18D, text `white/90`/`white/70`) → pill removed, back to plain `white/85`/`white/65` text (FMP-UI-17D) → a small "clear footer area," `rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-surface/85 backdrop-blur-md shadow-sm`, using this page's own NORMAL `text-text-secondary`/`text-text-muted` tokens instead of literal white (FMP-UI-17E) — deliberately lighter/softer/theme-adaptive than the FMP-UI-18D pill. **FMP-UI-17F** tightened its gap from the card (`mt-8`→`mt-5`) for closer visual grouping, per "place it closer to the form." `ThemeToggle`'s wrapper: `scale-90 opacity-70` (FMP-UI-17C) → `scale-75 opacity-55` (FMP-UI-17D) → `scale-75 opacity-65` (FMP-UI-17E) — still restores to full size/opacity on hover/focus-within throughout; the `ThemeToggle` component itself has never been touched by any of these units, only this page's wrapper `<div>` around it.
- **Mobile/tablet compact branding** (`lg:hidden`): **FMP-UI-18B** removed the small photo "hero band" FMP-UI-18 had given this block (that would have doubled up with the new shared full-screen layer sitting right behind it) — it's now just the logo chip + white/light text sitting directly on the shared background, the same way the desktop left panel's content always has. Mobile/tablet gets the real photo genuinely full-screen now too, not just a small band.
- Reusable takeaway 1 (FMP-UI-17B): when a user explicitly asks to reverse a documented, deliberate past decision, that's a legitimate instruction to follow — but it's worth surfacing the reversal once before a large rebuild.
- Reusable takeaway 2 (FMP-UI-17C): a "feels generic/empty/flat" complaint about an abstract CSS-only background is often best answered by reaching for a MORE SPECIFIC real-world visual convention than by tuning the same generic pattern further.
- Reusable takeaway 3 (FMP-UI-18) — **check the static-asset auth allowlist every time a new public image is added to this page:** `proxy.ts`'s `PUBLIC_PREFIXES` gates which paths skip the JWT-cookie check; a new image path missing from it gets silently redirected to `/login` (HTML instead of image bytes) for every unauthenticated visitor — this exact bug already happened once before (FMP-UI-11-fix) and was caught again here before shipping.
- Reusable takeaway 4 (FMP-UI-18) — a large user-supplied source image (8533×4800, ~33MB here) should never be linked/served as-is; resize+recompress into purpose-sized derived assets BEFORE committing, using whatever image library is already in the dependency tree (`sharp`, already present for Next.js's own optimizer — no new dependency needed).
- Reusable takeaway 5 (FMP-UI-18B) — when a background is scoped to one region of a page (here: the left panel only) and the user later asks for it "full screen," the fix is to hoist the background to a layer that's a SIBLING of every region that should show it (a shared `fixed inset-0`, `z-0`), not to duplicate the same image into each region separately — duplicating would have meant 2 (or more) copies of the same photo downloaded and composited independently, likely misaligned at the panel seam, versus one continuous image that both panels' content simply sits on top of via explicit `z-10` stacking.
- Reusable takeaway 6 (FMP-UI-18D, **corrected by FMP-UI-17E** — see below) — when a solid opaque card sits on top of a photographic background, "make it feel integrated, not pasted on" is very often a `backdrop-blur` job, not a transparency job: dropping opacity alone (without blur) just makes the busy photo show THROUGH the card, hurting form readability; blur was expected to soften whatever's directly behind the card into an ambient glow while keeping the card's own contents crisp. **What actually happened:** even at 90–95% opacity with strong blur, real user feedback across 2 rounds (FMP-UI-18D→FMP-UI-17D→FMP-UI-17E) was that the card still read as "too transparent" and hurt text readability — the aesthetic goal ("floating within the scene") was in real tension with the functional requirement ("clearly readable text"), and readability had to win. Settled at 98% opacity with only `backdrop-blur-sm` (barely any blur at all) — a lesson that for a form a user must actually READ and USE (as opposed to a purely decorative surface), err heavily toward opaque; a LIGHT blur is enough to avoid a harsh pasted-on edge without meaningfully affecting how solid the surface reads.
- Reusable takeaway 7 (FMP-UI-18D, **superseded for this specific footer in FMP-UI-17D** — kept as general advice) — text placed directly over a photographic background should never depend solely on a single global overlay/gradient for contrast at every position it might appear — a small text element (like a footer) is often better given its OWN local translucent backing so its contrast is guaranteed regardless of which specific, unpredictable part of the photo happens to sit behind it. **What changed:** FMP-UI-17D removed this footer's own backing anyway, because a follow-up design review called the pill "too heavy" — the actual fix ended up being a STRONGER shared overlay at that specific region (the new right-side gradient) rather than a per-element backing. Both approaches solve "guarantee contrast here" — the choice between them is a visual-weight tradeoff (a local backing is more bulletproof against any future photo change; strengthening the shared overlay is lighter-weight but ties that region's contrast to the overlay staying tuned for it), not a correctness question.
- Reusable takeaway 8 (FMP-UI-17D) — a "background feels busy/washed-out behind X" complaint is a signal to check whether the SAME overlay is doing equal work everywhere, when different regions of the page actually need different amounts of darkening — here, the single diagonal overlay happened to leave its LEAST-darkened corner exactly where the login card sits, the opposite of what that region needed; the fix was a second, region-targeted gradient layered on top (transparent where the existing treatment already works, opaque where it doesn't) rather than adjusting the first gradient's own numbers and disturbing a look that was already correct elsewhere.
- Reusable takeaway 9 (FMP-UI-17D) — when a user-supplied source image contains an unwanted baked-in element (a watermark, a caption, a logo that isn't yours), the only FULLY reliable fix is cropping the source file itself before generating derived assets — relying on the RENDERED page's own `object-position`/cropping to keep it out of view only works for the specific viewport dimensions you tested, and silently reappears at others. Always re-view the regenerated file's actual pixels afterward rather than trusting the crop-percentage math alone — a first attempt here (crop 9% of height) was insufficient and only caught by that visual re-check, not predicted in advance.
- Reusable takeaway 10 (FMP-UI-17E) — a footer/caption sitting directly on a busy photographic background doesn't have to choose between "flat opaque high-contrast pill" (FMP-UI-18D, felt heavy) and "plain text with no backing" (FMP-UI-17D, felt invisible) — a THIRD option, a low-opacity/blurred version of the page's own existing surface material (`bg-surface/85 backdrop-blur-md` plus the page's normal text tokens, rather than a literal black block plus literal white text), reads as "part of the same design system as the card above it" instead of either "a separate dark sticker" or "unprotected text." When 2 opposite fixes for the same complaint both get reported as wrong in different ways, the answer is often a genuinely different THIRD material, not a compromise blend of the first two's exact values.
- Reusable takeaway 11 (FMP-UI-17F) — `justify-center` on a full-height flex column doesn't guarantee a brand mark near the TOP of that content reads as "top-anchored" — on a tall viewport, centering the whole content block can visually push its first element (here, the logo) well down from the actual top edge, reading as "lower" than intended even though it's still literally the first child. If a specific element within a centered block needs to read as anchored to an EDGE (top-left, in this case), the more direct fix is to stop centering the block itself and use padding/margin from that edge instead — asymmetric padding on an otherwise-centered block (as FMP-UI-18C tried) is a weaker version of the same fix and can still leave the element looking "centered enough to not read as anchored," per this exact history (18C's asymmetric `pt-10/pb-20` wasn't enough; 17F's full switch to top-anchoring was what actually fixed it).
- Reusable takeaway 12 (FMP-UI-17G) — "take inspiration from a reference design's STRUCTURE, not its visual style" (this unit's own framing, echoing FMP-UI-17B's identical instruction for the whole-page split-screen reference) applies just as cleanly at the scale of a single component: the reference card's layout shape (centered icon → heading → subtitle → badge) was reused wholesale, while every actual pixel (icon choice, colors, copy) came from this app's own existing tokens and brand voice — `bg-accent-light`/`text-accent` (RECAFCO red) never became the reference's blue, and the icon was chosen for literal semantic fit (`ShieldUser` = a person inside a shield, matching "secure user access") rather than copied from the reference's own icon.
- Reusable takeaway 13 (FMP-UI-17H) — when sizing text that's `hidden` below some breakpoint (only ever rendered inside a `hidden lg:block`-style ancestor), its "smallest real width" is the width at THAT breakpoint, not 0 — so a size that would be safe on a full desktop monitor can still overflow/wrap badly at the exact moment it first becomes visible. The fix here was a 3-step responsive escalation (safe size at the narrowest visible breakpoint, the requested bigger sizes reserved for wider tiers that can actually fit them) rather than either a single large size everywhere (overflow risk) or a single small-enough-for-the-narrowest-case size everywhere (under-shoots the request on genuinely wide screens).
- Reusable takeaway 14 (FMP-UI-17H) — a "this element needs to feel more premium/stronger" complaint about text near a component that ITSELF just got a premium redesign (here: the login card's FMP-UI-17G "Welcome Back" polish) is often a signal to check RELATIVE visual weight, not the text's own values in isolation — the left panel's typography hadn't regressed on its own since FMP-UI-17D, but the card next to it had visibly gained weight, making the same numbers suddenly read as "too small" by comparison.
- Reusable takeaway 15 (FMP-UI-17J) — a "this looks clickable but isn't" report is often satisfied entirely by CSS, with zero markup change needed: the module chips here were already structurally non-interactive (plain `<span>`, no `onClick`/`href`/`tabIndex`) BEFORE this unit, yet still read as buttons because their `hover:*` classes reacted like one. Always check whether an element's accessibility semantics are already correct before assuming a "make X non-interactive" request requires changing its tag/attributes — sometimes the only bug is a hover/cursor/transition class implying an affordance the markup never actually offered.
- Notes: no new brand/status color tokens were added by FMP-UI-17B, FMP-UI-17C, or FMP-UI-18 — the left panel's overlay reuses the exact same `--color-nav*` RGB values as literal rgba (needed because CSS gradients can't reference a CSS custom property AND vary its alpha independently in one declaration the way plain rgba can); everything else is existing tokens. `ThemeToggle` no longer lives in a centered column at all — see its own registry entry below for its current placement/sizing.
- **FMP-PERF-02 (asset swap only — no visual/layout change to this page's own markup, structure, or copy):** the 2 hero images are now `apps/web/public/login-hero.webp` (2048×1152, re-encoded from the live `login-hero.jpg` at quality 75 — same crop/photo, 983KB→396KB) and `apps/web/public/login-hero-mobile.webp` (1024×576 at quality 72, 95KB). The old `login-hero-mobile.jpg` was discovered to be a byte-for-byte duplicate of the desktop file (both 2048×1152) — a real regression from whatever process last regenerated it, meaning phones had been downloading the full desktop-resolution image; the new mobile asset is a genuinely smaller, independently-generated derivative. Both `<img>` tags gained explicit `width`/`height` attributes matching their real pixel dimensions (the fixed/inset-0 + h-full/w-full box these images render into is already fully CSS-determined regardless of image load, so this was already at ~zero CLS risk — the attributes are a best-practice addition, not a fix for an observed shift). `proxy.ts`'s `PUBLIC_PREFIXES` updated to the new `.webp` filenames (see Reusable takeaway 3 above — this is exactly the class of bug that check exists to catch). Two untracked, never-wired-in leftover files (`login-hero1.jpg` 360KB, `login-hero-mobile1.jpg` 73KB — an abandoned earlier optimization attempt, different crop/aspect ratio, never referenced by any code) were found alongside the live pair; not used as the source for this fix (unverified crop/provenance), left on disk pending an explicit cleanup decision. The 4 now-unreferenced `.jpg` files (both old live ones plus both orphans) were NOT deleted — file deletion in this environment requires explicit user confirmation as an irreversible action; flagged in FMP-PERF-02's own final report instead of force-deleted.
- **FMP-PERF-02 — logo swapped to `next/image`** in all 4 places `recafco-logo.png` (157KB, 193×150 source) is used at a much smaller display size: this login page (2 occurrences, `h-20`/`h-14`), `sidebar.tsx` (`h-auto w-11`, ~44px), and `welcome-transition.tsx` (`h-20 sm:h-24`) — all 4 marked `priority` (always-above-the-fold brand marks, so they opt out of `next/image`'s default lazy-load). `next/image` serves a correctly-downscaled, modern-format (WebP/AVIF where supported) variant instead of shipping the full source file at every display size; no `next.config.ts` change needed (local `/public` images need no `remotePatterns`/`domains` config, and this project's self-hosted `output: 'standalone'` PM2 deployment runs the built-in image optimizer locally — no CDN/internet dependency introduced).

### Platform-wide appearance mode: Light / Dark / System (FMP-UI-11)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/globals.css` (`@custom-variant dark`, the `.dark {}` token-override block, `--login-glow-center`), `apps/web/src/lib/theme.ts` (types + `localStorage` read/write + `resolveTheme`), `apps/web/src/app/_components/theme-provider.tsx` (`ThemeProvider`/`useTheme`), `apps/web/src/app/_components/theme-toggle.tsx` (the UI control), `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` (anti-flash script + provider wiring).
- Purpose: a genuinely platform-wide dark mode, implemented as a token-system change rather than a per-component rewrite. `ThemeProvider` resolves the user's `light`/`dark`/`system` preference to a concrete `light`/`dark` and toggles a `.dark` class on `<html>`; every component already built with this app's semantic Tailwind color utilities (`bg-surface`, `text-text-primary`, `border-border`, `bg-surface-secondary`, etc. — the overwhelming majority of this app, by this point in the project) re-themes automatically, because those utilities compile to `var(--color-x)` and `.dark` redefines `--color-x` for every element inside it. No dashboard/sidebar/contract-page file needed to change for this to reach them.
- Variants: `light` and `dark` are explicit, OS-independent choices; `system` (the default) resolves live from `window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` and keeps re-resolving if the OS preference changes while a tab stays open on `system` — but a manual `light`/`dark` choice is never silently overridden by a later OS change.
- Key tokens/classes: `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *));` (Tailwind v4's documented class-based dark-mode pattern, needed because "System" must be resolved in JS to something a pure `prefers-color-scheme`-only variant can't be told to ignore). The `.dark {}` override block touches exactly 9 structural tokens: `--color-background`, `--color-surface`, `--color-surface-secondary`, `--color-surface-hover`, `--color-border`, `--color-border-strong`, `--color-text-primary`, `--color-text-secondary`, `--color-text-muted` — the exact list this unit's own spec named (page background, card background, text primary/secondary, border, input background, table background, muted surfaces; "button surfaces" and "sidebar background" are covered by leaving `--color-accent`/`--color-nav` alone, see below). **Deliberately unchanged in `.dark`:** `--color-nav`/`--color-nav-hover`/`--color-nav-active`/`--color-text-inverse` (the sidebar is permanently dark chrome in BOTH platform themes by original design — it was never meant to get lighter) and every brand/status color (`--color-accent` and every `--color-success`/`warning`/`error`/`info`/`teal`/`secondary-accent`/`module-*` pair, including their "-light" pastel tints) — RECAFCO red stays RECAFCO red in both modes per this unit's own explicit requirement, and the status-tint chips remain fully readable (their paired foreground colors are untouched) but are NOT yet re-tinted for a dark page — a deliberately scoped gap, not an oversight; see this unit's own progress-tracker entry for the reasoning and as a marker for a possible focused follow-up.
- Accessibility behavior: `ThemeToggle` is a `role="radiogroup"` of 3 `role="radio"` buttons with `aria-checked`, each a real `<button>` (keyboard-operable, visible focus ring via `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-nav`).
- Used by: `login/page.tsx` today — **FMP-UI-13** moved it from below the footer (inline in the centered content column) to a `fixed right-4 top-4 z-20 sm:right-6 sm:top-6` page corner, per that unit's own "utility control, not part of the sign-in form" framing. **FMP-UI-17B** repositioned it again, from `fixed` (page-wide) to `absolute right-4 top-4` scoped to the new right panel's own corner — same reasoning as FMP-UI-13, just re-anchored now that the page has 2 panels instead of one centered column. **FMP-UI-17C** — after a "theme selector is visually too dominant" report — added `origin-top-right scale-90 opacity-70 transition-opacity hover:opacity-100 focus-within:opacity-100` to that same wrapper: the toggle itself renders 10% smaller and rests at 70% opacity, returning to full size/opacity on hover or when a control inside it has focus (so it's never actually harder to see or use, just quieter at rest). The `ThemeToggle` component's own JSX/props/behavior were not touched by either unit — every change described here is in `login/page.tsx`'s wrapper `<div>` around it. The preference it sets applies everywhere else in the app without needing a second toggle instance anywhere, since it's read from the same `localStorage` key/`ThemeProvider` on every page load.
- Reusable takeaway: a "should the whole platform re-theme" feature is cheap and low-risk EXACTLY in proportion to how consistently the app already avoids hardcoded colors in favor of semantic tokens — this unit could be implemented as "9 CSS variables + one small provider" specifically because every prior unit this session kept using `bg-surface`/`text-text-primary`/etc. instead of literal hex, so there was nothing left to individually migrate.
- Notes: preference persists to `localStorage` only (key `'recafco-theme'`, shared as `THEME_STORAGE_KEY` from `lib/theme.ts`) — no cookie, no database field, never sent to or read by the server; this is a pure client-side display preference with zero interaction with auth/session/permission logic. The anti-flash script in `layout.tsx` duplicates the literal key string (it must — it runs before any JS module loads) and is the one place that string has to be kept manually in sync with `theme.ts`'s exported constant.

### Post-login welcome transition (FMP-UI-Login-Polish, copy updated in FMP-UI-12, once-per-tab bug fixed/re-fixed in FMP-UI-12B/12C/12D, visual hierarchy refreshed in FMP-UI-12E, once-per-tab guard removed entirely in FMP-UI-12F, headline merged to one line in FMP-UI-12G)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/welcome/page.tsx` (server — cookie gate only), `apps/web/src/app/welcome/_components/welcome-transition.tsx` (client — the actual transition, no props).
- Purpose: a short (~1.8s) branded screen between a successful sign-in and landing on the real dashboard — a prominent logo, then a single headline ("Welcome to RECAFCO Factory Operations System" — **FMP-UI-12G** merged what FMP-UI-12E had split into a small/light line + a large/bold line back into one sentence, sized to stay on one line on desktop), "Preparing your workspace…" as a small supporting line, then an automatic `router.replace('/')`. `login/actions.ts`'s success redirect target changed from `/` to `/welcome` for this — the only functional change to the login action; credential checking and cookie logic are untouched.
- Variants: none — **FMP-UI-12F removed the once-per-tab `sessionStorage` skip guard entirely.** Every authenticated visit to `/welcome` (from a real login redirect, or by navigating here directly) always renders the full transition. There is no `?force=1` anymore either — with no guard left to bypass, it had nothing left to do.
- Key tokens/classes: **FMP-UI-12E** split the single fade+rise block into two: the logo (`inline-flex rounded-3xl border border-border bg-white p-6 shadow-lg`, image `h-20 sm:h-24`) and the heading group (`mt-8 max-w-xl lg:max-w-4xl xl:max-w-6xl` — the wider `lg`/`xl` max-width added in FMP-UI-12G specifically to give the one-line heading room to fit), each with its own `transition-all duration-700 ease-out` fade+rise (`opacity-0 translate-y-3` → `opacity-100 translate-y-0`), the heading group carrying `delay-150` so it visibly follows the logo by a beat. **FMP-UI-12G** heading: a single `<h1 className="text-3xl font-extrabold leading-tight tracking-tight text-text-primary sm:text-4xl lg:whitespace-nowrap lg:text-4xl xl:text-5xl">Welcome to RECAFCO Factory Operations System</h1>` — wraps naturally below `lg` (mobile/tablet), then `whitespace-nowrap` locks it to one line from `lg` (1024px) up, where the widened container has enough room; font scales `text-3xl` → `sm:text-4xl` → `lg:text-4xl` → `xl:text-5xl` (kept one step smaller at `lg` than `xl` so the one-line-nowrap version fits at the narrower end of that breakpoint before growing further once there's more room). Every color is a semantic token (`bg-background`/`text-text-primary`/`text-text-muted`/`border-border`) — FMP-UI-11's `.dark` overrides on those exact tokens mean this screen already supports Light/Dark/System with zero changes of its own.
- Accessibility behavior: a small always-visible "Continue now" text-button beneath the animation is a manual fallback in case the automatic redirect doesn't fire for any reason — an older user is never left on a screen with no visible way forward.
- Reusable takeaway (superseded by FMP-UI-12F, kept for history — see that entry below): a "show this once, not every time" requirement backed by no server-side state used to be handled with a `sessionStorage` read/write guard; that approach is exactly what kept re-breaking across FMP-UI-12B/12C/12D, because "once" needed a precise, unambiguous definition of when the counter resets that a plain presence-flag can't express on its own.
- Notes: gated by the same `recafco_access` cookie every other protected page already checks (`page.tsx` redirects to `/login` if absent) — this page has no auth logic of its own, only a presence check.
- **FMP-UI-12 — re-verified, not re-built:** this unit's own "correct auth flow" requirements (welcome only after real success; failed login still shows the normal error; `mustChangePassword` isn't skipped; the sign-in button already disables and shows a loading label during submission) were all already true from when this feature and the button's own `disabled={isPending}` state were first built — confirmed by re-reading `login/actions.ts` and `login-form.tsx` rather than assuming they needed changes. Worth remembering next time a task re-lists requirements a past unit already satisfied: check first, since re-implementing something already correct risks introducing a regression for no reason.
- **FMP-UI-12B — the real bug: the `sessionStorage` guard outlives a single login.** `recafco_welcome_shown` persists for the whole browser TAB's lifetime, not "one login" — nothing (a server action like `logoutAction` can't touch client storage) ever cleared it on logout, so the SECOND login in the same tab (e.g. after testing a failed login, or just logging out and back in) silently skipped the welcome screen, looking exactly like "login goes straight to the dashboard." Fixed by having `LoginForm`'s submit button clear the flag on every click (see its own entry below) — the flag itself is still only ever SET inside this component, unchanged. The literal key string moved to a new shared `apps/web/src/lib/welcome.ts` (`WELCOME_SESSION_FLAG_KEY`) so the two files can never drift on it again. Reusable takeaway: a "show once" flag keyed only by presence (not by WHICH login/session it belongs to) will always outlive the thing it's meant to gate unless something explicit resets it at the START of each new instance of that thing — and if the natural "reset point" (logout) is server-side while the flag is client-side, the reset has to move to whatever client-side moment starts the next cycle instead (here, clicking "Sign in").
- **FMP-UI-12C — the FMP-UI-12B fix was attached to the wrong event.** A submit button's `onClick` only fires for a literal click; a keyboard Enter-key submission inside a text field can dispatch a form's `submit` event without ever routing through a synthetic `click` on the button first (browser-dependent, per the HTML spec's implicit-submission behavior). So logging back in via Enter (instead of a mouse click) could still find the stale flag from an earlier login in the same tab and skip straight to the SKIP branch before ever rendering. Fixed by moving the authoritative clear to a `useEffect(() => {...}, [])` on `LoginForm`'s mount (see its own entry below) — this fires once whenever the login page is reached at all, regardless of how the eventual submission happens, so no submission path can race it. The FMP-UI-12B `onClick` clear is kept as a harmless, redundant secondary. Also added dev-only `NODE_ENV === 'development'`-guarded trace logs at 6 checkpoints across this feature (mount/SKIP/SHOW/REDIRECT here, plus one each in `LoginForm` and `login/actions.ts`) to make this flow provable without browser automation — guarded rather than removed, so they stay available if this bug class recurs. Reusable takeaway: a "run this before X happens" requirement should hang off the lifecycle of the component/page that precedes X, not off one specific DOM event that's merely the MOST COMMON way X gets triggered — an event-specific hook only covers the paths that actually fire that event.
- **FMP-UI-12D — re-audited after a user report that the welcome screen was STILL not showing even after FMP-UI-12C.** Re-confirmed `login/actions.ts` (redirects to `/welcome`) and `proxy.ts` (no rule redirects an authenticated request away from `/welcome`) were both still correct — no code change was needed in either. Found the web dev server's own compiled dev-build output (`.next/dev/server/...`) already contained the current source's `/welcome` destination string, ruling out the "stale non-watch-mode process" bug class that hit the API twice before (FMP-UI-10B/10D) for THIS file. Temporarily bumped the redirect delay to 3000ms, added a bright "WELCOME ROUTE RENDERED" marker, and disabled the sessionStorage guard outright (`TEMP_DISABLE_SESSION_GUARD`) to make the screen impossible to miss during a live click-through test and to rule out the guard as a variable — all reverted in FMP-UI-12E once the user's next message (asking to polish this same screen's visuals) implied the underlying flow was in fact working. Reusable takeaway: when source code and compiled server output both check out but the reported symptom persists, the remaining candidates are runtime state the developer can't inspect from the editor — a stale dev-server process, browser-side caching, or (as turned out to be likeliest here) simply not having restarted/hard-refreshed after the previous fix landed; temporary, maximally-visible debug scaffolding (oversized text, a guard bypass, a longer delay) is the right tool for narrowing that down without browser automation, as long as it's clearly marked `TEMP-` and reverted once confirmed.
- **FMP-UI-12E — visual refresh, not a logic change.** Restyled the heading from 2 same-sized lines inside one `<h1>` into an explicit hierarchy — a small/light "Welcome to" `<p>` above a large/bold `<h1>` for "RECAFCO Factory Operations System" — and enlarged the logo (`h-14`→`h-20 sm:h-24`, chip `p-4 rounded-2xl shadow-md`→`p-6 rounded-3xl shadow-lg`) so it reads as this screen's own hero moment rather than reusing the login page's smaller chip verbatim. Split the single fade+rise wrapper into 2 (logo, then heading group with `delay-150`) for a subtle staggered entrance. Removed all FMP-UI-12D temporary debug scaffolding (the "WELCOME ROUTE RENDERED" marker, the guard bypass, the 3000ms delay, and this component's `[welcome-trace]` console logs) now that the underlying routing bug is resolved; settled the redirect delay at 1800ms (within the requested 1.5–2s window — a little more than the original 1500ms since the staggered 2-block entrance needs slightly more time to read before redirecting).
- **FMP-UI-12F — removed the once-per-tab guard entirely, after it broke a 4th time.** The user reported the welcome screen skipping again right after FMP-UI-12E's (purely visual) changes — meaning the guard's failure modes were never really about the visual code at all; the `sessionStorage` "already shown" approach itself was fundamentally fragile, having needed a fix at FMP-UI-12B (flag never cleared on logout), a different fix at FMP-UI-12C (Enter-key submission bypassing the FMP-UI-12B fix), and turned out to still be a live suspect at FMP-UI-12D even after that. Rather than debugging a 4th variant of the same mechanism, removed it outright: `WelcomeTransition` no longer reads or writes `sessionStorage` at all, has no `force` prop, and `/welcome/page.tsx` no longer reads `searchParams`. The shared constant file `apps/web/src/lib/welcome.ts` (`WELCOME_SESSION_FLAG_KEY`) is now deleted — nothing imports it. `LoginForm` no longer has a mount effect or an `onClick` handler clearing anything; it only submits the form. Reusable takeaway: a "show once" requirement gated on client-side storage keeps needing new patches because "once" is ambiguous (once per tab? once per login? once per session?) and every patch to that ambiguity is really just moving where a NEW edge case can hide. Since this screen is provably only ever reached via one single `redirect()` call right after a real successful login (`login/actions.ts`), the guard was solving a problem ("what if the user revisits `/welcome` without a fresh login") that the app's own routing already prevents by construction — removing the guard didn't trade correctness for simplicity, it removed a mechanism that was never actually load-bearing.
- **FMP-UI-12G — pure typography pass, no logic touched.** Merged FMP-UI-12E's 2-tier heading (`<p>` "Welcome to" + separate `<h1>` "RECAFCO Factory Operations System") into one `<h1>Welcome to RECAFCO Factory Operations System</h1>`, per the user's explicit request for a single premium headline. The tricky part is "one line on desktop, wraps on mobile without overflowing" — `whitespace-nowrap` alone would force the browser to render past any container's width without wrapping, so it only kicks in at `lg:` (1024px+), paired with widening the heading's own container (`max-w-xl` → `lg:max-w-4xl xl:max-w-6xl`) so there's actually room for a ~44-character sentence at `text-4xl`/`text-5xl` to fit without spilling past the viewport. Below `lg`, no `nowrap` class applies, so the browser wraps normally inside the narrower `max-w-xl`, same as any other paragraph. Reusable takeaway: "one line on desktop, wraps on mobile" is never `whitespace-nowrap` alone — that class fights word-wrap, not container width, so it has to be paired with a matching width increase at the same breakpoint or the line will simply overflow past the container/viewport instead of wrapping.

### Compact brand row on a dark sidebar, with a small white chip just around the logo (FMP-UI-09, supersedes the earlier "full white panel" version)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/sidebar.tsx` (the "Logo / Brand" header row inside `sidebarContent`); asset at `apps/web/public/recafco-logo.png`.
- Purpose: the real RECAFCO logo has dark-grey/black elements (Arabic wordmark, wing mark) on a transparent background — placed directly on the sidebar's dark `bg-nav`, those elements would be invisible (only the red parts would show), so the logo still needs A contrasting backing. FMP-UI-07's first attempt gave the ENTIRE brand row a `bg-white` background (a full light panel atop an otherwise-dark sidebar) — this read as a large logo disconnected from the rest of the dark sidebar (reported in FMP-UI-09). The fix keeps the row's own dark background (no override — it inherits `bg-nav` from the `<aside>`) and gives ONLY the logo image itself a small white chip (`rounded-md bg-white p-1`, image `w-11` ≈44px wide), with "RECAFCO FMP" + a "Factory Management" subtitle in light text beside it — a compact icon-plus-label brand mark, not a separate panel.
