- Key tokens/classes: row `h-16 px-4 border-b border-nav-hover` (no background class — dark by inheritance); logo chip `rounded-md bg-white p-1` sized to the image only; text `text-sm font-bold text-text-inverse` (title) + `text-[10px] text-text-inverse/60` (subtitle); mobile close button and the link's focus ring use dark-background-appropriate colors (`text-text-inverse/70`, `focus-visible:ring-white/50`), not the light-background colors the full white panel briefly used.
- Reusable takeaway: when a design correction says an element "looks disconnected/too large" against its surrounding surface, the fix is usually to shrink the CONTRAST-NEEDING PART down to just what needs it (here, the logo alone gets a chip) rather than extending the contrasting background outward to cover more of the surface — the latter is what created the "disconnected panel" feeling being complained about in the first place.
- Notes: the logo source (`recafco-logo.png`, transparent PNG) was cropped to its real content bounding box and downscaled before being placed in `apps/web/public/` — the original file ships with ~20% of surrounding transparent padding baked into its square canvas. The same cropped asset (larger, in its own white chip) is reused on the login page (`apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx`); the ORIGINAL uncropped square file was resized separately to `apps/web/src/app/icon.png` for the browser favicon (Next.js App Router auto-detects this filename — no metadata code needed).

### TopHeader (Sign out button restyled in FMP-UI-18; user avatar added in FMP-UI-25)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/top-header.tsx`
- Purpose: Top bar with mobile hamburger, user info, and logout button. Also renders each page's contextual middle slot: 8 different Contract Management breadcrumb variants. **FMP-UI-14** removed the Executive Dashboard's title from this header entirely (see "Executive Dashboard hero title" below for where it lives now) — this header's only job now is navigation chrome (hamburger, breadcrumb slot, user info, Sign out), never a page's own main heading.
- Variants: Single variant; hamburger only visible on mobile (`md:hidden`).
- Key tokens/classes: `relative h-14 bg-surface border-b border-border`.
- Accessibility behavior: Hamburger calls `onMenuOpen(e.currentTarget)` for focus restoration.
- Used by: `AppShell`
- **User area (FMP-UI-25):** `[avatar] name / role [Sign out]`. Avatar is a 36px circle (`size-9 rounded-full border border-border-strong bg-surface-secondary text-sm font-bold text-text-primary`, `aria-hidden`) showing `avatarInitial(displayName)` — the first letter or digit, so a bracketed name like "[UAT] Manager" gives "U"; "?" when there is none. Neutral surface tokens only, so it needs no `dark:` variant. Name is `text-sm font-semibold`, role `text-[13px] text-text-secondary`; both `truncate` inside `max-w-48`. The avatar stays visible below `sm`, where the name/role block is hidden. No profile image support yet — when one is added, render it inside the same circle.
- **Readability floor (FMP-UI-25):** dashboard text is 12px minimum (13px for row/body text, 16–24px for values); 10–11px is no longer used for labels on the Maintenance dashboard. Meaningful helper text uses `text-text-secondary`, not `text-text-muted`. `MmsCard`, `MmsKpiCard`, Manager Attention, Recent table, Quick Actions and Modules Summary all follow this; the Platform Dashboard card's Live / Setup Pending badge is the one 11px exception.
- Notes: `'use client'`; logout via `<form action={logoutAction}>` server action. Props: `user: ShellUser`, `onMenuOpen: (el: HTMLElement) => void`. Reads `usePathname()` itself (no prop needed) to decide its middle slot. `EXECUTIVE_DASHBOARD_PATH`/`isExecutiveDashboard` are still present internally, now solely to keep the breadcrumb slot suppressed on `/dashboard` (unchanged behavior — that route never had a breadcrumb).
- **Sign out button (FMP-UI-18):** neutral outline (`h-9 px-3 rounded-md border-border bg-surface text-text-secondary`, barely-different hover) → soft RECAFCO-red badge treatment: `border-accent/30 bg-accent-light text-accent font-semibold rounded-lg px-4`, hover inverts to a solid `bg-accent`/`text-accent-foreground` fill (white text), focus ring gained an offset (`focus:ring-focus focus:ring-offset-2`, matching every other header/executive button's own standard focus token). Reuses the exact `bg-accent-light`/`text-accent` combo already established for red status badges elsewhere (e.g. `workflow-task-priority-badge.tsx`'s CRITICAL badge) rather than a new token or a first `dark:` variant — see reusable takeaway below. `logoutAction`/form wiring untouched.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-18) — when a "make this more visible/less alarming" request calls for a red-but-not-destructive tone, prefer `--color-accent` (RECAFCO brand red, used for normal emphasis) over `--color-error` (reserved for actual fault/error states) — and reuse the `bg-accent-light`/`text-accent` pairing already established for status badges app-wide rather than introducing a new token or a first-ever `dark:` Tailwind variant. Those `-light` tokens are deliberately not re-tinted between themes (see globals.css's own FMP-UI-11 note), so reusing them keeps this button visually consistent with every other red badge already shipping correctly in dark mode, instead of creating a one-off inconsistency.

### Executive Dashboard hero title (FMP-UI-04B moved the title INTO TopHeader; FMP-UI-14 moved it back OUT, into the page body; FMP-UI-15 added a tagline + summary strip; FMP-UI-15B removed the strip again; FMP-UI-23B eased the sizing; FMP-UI-23C removed the product-name title entirely, keeping only the former tagline as one large `<h1>`; FMP-UI-23D sized that heading up again and replaced its `space-y` gap with a real flex `gap`)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/dashboard/page.tsx`
- Purpose: history worth keeping, since this hero has now changed shape 7 times. FMP-UI-04B moved it out of the page body and into `TopHeader`; FMP-UI-14 moved it back to the page body as a proper hero heading; FMP-UI-15 layered a "Factory Operations Control Center" tagline, a helper line, and a small executive summary strip onto it; FMP-UI-15B removed the helper line and the entire summary strip (clutter feedback); FMP-UI-23B eased both lines' sizing/spacing to help fit the new 11-card grid; FMP-UI-23C removed the "RECAFCO Factory Management Platform" title line ENTIRELY (sidebar already carries the brand) and promoted the former tagline to be the page's one `<h1>`; **FMP-UI-23D** sized that single heading up one more step at the smaller breakpoints and replaced the hero-wrapper's `space-y-1.5` with a real `gap-6 lg:gap-8` (24px→32px) so the heading no longer reads as "attached to" the cards — a direct "too cramped" follow-up to FMP-UI-23C's own change.
- Key tokens/classes (current, FMP-UI-23D): `<h1 className="text-center text-3xl font-extrabold tracking-tight text-text-primary sm:text-4xl lg:text-5xl">Factory Operations Control Center</h1>` (was `text-2xl sm:text-3xl lg:text-5xl` — `lg:text-5xl` itself is unchanged across FMP-UI-23C→23D, already at the "if it still fits well" ceiling; only the mobile/tablet steps grew) — the ENTIRE hero, one line, no subtitle/product name/chip strip. The wrapping `<div className="flex flex-1 flex-col justify-center gap-6 lg:gap-8">` (was `space-y-1.5`) is the same block that vertically-centers the 3-row/11-card grid — switching from `space-y` (margin-top on non-first children only) to CSS flex `gap` was deliberate: `gap` applies uniformly between EVERY adjacent child pair (heading→error-banner, heading→grid, or banner→grid on the rare error/empty-cards path), so the heading can't end up touching whatever renders directly below it in ANY state, not just the common no-error one.
- **FMP-UI-15 (superseded by FMP-UI-15B, superseded again by FMP-UI-23C) — summary strip history, kept for context:** the strip was always real-data-or-nothing, never invented — 3 always-safe chips (`{cards.length} Modules`, "Executive View", "Updated Today") alongside 3 OPTIONAL chips gated on real values already present on the fetched `cards` array. **FMP-UI-15B deleted it entirely** (`SummaryChip`, `findMetricValue`, the derived variables, the unused `PlatformModuleCard` import) — none of it survives.
- Accessibility behavior: the heading is the page's real `<h1>` (previously the header's title was a `<p>`, not a heading element at all) — unchanged by FMP-UI-23C/23D, just sized/spaced differently.
- Reusable takeaway 1: a "the header feels visually heavy" complaint about a page-specific title living in shared chrome is a sign that title belongs to the PAGE, not the header — shared chrome (`TopHeader`, used by every protected route) should only ever carry things true of every route it wraps, never one specific page's own content.
- Reusable takeaway 2 (FMP-UI-15): when a task suggests specific "nice to have" real-data chips for a summary strip, check whether those exact numbers are ALREADY being fetched for something else on the same page before reaching for a new API call.
- Reusable takeaway 3 (FMP-UI-15B) — an "additive, optional, honest-data" feature being fully correct and non-fabricated doesn't guarantee it earns its place long-term — a later, purely aesthetic "this feels cluttered" judgment call is a legitimate reason to remove a feature outright.
- Reusable takeaway 4 (FMP-UI-23C) — when a brand/heading is duplicated across 2 layout regions (here: the full product name in both the sidebar AND the page hero) and one of those regions gets a "too branded/repetitive" complaint, check whether the OTHER region already covers the same information before assuming the fix is "make it smaller" — the actual fix was removing the duplicate line entirely and promoting the one line that was still unique (the operational tagline, not the product name) to be the sole heading. Also: "make the heading bigger" and "keep the page compact" are not in tension when a multi-line block is being replaced by a single, larger line — the net line COUNT dropping can offset a larger font size dropping the height.
- Reusable takeaway 5 (FMP-UI-23D) — `space-y-*`'s margin-top-on-non-first-children mechanism and CSS flex `gap` LOOK interchangeable for a simple 2-child block, but stop being equivalent the moment a 3rd conditional sibling (here: the error/empty-cards banner) can appear between them — `gap` covers every adjacent pair uniformly for free, `space-y` needs the SAME value to already have been applied consistently to be equivalent, and a future value bump to one is easy to forget to re-verify against the conditional-sibling case. When a spacing task explicitly separates "gap between A and B" as its own named requirement (as this ticket did), prefer `gap` on the shared flex/grid parent over `space-y`/manual margins, especially once more than 2 children can occupy that parent.

### Breadcrumbs
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/breadcrumbs.tsx`
- Purpose: Page-level breadcrumb trail with accessible markup.
- Variants: Items with `href` render as links; last item always has no `href` (current page).
- Key tokens/classes: `text-text-muted text-sm`; links use `hover:text-text-primary`; ChevronRight separator from lucide-react.
- Accessibility behavior: `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">`; last item gets `aria-current="page"`.
- Used by: All administration pages, (protected) landing pages.
- Notes: Props: `items: BreadcrumbItem[]` where `BreadcrumbItem = { label: string; href?: string }`.

### MetricCard
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/metric-card.tsx`
- Purpose: Dashboard metric card showing a label, value, icon, and status.
- Variants: `ok` (shows value, optionally links), `restricted` (Lock icon badge), `unavailable` (WifiOff icon badge).
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-surface rounded-lg border border-border p-5 shadow-sm`
- Accessibility behavior: Wraps in `<Link>` only when `href && status === 'ok'`; status badges use icon + text.
- Used by: each module's own `*/dashboard/page.tsx` (production, safety-compliance, maintenance, incidents, factory-tasks) and Contract Management's KPI grids/summary cards. (No longer used by the root `(protected)/page.tsx`, which is now a redirect to `/dashboard` — see `ExecutiveModuleCard` for the new Executive Platform Dashboard, FMP-UI-01.) FMP-UI-07 — also used indirectly via `ExecutiveKpiGrid` on every Executive Module Landing Page.
- Notes: `MetricStatus = 'ok' | 'restricted' | 'unavailable'`. Props: `label`, `value?: string | number | undefined`, `icon: LucideIcon`, `iconColor?`, `href?`, `status`, `source?`. Export `MetricCardProps` and `MetricStatus`.

### ExecutiveModuleCard
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/dashboard/_components/executive-module-card.tsx`
- Purpose: Compact, premium-style dashboard card for the Executive Platform Dashboard (`/dashboard`) — one per main platform module (Contract Management, Estimation, Technical, Erection, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Production & Planning, Maintenance Management, Storage Yard & Delivery, Quality Control, Task Management — 11 since FMP-UI-23, was 10 since FMP-UI-10, was 8 originally; see `_lib/executive-modules.ts` for the full rename/reorder history of these titles). Deliberately separate from `MetricCard`; not for detail-page KPI strips.
- Variants: Single variant, 11 color accents (`ModuleAccent`: `contracts`/`estimation`/`technical`/`erection`/`qaqc`/`storage`/`safety`/`incident`/`production`/`maintenance`/`tasks` — see `ACCENT_PALETTE`; `estimation` added FMP-UI-23, a gold/mustard tone distinct from `erection`'s amber and `storage`'s slate; `qaqc` and `storage` added FMP-UI-10, deliberately picked to NOT be near-duplicates of `production`'s purple or `erection`'s amber since both sit close to those in the card order). Each metric renders either a number (`text-[17px] font-extrabold`, **FMP-UI-23B** — eased down from the 22–28px band across FMP-UI-09B/10C/15, part of the 11-card no-scroll compaction pass) in a `palette.light`-tinted tile at ~56% opacity (FMP-UI-06B — was a *solid* `palette.light` fill in FMP-UI-06, which still read as "color-blocked"; before that, flat `bg-background` grey), or, when `value === null`, "Not available" in muted text — never a fabricated 0 (FMP-UI-10's QA/QC and Storage & Delivery cards, and FMP-UI-23's Estimation card, are the ones where every single metric is `null` — none of the 3 has a real backend yet).
- **Button color — module-specific via literal hex, never a CSS token (FMP-UI-05, unchanged mechanism in FMP-UI-06):** FMP-UI-04D had removed all per-module button color after 3 straight "invisible button" reports; FMP-UI-05 brought it back through `ACCENT_PALETTE` — a literal hex string per module (e.g. `contracts: { base: '#1e3a8a', light: '#eff6ff' }`) applied via `style={{ backgroundColor: palette.base }}` on the `<Link>` — no CSS custom property, no Tailwind color utility class, involved anywhere. Hover was `hover:brightness-90`, eased in FMP-UI-06 to `hover:brightness-95` (milder, "not too heavy") — still a generic token-free Tailwind filter utility. Text is the plain `text-white` utility. **Why literal hex, not `var(--color-module-*)`:** FMP-UI-04C tried a `var(--color-module-*)` inline style and it still failed live, because it and the Tailwind class both ultimately read the same custom property from whatever CSS the browser had actually loaded — a literal hex needs no CSS resolution of any kind. Verified in the compiled build output every round since: all 8 hex values present, zero `bg-module-*`/`bg-accent` classes remain on the button.
- Icon badges reuse `ACCENT_PALETTE.light`/`.base` via inline `style` (the same literal-hex mechanism as the button) since FMP-UI-05 replaced FMP-UI-04D's separate `ICON_ACCENT_CLASSES` Tailwind-token approach — one single per-module color source for the whole card (icon badge + metric tiles + left accent + button), not two parallel systems.
- Key tokens/classes (FMP-UI-06/06B/09B polish, eased back down one step in FMP-UI-10C for the 5-column grid, refined again in FMP-UI-14 and FMP-UI-15, eased down ONE MORE step in **FMP-UI-23B** for the 11-card no-scroll grid): `rounded-xl border border-border/60 bg-surface shadow-sm p-3` (was `p-3.5`) plus a **3px** left accent (thinned from FMP-UI-05's 4px) at ~80% opacity via an 8-digit hex (`` `${palette.base}cc` ``, CSS's own alpha-channel-in-hex syntax — no color-math helper needed) — still an inline `borderLeftWidth`/`borderLeftColor` style overriding just that one side of the otherwise-neutral class-based border. **FMP-UI-15** added `hover:border-border` (full opacity on hover, `/60` at rest — a small refined hover cue) alongside the existing `hover:shadow-lg`. Icon in a `size-8` rounded-lg badge (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `size-9`; icon itself `size-4`/16px, was `size-4.5` — `palette.light` bg + `palette.base` fg via inline style); header row is `items-start` (icon `mt-0.5`) so the icon aligns with the title's TOP line rather than its vertical midpoint once a long name wraps to 2 lines; title `text-[15px] font-bold leading-tight` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `18px`); description `text-[11px]` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `text-xs`) in `text-text-muted` with a `mt-0.5` gap under the title (was `mt-1`), never `truncate`; metric tile `rounded-lg` on `` `${palette.light}70` `` at `px-2 py-1.5` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `rounded-xl`/`px-2.5 py-2`), gap eased `gap-2.5`→`gap-2` (**FMP-UI-23B**; FMP-UI-15 had widened it the other way, `gap-2`→`gap-2.5`), with the number at `text-[17px]` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `22px`) and a small accent dot (`palette.base`) beside each `text-[11px]` label (was `text-xs`) — the label row is `items-start` (dot `mt-1`) so a label wrapping to 2 words-per-line doesn't look vertically mis-centered against its own bullet dot; header-to-metrics gap `mt-2` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `mt-2.5`).
- **FMP-UI-15 — status badge, header top-right, platform-wide semantic tokens (not the module's own literal-hex accent):** `<span>Live</span>` (`bg-success-light text-success`) or `<span>Setup Pending</span>` (`border border-border bg-surface-secondary text-text-secondary`) sits in a `flex items-start justify-between` row alongside the icon+title block, computed from the same `isPlaceholder = metrics.every(m => m.value === null)` this component already derived — no new prop. Deliberately uses this app's existing `--color-success`/`-light` semantic tokens (the same ones error banners etc. already use elsewhere) rather than `ACCENT_PALETTE`'s literal hex — a "is this module live" status is a platform-wide concept, not something that should vary by the module's own brand color.
- Layout (structure unchanged since FMP-UI-04D): card root `flex flex-col h-full min-h-48` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `min-h-56` — still a floor only, never a cap); header+metrics wrapped in a `flex-1` div; button-look element lives in its own `mt-auto pt-2` wrapper (was `pt-2.5`), `w-full`, `min-h-9` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `min-h-10`) at `py-1.5 text-[13px]` (was `py-2 text-sm`) (`min-h`, not a fixed `h`, so a long label like "Open Quality Control" can wrap to 2 lines and grow the button instead of clipping it; the grid's own `align-items: stretch` default then keeps every other card in that row matching the taller one, so heights stay equal across the row regardless of which card's button wrapped), `rounded-lg`. **FMP-UI-15** added the button's own resting `shadow-sm` plus `group-hover:shadow-md` (alongside the existing `group-hover:brightness-95`) for a slightly more premium hover lift, matching the shadow-on-hover language the login page's submit button already uses. No `overflow-hidden`, no `max-h-*` anywhere in this component — nothing left that could ever clip the button.
- **FMP-UI-09B — every size bumped one step, NOT stretched to fill leftover row height** (FMP-UI-10C eased every one of these back down one step for the narrower 5-column grid — the underlying "don't stretch, resize the content" principle is unchanged): the previous card's natural content height (~240px) left a large one-sided empty gap below the whole 2-row grid on a typical 1080p desktop `<main>` (~900–950px tall). The fix grows the card's own actual content (icon/title/description/metric/button sizes and paddings) rather than using `xl:auto-rows-fr` to stretch the existing smaller content to fill a taller row — stretching would have just relocated the same "too much empty space" complaint to a gap between the metrics and the button instead of removing it. The dashboard page (`dashboard/page.tsx`) separately centers the whole grid within its own available height so any leftover slack past the card's own natural size becomes balanced top/bottom breathing room instead of one lopsided gap at the bottom.
- **Placeholder status block, computed from data already present, not a new prop:** when every metric's `value` is `null` (`metrics.every(m => m.value === null)`), the 2×2 metrics grid is replaced by one compact box instead of 4 repeated "Not available" tiles. Applies automatically to Estimation, Storage Yard & Delivery, and Quality Control today (and any future all-null placeholder module) — no backend flag or new prop needed, since the metrics array already fully describes this state. Wording history: FMP-UI-10C's "Status: Coming next / Data: Not configured yet" → FMP-UI-14's "Module Status" label + "Configuration Pending" value → **FMP-UI-15's current wording**, a bold `<p>Setup Pending</p>` headline plus a plain `<p>Module will be configured in a future unit.</p>` explanatory line — deliberately sharing the words "Setup Pending" with the header badge above it (both now read as one consistent state, not two differently-worded half-finished-sounding ones). **FMP-UI-23B** shrank this block's own padding/text (`px-3 py-2.5`→`px-2.5 py-2`, headline `text-sm`→`text-[13px]`, note `text-xs`→`text-[11px]`) — deliberately kept SHORTER than the compacted 2-row metrics grid beside it, not just proportionally re-scaled, so a placeholder card sharing a grid row with a live card can never become the row's tallest member (see this unit's own reusable takeaway below). Same detection logic, same honest "no fake numbers" guarantee across every wording/sizing revision — cosmetic only, never the underlying data contract.
- No disabled "Coming Soon" state exists on this component (still deliberately not added, considered again in FMP-UI-06 and FMP-UI-10): `PlatformDashboardService` only ever includes a card for a user who already holds real access to reach its real route (a permission for the real built modules, or `isExecutiveManagerOrAdminAccess` for Estimation, Storage Yard & Delivery, and Quality Control — see that function's own doc comment), so no rendered card can have an unavailable route; a not-yet-built module still gets a real, working card and route, just with every metric honestly `null` (rendered via the placeholder status block above, not fake tiles).
- **FMP-UI-07B — whole card is the clickable target, not just the button:** the card root is now the `<Link>` itself (`href` moved here); "Open `{title}`" became a plain `<span>` styled identically (HTML forbids a nested `<a>` inside an `<a>`, so only one real anchor can exist per card). `group` on the root + `group-hover:brightness-95` on the span reproduces the exact same hover feedback the button alone used to give, now triggered by hovering anywhere on the card. The focus ring moved from the (no-longer-focusable) span onto the root `<Link>` — one clear keyboard focus stop per card, not two pointing at the same destination. Senior-friendly rationale: a full-card hit target is far easier to hit reliably than a small button near the bottom.
- Accessibility behavior: the whole card is a real `<Link>`; focus ring via `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-focus focus:ring-offset-2` on that root element (moved here in FMP-UI-07B, previously on the inner button).
- Used by: `(protected)/dashboard/page.tsx`, in a `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3 xl:grid-cols-4` grid (FMP-UI-23 — eased back from `xl:grid-cols-5`, FMP-UI-10's own 10-card width, now that Estimation makes 11 cards: 5 columns would orphan the 11th card alone on its own row (5+5+1); 4 columns gives a clean 3-row 4+4+3 spread instead), `gap-2.5` (**FMP-UI-23B**, was `gap-3`). Sits directly under the page's error/empty states — FMP-UI-04B removed the page's own hero card entirely (see `TopHeader`'s centered title for where it went).
- Notes: Props: `title`, `description`, `href`, `icon: LucideIcon`, `metrics: PlatformMetric[]` (`{ label: string; value: number | null }`, from `@/lib/platform-api`), `accent: ModuleAccent` (exported type, unchanged shape across every round; the page's own `CARD_ACCENTS: Record<PlatformModuleCode, ModuleAccent>` maps each card to its accent). FMP-UI-01 (original) → FMP-UI-03 (compact/colored) → FMP-UI-04 (density pass) → FMP-UI-04B (layout hardening, hero removed) → FMP-UI-04C (dual-path color fix, still failed live) → FMP-UI-04D (all per-module button color removed) → FMP-UI-05 (per-module button color restored via literal hex, never a token) → FMP-UI-06 (card/metric-tile/typography weight polish; icon badges folded into the same literal-hex `ACCENT_PALETTE` the button already used) → FMP-UI-06B (metric tiles diluted further — solid tint still read as "color-blocked"; header title moved out of this component's own remit into `TopHeader`, sized up, subtitle removed) → FMP-UI-07 (`ACCENT_PALETTE`/`ModuleAccent` extracted out to `_lib/module-accent.ts` so the new Executive Module Landing Pages can reuse the exact same colors; this file now imports and re-exports them — zero visual change here. `href`s now come from `PlatformDashboardService` pointing at each module's landing page instead of its operational dashboard — see `ExecutiveModuleNav` below) → FMP-UI-07B (root element became the `<Link>` itself so the whole card is clickable; the button became a `<span>` — see this component's own note above) → **FMP-UI-23B** (every size dimension eased down one more notch — see this entry's own "Key tokens/classes" line above for the full list — so 11 cards in 3 rows fit a normal 1920×1080 viewport with no page scroll; zero mechanism change). Label is literally `Open {title}`, which already equals the exact required string since that's the card's own title from `PlatformDashboardService`; never a separate label lookup table. (The previous `ModuleCard` component was removed in FMP-UI-01 — its only caller, the old root dashboard, was replaced by this page.)

### Contract Management submenu in the executive sidebar (FMP-UI-26)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/sidebar.tsx` (executive branch of `<nav>`, `executiveContractSubItems`, `isExecutiveContractSubItemActive`).
- Pattern: a flat executive row with children. The child list is the same permission-filtered `visibleContractItems` the non-executive dropdown uses, so a permission change affects both. It renders only while one of its routes is current, which keeps other modules to one open list at a time.
- Child styling: indented `ml-6` with a `border-nav-hover` rule, `text-[15px]` labels, `size-4` icons, `py-2`. Active child: `border-l-4 border-accent bg-nav-active font-semibold`.
- Parent active rule: the row is active on its own landing page or while any child is current. Technical and Erection keep their own flat rows and do not light up Contract Management.
- **FMP-UI-27 — the parent is a disclosure button, not a link.** It toggles the child list (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls="executive-contracts-nav-items"`) and shows a right-aligned `ChevronRight` when collapsed or `ChevronDown` when expanded. Open state starts `true` on submenu routes and re-opens on navigation in (`executiveContractsOpen` + effect). Child labels were raised to `text-base` / `py-2.5` for older users. Its own dashboard is reached through the child "Dashboard" item.
- **FMP-UI-30 — cleaned from 10 children to 7:** Dashboard, Contract List, Payments, Issue Log, Claim Log, Closeout Requests, Contract Parties. Removed: Schedule (now its own main-sidebar item, "Schedule Planning" — see `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS` below), Workflow & Team Tasks (overlapped with the separate Task Management module), Erection Dashboard (overlapped with the separate Erection module). Because the child list comes from the SAME `CONTRACT_ITEMS` array the non-executive dropdown and the Contract-Management-only flat section both read, this cleanup applied to all 3 render paths at once, not just this one. `executiveContractSubActive` (the parent's own active check) automatically stopped including `/contracts/schedule` once Schedule left `CONTRACT_ITEMS` — no logic change needed there, only the data it reads shrank.
- Reusable takeaway: when a sidebar has two render branches (flat for one persona, grouped for others), a feature added to only one branch is invisible to the other persona. Check every branch before concluding a menu is missing.
- Reusable takeaway 2 (FMP-UI-30): when several render paths derive their item list from ONE shared array, removing an item from that array is a single edit with a 3-way effect — worth naming explicitly in the change's own doc comment (as `CONTRACT_ITEMS`'s now does) so a future reader doesn't have to re-discover which other 2 places are affected.

### Executive-only sidebar mode (FMP-UI-03)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/sidebar.tsx` (`EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS`, `isExecutiveItemActive`, the `executiveMode` branch inside `<nav>`); classifier in `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_lib/module-visibility.ts` (`isExecutiveManagerAccess`)
- Purpose: a permission-only classifier (true when a user can see all 6 operational modules but has no Administration access — exactly the `EXECUTIVE_MANAGER` role's shape, never checked by role code) switches the sidebar to a flat list with no "Dashboard" link (redundant — that IS the landing page for this shape), larger text/icon/spacing (`text-base`/`size-5`/`py-3.5`, vs `text-sm`/`size-4`/`py-2` elsewhere), and a clearer active state (solid `bg-nav-active` + 4px accent left border). Every other persona's existing nav rendering (`MAIN_GROUPS`, the Contract Management dropdown, the Contract-Management-only flat section, Administration) is wrapped untouched in the `else` branch of one ternary — zero regression risk for any other role.
- FMP-UI-23 (superseded by FMP-UI-28 below) — was reordered to 11 items (Contract Management, Estimation, Technical, Erection, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Production & Planning, Maintenance Management, Storage Yard & Delivery, Quality Control, Task Management) and relabelled "Incident Report" → "Incident Management", "Production Planning" → "Production & Planning", "Quality Assurance & Control" → "Quality Control" — those renames still stand.
- FMP-UI-28 (superseded by FMP-UI-29 below) — reordered to the real factory workflow and removed Estimation (10 items): Contract Management, Technical, Production & Planning, Storage Yard & Delivery, Erection, Quality Control, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Maintenance Management, Task Management. Removing it was reversed one unit later; the order chosen for the other 9 items still stands. The now-unused `Calculator` icon import was removed, then restored in FMP-UI-29.
- **FMP-UI-29 — Estimation restored, right after Contract Management (11 items):** direct feedback, after seeing the FMP-UI-28 sidebar live, that it was missing. Placed there (not at the end) because cost estimation/quotation is a pre-contract-execution step in the real workflow — the same reasoning FMP-UI-23 originally used. It is NOT a shared array with `_lib/executive-modules.ts`'s `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`, which keeps its own copy and was never touched by either FMP-UI-28 or this unit — the Platform Dashboard card, module switcher, and Previous/Next for Estimation were unaffected by either change. Final order: Contract Management, Estimation, Technical, Production & Planning, Storage Yard & Delivery, Erection, Quality Control, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Maintenance Management, Task Management.
- **FMP-UI-29 — font size increased, direct feedback:** across the Contract Management button, its submenu, and every flat item, `text-base` (16px) → `text-lg` (18px); row icons `size-5`→`size-6`; submenu/chevron icons `size-4`→`size-5`; submenu row `py-2.5`→`py-3`. Scoped to the executive flat list's own classes only — the dropdown, Administration section, and Contract-Management-only flat section (all shared with other personas) are untouched.
- **FMP-UI-30 — Schedule Planning added after Task Management (12 items):** Contract Management, Estimation, Technical, Production & Planning, Storage Yard & Delivery, Erection, Quality Control, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Maintenance Management, Task Management, Schedule Planning. Same route (`/contracts/schedule`, was Contract Management's own "Schedule" submenu item), same `Calendar` icon, no new route/permission. Needs no active-state special case — `isExecutiveItemActive()`'s existing generic fallback (the one every href besides `/contracts/executive` already uses) handles it. Also added, same position (after Task Management), to the non-executive `MAIN_GROUPS` "Operations" array and to `CONTRACTS_MANAGEMENT_EXTRA_ITEMS` (renamed from `TECHNICAL_AND_ERECTION_ITEMS`, the Contract-Management-only persona's own copy of Technical/Erection) — so no persona loses their only sidebar path to this page once it left the Contract Management submenu.
- FMP-UI-10 (Estimation/Quality Control/Storage Yard & Delivery have no dedicated permission; see `isExecutiveManagerOrAdminAccess` in `module-visibility.ts`) — moot for this list as of FMP-UI-28 (Estimation removed; Quality Control and Storage Yard & Delivery still ride on the section-level `isExecutiveManagerAccess` gate alone, unchanged). If either ever gets a real dedicated permission, add a proper per-item check then rather than leaving it permanently riding on the section-level gate alone.
- FMP-UI-28 — sidebar width `w-64` (256px) → `w-70` (280px), both the desktop `<aside>` and the mobile drawer. `w-70` is Tailwind v4's own token for 17.5rem — no arbitrary-value class needed.
- **FMP-UI-29 — widened again, `w-70` → `w-80` (320px, a standard Tailwind token, both `<aside>`s),** to keep the longer labels from wrapping more tightly now that the executive list renders at `text-lg` instead of `text-base`. The bottom user-info footer and every other section size from the same parent width via padding/truncation, not a fixed width, so nothing else needed to change.
- **FMP-UI-10C — spacing loosened for the now-reliably-2-line labels:** item `py-3`→`py-3.5`, icon-to-label `gap-3.5`→`gap-3`, items container `space-y-1`→`space-y-1.5`. `leading-snug` (1.375) and `text-base` font size were deliberately left unchanged — 1.375 already sits inside this unit's own requested 1.25–1.35-ish range, and shrinking the font to fit longer labels would have worked against "aged managers need readability," the opposite of what more padding is for.
- Reusable takeaway: when a persona needs a meaningfully denser/larger *or* meaningfully shorter nav than everyone else (not just 1-2 items hidden — see CM-41's "swap the whole source list" precedent), branch the *rendering*, not the item-filtering — wrap the entire existing JSX for every other persona in one `{condition ? <NewVariant/> : (<>{existingUnchangedJSX}</>)}`, so the new variant can have completely different classes/behavior without risking a single line of the existing, already-verified path for other roles.
- `isExecutiveItemActive()` — a `/dashboard`-suffixed href's generic "active for its whole base path" rule (`isActive()`) assumes the base belongs to one module alone; `/contracts/dashboard` breaks that assumption since Technical/Erection/Schedule/etc. are siblings under the same `/contracts` prefix with their own nav entries. Reusable takeaway: before reusing `isActive()`'s dashboard-suffix shortcut for a new flat nav item, check whether its base path is actually single-owner — if siblings exist under the same prefix, fall back to an exact/nested match instead.
- FMP-UI-04 — item row switched `items-center` → `items-start` with the icon at `mt-0.5` and the label at `leading-snug`, so a label that wraps to 2 lines at this sidebar width ("Maintenance Management") reads as top-aligned/intentional instead of the icon floating in the vertical middle of two lines.
- FMP-UI-05 — `rounded-md` → `rounded-r-md` on the item so the active/hover row's left accent border sits flush against the sidebar's edge instead of curving away from it; hover background softened (`hover:bg-nav-hover` → `hover:bg-nav-hover/70`) and given `duration-150` for a smoother transition.
- FMP-UI-07 — `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS` hrefs now point to each module's Executive Module Landing Page (e.g. `/safety-compliance/executive`, not `/safety-compliance/dashboard`), kept in sync with `_lib/executive-modules.ts`. `isActive()`'s "`/dashboard`-suffix also matches the bare module base path" rule was generalized to also cover `/executive`-suffixed hrefs (same reasoning, so a landing-page item still highlights while viewing a record reached from it). `isExecutiveItemActive()`'s exact-match override — needed because Contract Management shares its `/contracts/*` prefix with Technical/Erection's own sidebar entries, unlike every single-owner module — moved from guarding `/contracts/dashboard` to guarding the new `/contracts/executive`. `CONTRACT_TOP_LEVEL_SLUGS` (used by the separate, non-executive `isContractItemActive()`) gained `executive`/`erection-executive` so the regular Contract List item's own active-state logic isn't confused by the two new sibling routes either.

### ExecutiveModuleNav (FMP-UI-07, module-switcher chips collapsed behind a disclosure in FMP-UI-16)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-module-nav.tsx`
- Purpose: shared header navigation for every Executive Module Landing Page — breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > [Module]"), text-labeled Back to Platform Dashboard / Previous / Next buttons, and a module-switcher. One component so all 11 landing pages (FMP-UI-23 added Estimation as the 11th) agree on ordering and styling.
- Variants: Previous/Next buttons render conditionally (absent at the first/last module the VIEWER can see — see the FMP-UI-07B access note below, this is no longer just "first/last in the fixed order").
- Key tokens/classes: nav buttons `h-9` (**FMP-UI-16**, was `h-11` — this row is navigation chrome, not a primary content action, so it doesn't need the same touch-target height) with visible text labels, never icon-only (explicit senior-friendly requirement). **FMP-UI-16 — the "Other modules" chip row is now collapsed by default**, behind a native `<details>`/`<summary>` "Switch module" button styled identically to the other nav buttons (no client JS/state needed — free keyboard/screen-reader support). It used to always render below Back/Previous/Next (a caption + a full chip row, permanent vertical cost on all 10 pages whether or not anyone used it) — a "the page scrolls too much" report specifically named this row. The sidebar (always visible, lists every module) plus Back/Previous/Next already cover "get to any other module," so the chip row was a second, redundant path to the same thing; it's now zero-height until a manager actually opens the disclosure, at which point the exact same chips render inline (same styling as before — active chip keeps the literal-hex `ACCENT_PALETTE`, inactive chips stay neutral grey, per FMP-UI-07D).
- Accessibility behavior: chip row (once opened) is `role="navigation" aria-label="Switch module"`; active chip gets `aria-current="page"`. The `<details>` element itself is natively keyboard-operable (Enter/Space toggles) with no extra work.
- Used by: every Executive Module Landing Page (`/contracts/executive`, `/executive/estimation`, `/contracts/technical`, `/contracts/erection-executive`, `/safety-compliance/executive`, `/incidents/executive`, `/production/executive`, `/maintenance/executive`, `/executive/storage-delivery`, `/executive/qaqc`, `/factory-tasks/executive`) — so this one change reduced the "Other modules" vertical footprint on ALL 11 pages, not just Contract Management (the page FMP-UI-16 was pilot-redesigned on).
- Notes: Props: `code: PlatformModuleCode`, `permissions: string[]` (FMP-UI-07B — required, not optional). Order/metadata comes from `_lib/executive-modules.ts`'s `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`/`getVisibleModules()`/`getModuleNeighbors()` — the single source of truth also used by `sidebar.tsx`'s `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS`. **FMP-UI-23** reordered this array to the required sequence and added the `ESTIMATION` entry — every page using this component picked up the new order/chip automatically, with zero changes to this component itself.
- **FMP-UI-07D — a real, separate bug fixed elsewhere, not in this component:** the breadcrumb this component renders was always correct, but `TopHeader` was independently rendering a SECOND, wrong breadcrumb above it for 3 Contract Management routes — fixed in `_lib/contract-workspace-breadcrumb.ts`, not here.

### ExecutiveQuickLinks (FMP-UI-07B)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-quick-links.tsx`
- Purpose: a row of secondary shortcut buttons on each Executive Module Landing Page straight into that module's own existing filtered list views (e.g. Maintenance's "Waiting for Parts", Production's "Scheduled") — sits above the page's one primary "View [Records]" button, never replacing it.
- Variants: renders `null` if given an empty `links` array (no module currently needs this, but keeps the component honest rather than rendering an empty section).
- Key tokens/classes: `h-10` secondary buttons, `border border-border bg-surface` (never the primary accent color — visually subordinate to the one primary CTA per page).
- Used by: all 8 Executive Module Landing Pages, each with its own hardcoded `links` array of real routes/query params already supported by that module's own list page today (verified by reading each list page's filter-parsing logic before adding any link) — never a fabricated route.
- Notes: Props: `links: QuickLink[]` where `QuickLink = { label: string; href: string }`.
- **FMP-UI-07C — "Operational Dashboard" convention:** every module that has a real, separate `/module/dashboard` route distinct from its own list page (Contract Management, Safety, Incidents, Production, Maintenance, Task Management) now leads its Quick Links with `{ label: 'Operational Dashboard', href: '/module/dashboard' }` — the explicit path back to the full dense dashboard from the simplified landing page, so "operational details only when needed" always has exactly one obvious link. Erection doesn't need this entry — its primary "View Erection Work Queue" button already targets `/contracts/erection-dashboard` directly. Technical has no separate operational dashboard, so it has none either. Add this same first-link convention to any future module that gets both a landing page and a separate dense dashboard.
- **FMP-UI-10 note:** QA/QC and Storage & Delivery don't use this component at all — they have no operational routes to link to yet (see `ExecutiveComingSoon` below instead).

### ExecutiveComingSoon (FMP-UI-10)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-coming-soon.tsx`
- Purpose: the entire body of a placeholder Executive Module Landing Page for a module with no real backend yet — an icon and a plain message, deliberately never a fake table, record, or metric. This is the ONLY module-body content Quality Control's, Storage Yard & Delivery's (renamed from "QA/QC"/"Storage & Delivery" in FMP-UI-10C; "Quality Assurance & Control" → "Quality Control" in FMP-UI-23), and Estimation's (new, FMP-UI-23) landing pages render (besides `ExecutiveModuleNav`/`ExecutiveModuleTitle` and a "Back to Platform Dashboard" button) — no `ExecutiveKpiGrid`, no `ExecutiveAttentionPanel`, no `ExecutiveQuickLinks`, since none of those have any real data to show yet either.
- Variants: Single variant.
- Key tokens/classes: `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface-secondary p-8 text-center`, a muted `Construction` icon.
- Used by: `/executive/qaqc`, `/executive/storage-delivery`, `/executive/estimation` (FMP-UI-23).
- Notes: Props: `message: string`. Reusable as-is for any future module that reaches this same "landing page exists, real module doesn't yet" stage. See `ExecutiveModuleCard`'s own FMP-UI-10C note for the equivalent "all metrics null" placeholder treatment used on the DASHBOARD CARD itself (a different, more compact component — this one is the landing page's own body).

### ExecutiveModuleTitle (FMP-UI-07, simplified in FMP-UI-07D, gained an optional actions slot in FMP-UI-16)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-module-title.tsx`
- Purpose: the landing page's own header block — module icon/title/one-line description, optionally with primary actions on the right.
- Variants: Single variant, plus the optional `actions` slot (see below).
- Key tokens/classes: icon badge `size-14 rounded-xl` using the module's `ACCENT_PALETTE` (light bg, base fg); title `text-3xl font-bold`. **FMP-UI-16** wrapped the existing icon+title+description block and the new actions slot in `flex flex-col gap-4 lg:flex-row lg:items-start lg:justify-between` — stacked below `lg`, side-by-side (actions right-aligned) at `lg` and up.
- **FMP-UI-16 — optional `actions?: React.ReactNode` prop, purely additive:** when passed, renders in a `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 lg:justify-end` block to the right of the title on desktop. Every page that doesn't pass it (every Executive Module Landing Page except Contract Management, as of this unit) renders byte-for-byte identically to before — this was specifically so updating this ONE shared component could benefit Contract Management immediately without visually changing the other 9 pages at all. Purpose: a manager's primary actions (e.g. Contract Management's "View Contract List" button + its Quick Links row) now sit right next to the title instead of in a separate section at the bottom of the page — visible without scrolling. See `contracts/executive/page.tsx` for the first real usage; other module pages are candidates to adopt this same slot next (see this unit's own progress-tracker entry).
- Used by: every Executive Module Landing Page, directly under `ExecutiveModuleNav`.
- Notes: Props: `title`, `description`, `icon: LucideIcon`, `accent: ModuleAccent`, `actions?: React.ReactNode` (FMP-UI-16). Pure presentational.

### ExecutiveKpiGrid (FMP-UI-07, gained an optional fixed-column-count prop in FMP-UI-16, a `dense` passthrough in FMP-UI-16B)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-kpi-grid.tsx`
- Purpose: renders every field a module's own real dashboard API already returns as a `MetricCard`, so a landing page's "Summary" section is never a hand-picked subset or a recomputed figure — whatever the module's own dashboard already computes is shown in full, humanized (`totalOpen` → "Total open") and colored by a name-based heuristic (`overdue`/`critical`/`blocked` → `text-error`; `open`/`pending`/`waiting`/`paused`/`progress` → `text-warning`; `completed`/`resolved`/`closed`/`ready` → `text-success`; else `text-accent`).
- Variants: Single variant; `null`/`undefined` values render `MetricCard`'s own `status="unavailable"` state, never a fabricated number. Default grid is responsive `grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-4` (unchanged) unless the new `columns` prop is passed.
- **FMP-UI-16 — optional `columns?: 2 | 3 | 4` prop:** forces a fixed column count instead of the responsive escalation. Needed because the default breakpoints key off VIEWPORT width, not the grid's own container width — placed inside a narrower column (e.g. side-by-side with "Needs Attention"), the default classes would still try to force 4 columns into a space only wide enough for 2 on any real desktop viewport. Optional and additive; every existing caller that doesn't pass it keeps the exact original responsive behavior.
- **FMP-UI-16B — optional `dense?: boolean` prop:** forwarded straight to each `MetricCard`'s own existing `dense` prop (smaller icon/number/padding). Contract Management passes both `columns={2} dense` once its Summary column narrowed from an even half (FMP-UI-16) to ~65% of the page width (FMP-UI-16B), so its 2×2 tiles stay comfortably sized rather than cramped. Optional and additive; every existing caller that doesn't pass it keeps `MetricCard`'s original (non-dense) sizing.
- Used by: all 10 Executive Module Landing Pages (Erection excludes its one non-numeric `paymentPendingAfterErectionAvailable` flag before passing metrics in).
- Notes: Props: `metrics: Record<string, number | null | undefined>`, `icon: LucideIcon`, `columns?: 2 | 3 | 4` (FMP-UI-16), `dense?: boolean` (FMP-UI-16B).

### ExecutiveAttentionPanel (FMP-UI-07; Contract Management stopped using this component in FMP-UI-16B — see that page's own bespoke panel below)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/executive-attention-panel.tsx`
- Purpose: the landing page's "Needs Attention" section. Three real branches, never a fabricated one: `recordItems` (real per-record rows — Erection's `overdueAttention`), `countItems` (real aggregate counts, e.g. `overdueTasks`), or `available={false}` with an honest `note` (Technical, Production, and Contract Staff-tier all use this branch today, each for a different real reason — see progress-tracker.md's FMP-UI-07 entry).
- Variants: `available: false` → muted note panel. `recordItems` present and non-empty → clickable list, each row linking to its own real record. `countItems` present → by default only entries with `value > 0` render, as warning-tinted stat chips (an alert list); pass `showZeroCounts` (FMP-UI-07D) to render every entry including zero instead, styled neutrally grey rather than warning-orange for the zero ones. Either present-but-empty case → `emptyMessage` (defaults to "Nothing currently needs attention in this module.").
- Key tokens/classes: warning chips use `border-warning/30 bg-warning-light`; zero-count chips (FMP-UI-07D) use `border-border bg-surface-secondary` with `text-text-secondary` for the number instead of `text-warning` — all existing tokens, not new ones.
- Used by: 7 of the 11 Executive Module Landing Pages (Technical, Erection, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Production & Planning, Maintenance Management, Task Management). **Not** Contract Management as of FMP-UI-16B (see below) — Estimation, Quality Control, and Storage Yard & Delivery don't use it either (they render `ExecutiveComingSoon` instead).
- Notes: Props: `available: boolean`, `note?`, `recordItems?: AttentionRecordItem[]`, `countItems?: AttentionCountItem[]`, `showZeroCounts?` (default `false`), `emptyMessage?`. Exported types `AttentionRecordItem { id, label, description?, href }` and `AttentionCountItem { label, value, href? }`. Optional props typed `T | undefined` explicitly (not just `?`) — required by this repo's `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true`, per CM-71H.8's own reusable takeaway below.
- **FMP-UI-16B — why Contract Management stopped using this component:** a manager reported its `countItems` chip-row rendering as "loose chips, not a structured executive panel," with no way to distinguish severity (every chip used the same warning-orange tint once its value was non-zero) and no way to mark one figure as deliberately neutral. Rather than redesign this shared component's `countItems` rendering (which would have changed the same chip row on the other 6 pages that use it too, none of which asked for that), Contract Management now renders its own bespoke row-list panel directly in `contracts/executive/page.tsx` — same precedent as that page's own pre-existing bespoke Recent Contract Updates table. See that page's own entry below for the replacement's shape.

### Compact executive detail page layout (FMP-UI-16, piloted on Contract Management only; refined further in FMP-UI-16B and FMP-UI-16D)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/executive/page.tsx` — this is a PAGE-COMPOSITION pattern, not a new shared component; the pieces it's built from (`ExecutiveModuleNav`, `ExecutiveKpiGrid`, `ExecutiveQuickLinks`) are shared, but how one page arranges them is decided per-page, in each `page.tsx` — there is no single `ExecutiveModuleLayout` wrapper component all 10 pages render through. (As of FMP-UI-16B, this page's Needs Attention section is its own bespoke panel, not the shared `ExecutiveAttentionPanel`; as of FMP-UI-16D, this page no longer imports or renders `ExecutiveModuleTitle` at all — see both components' own registry entries above.)
- Purpose: a manager reported the Contract Management landing page "scrolls unnecessarily, actions are placed too far down, Other Modules chips take too much space, everything is stacked vertically." This pattern addresses all 4 at once: actions moved into the hero (via `ExecutiveModuleTitle`'s new `actions` slot), Summary+Needs Attention placed side-by-side (`grid lg:grid-cols-2`) instead of stacked, the old bottom "Actions" section removed entirely (its content moved to the hero, not duplicated), and Recent Contract Updates capped at 5 rows (was 6).
- Key tokens/classes: outer container `max-w-6xl space-y-5 px-5 py-5 lg:px-6` (was `max-w-5xl space-y-8 px-5 py-6`); Summary/Needs Attention wrapper `grid gap-4 lg:grid-cols-2` (each section keeps its own `space-y-3` heading+content, just side-by-side now instead of full-width stacked); hero actions block passed as `<ExecutiveModuleTitle actions={<>...</>}>` containing the same accent-colored primary button (resized `h-12`→`h-10` to match the Quick Links row's own height, "consistent height" per this unit's own requirement) plus the unchanged `ExecutiveQuickLinks` row.
- Reusable takeaway: this is a PILOT, not a platform-wide change — Technical, Erection, Safety & Compliance, Incident Report, Production Planning, Maintenance Management, and Task Management all still use the older single-column stacked composition (Summary → Needs Attention → Recent Activity → a separate bottom Actions section). They CAN adopt the exact same pattern cheaply now, since the prerequisite shared-component changes (`ExecutiveModuleTitle`'s `actions` slot, `ExecutiveKpiGrid`'s `columns`/`dense` props) already exist platform-wide — the remaining work for each page is purely the page-composition change demonstrated here, not any new shared-component work. See progress-tracker.md's FMP-UI-16/FMP-UI-16B entries for the explicit list of which pages still need this.
- **FMP-UI-16B — refined the same pilot further, all still Contract-Management-specific:**
  1. **Module Header Card:** the title+actions row is now wrapped in `<div className="rounded-xl border border-border bg-surface p-5 shadow-sm lg:p-6">` — an actual card, instead of floating directly on the page background. `ExecutiveModuleTitle` itself has no card styling of its own; this page just wraps it.
  2. **Summary/Needs Attention split:** changed from an even `lg:grid-cols-2` to an explicit ~65/35 split, `lg:grid-cols-[13fr_7fr]` (13:7 ≈ 65:35). `ExecutiveKpiGrid` now also passes `dense` so its 2×2 tiles stay comfortably sized in the narrower ~65% column.
  3. **Needs Attention is now a bespoke row-list panel**, not the shared `ExecutiveAttentionPanel`'s chip row — see that component's own FMP-UI-16B note above for why, and `ExecutiveAttentionPanel`'s registry entry for the exact replacement shape (a `rounded-xl border bg-surface` card containing a `divide-y` list of full-width `<Link>` rows, each with a label and a bold, color-coded number: `text-error` for Overdue Workflow Tasks/Critical Contracts, `text-warning` for Open Claims, always `text-text-primary`/neutral for Closing Soon regardless of value).
  4. **Recent Contract Updates** capped at 3 rows (was 5).
  - Reusable takeaway: when a shared component's exact rendering doesn't match one page's own specific new spec (here: `ExecutiveAttentionPanel`'s chip row vs. a requested "structured row-list panel" with per-item severity), building that ONE page its own bespoke version (as this page already did for its Recent Contract Updates table) is preferable to changing the shared component's behavior for every other page that uses it too, none of which asked for the change.
- **FMP-UI-16D — removed the repeated module identity block entirely, not just restyled it:** a manager pointed out that the FMP-UI-16B "Module Header Card"'s icon + "Contract Management" title + one-line description repeated information already obvious from the sidebar's active item, the breadcrumb (`ExecutiveModuleNav`, unchanged), and the module card the manager had just clicked to get here — none of it new information, just vertical space. This page no longer imports `ExecutiveModuleTitle` at all (that shared component itself is untouched — still rendered by the other 9 Executive Module Landing Pages exactly as before). The card that used to hold the title+actions row now holds ONLY the actions, relabeled with a small "Quick Actions" heading (`text-sm font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-text-secondary`, matching every other section heading's own styling on this page) — same primary "View Contract List" button + the same `ExecutiveQuickLinks` row, unchanged content and hrefs, now the very first real content section after navigation instead of sharing a card with a repeated title.
  - Reusable takeaway: "this information is already visible elsewhere on the same screen" (sidebar active state + breadcrumb + the card just clicked) is a stronger reason to DELETE a section than to restyle it — FMP-UI-16B had already made this same title block look more polished (a proper card), but polish doesn't fix a section that shouldn't exist at all. Worth checking, next time a "make this look nicer" request comes in for content that's arguably redundant with navigation chrome already on screen, whether the more valuable fix is removal rather than another visual pass.

### PermissionGate
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/permission-gate.tsx`
- Purpose: Client-side conditional render based on permission presence in the user's permission array.
- Variants: Single variant; `fallback` prop for alternate content.
- Key tokens/classes: None — pure logic.
- Accessibility behavior: No wrapper elements; renders children or fallback directly.
- Used by: Pages that need to conditionally render UI based on permissions.
- Notes: Props: `permission: string`, `permissions: string[]`, `children: ReactNode`, `fallback?: ReactNode`.

### StatusBadge
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/administration/_components/status-badge.tsx`
- Purpose: Display active/inactive state of any organization entity.
- Variants: `isActive: true` → green pill; `isActive: false` → muted grey pill.
- Key tokens/classes: `bg-success-light text-success` / `bg-surface-secondary text-text-muted`; `rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium`
- Accessibility behavior: `aria-label` attribute set to "Active" or "Inactive".
- Used by: `departments/page.tsx`, `plants/page.tsx`, `locations/page.tsx`, `users/page.tsx`, `roles/page.tsx`
- Notes: Requires `isActive: boolean` prop only.

### PageHeader
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/administration/_components/page-header.tsx`

### LoginForm (kept up to date across FMP-UI-08/08C/11/12/12B/12C/12F/17I — see "Centered factory command center login layout" above for the surrounding page; this entry covers the form itself)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/login/_components/login-form.tsx`
- Purpose: the login form — email field (labeled "Email", placeholder "Enter company email" as of FMP-AUTH-01; the input keeps name="username" so the API can still accept an old internal username silently), password field (with a show/hide toggle since FMP-UI-12), submit button, inline error banner on failed login.
- Variants: Single variant; shows the error banner (`role="alert"`) only when `state?.error` is set.
- Key tokens/classes: `h-12` inputs with a leading `Lock`/`User` icon; password field also has a trailing `Eye`/`EyeOff` toggle button (FMP-UI-12) — `pl-11 pr-11` on that one input to clear both icons. **FMP-UI-17I** added `placeholder="Enter email ID or username"` / `placeholder="Enter password"` to the 2 inputs, styled `placeholder:text-text-muted` (the same muted token the leading icons already use) — a native HTML attribute, not a `value`, so the fields stay uncontrolled and autofill/autocomplete behavior is unaffected; no layout shift, since it renders inside the same fixed `h-12` box. Focus rings are `focus:ring-nav` (navy) on every input and the button (FMP-UI-11 — moved off `ring-accent`/red specifically so a plain focused field never looks like it has an error; the error banner's own `border-error`/`text-error` is a different token, so an actual error stays visually distinct from focus). Submit button: `bg-accent` with `disabled:opacity-50` while `isPending`, label swaps to "Signing in…" — this already satisfies "disable during submit / show loading state / prevent double submit," unchanged since FMP-UI-08.
- Accessibility behavior: every input has an associated `<label>`; error shown with `role="alert"`; the password-visibility button is a real `type="button"` (never submits the form) with `aria-label` swapping between "Show password"/"Hide password" and its own `focus-visible:ring-nav` ring.
- Used by: `app/login/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useActionState(loginAction, null)`; the password field's `showPassword` state (FMP-UI-12) only flips the native input's `type` attribute — it stays an uncontrolled field (no `value`/`onChange`), so toggling visibility never clears or resets whatever the user already typed. Server action (`login/actions.ts`) sets cookies and `redirect()`s to `/welcome` on success (see that entry above) or `/change-password` if a forced password change is pending, unaffected by anything in this file. **FMP-UI-12F** — this form previously (FMP-UI-12B/12C) also cleared the welcome screen's `sessionStorage` "already shown" flag on submit-button click and on mount, to work around that screen's once-per-tab skip guard. That guard was removed entirely in FMP-UI-12F (see the welcome-transition entry above), so this form no longer touches `sessionStorage` at all — it only submits the form now, same as it did before FMP-UI-12B ever introduced the workaround.

## API Patterns (Unit 06)

### PermissionGuard + @Permissions() — Server-side Authorization Pattern
- Guard: `apps/api/src/common/guards/permission.guard.ts`
- Decorator: `apps/api/src/common/decorators/permissions.decorator.ts`
- Purpose: Gate any controller endpoint on one or more permission codes loaded live from DB.
- Usage: Apply `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard, PermissionGuard)` at controller class level; add `@Permissions('module.action')` at handler level. Multiple codes = AND (all required).
- Permission resolution: `JwtAuthGuard` loads `role.permissions[]` from DB on every authenticated request (live, no cache). `PermissionGuard` reads the loaded `user.permissions` string array.
- Used by: `UsersController`, `RolesController`, `DepartmentsController`, `PlantsController`, `LocationsController`
- Notes: No `@Permissions()` on a handler = public within the guard chain (any authenticated user passes). `Reflector.getAllAndOverride` checks handler then class, enabling per-endpoint overrides.

### AuthUser Type — Authenticated Request Context
- Path: `apps/api/src/common/types/auth-user.ts`
- Purpose: Shape of `req.user` after `JwtAuthGuard` processes a request.
- Fields: `id`, `username`, `displayName`, `roleId`, `roleCode`, `roleName`, `permissions: string[]`, `mustChangePassword`, `isActive`, `sessionId`, `departmentId: string | null`
- Notes: `permissions` is an array of dot-notation codes (e.g. `'users.read'`). DB is authoritative — never trust JWT for role/permissions. `departmentId` populated from DB by `JwtAuthGuard` (Unit 15); used by `DepartmentAccessService.buildDeptFilter` for `OWN_DEPARTMENT` scope.

### RoleId-based User Mutations
- Pattern: Role assignment uses `PATCH /administration/users/:id/role` with `{ roleId: string }` body; `UpdateUserDto` has no role field.
- Notes: Privilege escalation guard in `UsersService.updateRole()`: only `SUPER_ADMIN` may assign `SUPER_ADMIN`. Last-active-SUPER_ADMIN protection in both `deactivate()` and `updateRole()`.

### IncidentForm (sections/Location field added FMP-UI-22; evidence info note added FMP-INC-01B; real optional evidence upload added FMP-INC-01C; date/transition fixes FMP-INC-01D)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_components/incident-form.tsx`
- Purpose: Create / edit form for incident reports — title, severity, occurredAt (datetime-local), description, immediateAction, affected plant, affected department, affected location, optional evidence files (create only).
- Variants: Used for both new (create) and edit (DRAFT update) by passing different `action` and `defaultValues`.
- Key tokens/classes: `space-y-6 max-w-2xl`; 3 local `SectionCard`s (Incident Details / Immediate Response / Location) + a 4th (Evidence Attachments) when `showEvidenceUpload`; standard form input tokens.
- Accessibility behavior: All inputs labelled; required fields marked with aria-hidden asterisk; field errors shown with `role="alert"`.
- Used by: `incidents/new/page.tsx`, `incidents/[id]/edit/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useActionState(action, { error: null })`. Props: `action`, `submitLabel`, `plants: OrgRef[]`, `plantsFailed?`, `departments: OrgRef[]`, `deptsFailed?`, `locations: OrgRef[]`, `locationsFailed?`, `defaultValues?: Partial<Incident>`, `showEvidenceUpload?` (renamed from `showEvidenceNote`, FMP-INC-01C). **FMP-UI-22** — grouped into 3 sections (Incident Details / Immediate Response / Location); added a real "Location / area" field (`affectedLocationId` — already existed on `CreateIncidentDto` and was already read by `createIncidentAction`, but no form field ever rendered it); added a local `OrgSelect` sub-component giving Plant/Department/Location each the same 3-state treatment (fetch-failed error / genuinely-empty message / real select) `DepartmentAndPlantFields` (factory-tasks, FMP-UI-20D) and `SafetyInspectionForm` (safety-compliance, FMP-UI-21C) already established — none of the 3 fields are required for an incident (`CreateIncidentDto`'s own `@IsOptional()`), so nothing here blocks submission. The old top-of-page "saved as draft" framing was removed; a small note near the submit button covers the same fact without making it the page's main message. **FMP-INC-01C** — the form's own submission wiring changed from `<form action={dispatch}>` to `onSubmit={handleSubmit}`: staged `File` objects (local `useState`, from the new `EvidenceFilePicker`) are appended to a real `FormData(e.currentTarget)` under `evidenceFiles` before calling `dispatch(formData)` directly.
- **FMP-INC-01D** — that manual `dispatch(formData)` call was itself calling `useActionState`'s returned action OUTSIDE a transition (the exact cause of React's "called outside of a transition" console warning); fixed with `startTransition(() => { dispatch(formData); })` (React's own documented fix for this exact situation) — `pending` now correctly tracks the submission again. Same `handleSubmit` also now converts the raw `datetime-local` value to a real UTC ISO string (`.toISOString()`) before it's sent — a naive local-time string has no timezone of its own, and would otherwise be parsed by the SERVER in the SERVER's own timezone, not the reporting user's. A `maxOccurredAt` value (the `occurredAt` input's `max` attribute) is computed once in a `useEffect` AFTER mount, never during render — computing `new Date()` directly in a client component's render body would embed the SERVER's clock into the SSR output while the CLIENT's hydration pass computes a different value from the browser's own clock, a real hydration mismatch; this is purely a UX helper, `IncidentsService.validateOccurredAt()` remains the real, unbypassable check.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-22) — a shared form component's Props change (adding `locations`/`locationsFailed` here) forces every call site to be updated to keep compiling; when that happens, apply the SAME real fix to the sibling call site too (here: `[id]/edit/page.tsx`'s own identical admin-gated dropdown bug) rather than leaving it half-fixed next to its now-correct twin.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01C) — "the id doesn't exist yet" (FMP-INC-01B's blocker for evidence upload on create) doesn't mean the FILES can't be collected yet — only that the UPLOAD REQUEST has to wait. Staging files in the same client component that already owns the form, then uploading them server-side right after the id becomes known (inside the same create action, before its own redirect), turns a real technical constraint into a single seamless save instead of a 2-step "create, then go find the upload button" flow.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01D) — any manual call to a `useActionState` action (as opposed to letting `<form action={dispatch}>` call it automatically) MUST be wrapped in `startTransition` — this is a general rule for this entire codebase's growing set of "onSubmit intercepts the form to augment FormData first" patterns, not specific to this one form; the same risk exists anywhere else that pattern gets reused.

### ActivityTimeline (EVIDENCE_UPLOADED/EVIDENCE_REMOVED added FMP-INC-01; `comments` prop removed, universal wording, STATUS_CHANGED specific labels added FMP-INC-01E)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_components/activity-timeline.tsx`
- Purpose: Chronological list of `IncidentActivity` events only (as of FMP-INC-01E — no longer merges in comments).
- Variants: Each row shows a bullet dot + label + actor + optional detail line (previous→new status, severity change, reopen reason) + timestamp.
- Key tokens/classes: `space-y-4`; bullet `size-6 rounded-full bg-surface-secondary`.
- Accessibility behavior: Rendered as `<ol aria-label="Incident activity timeline">`.
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props (FMP-INC-01E): `activities: IncidentActivity[]` only — `comments` was dropped entirely; the detail page now renders its own separate Comments card directly. Every event renders as `"{label} by {actor}"` (FMP-INC-01E made this the ONE universal convention — previously only `EVIDENCE_UPLOADED`/`EVIDENCE_REMOVED` used this order via a `BY_ACTOR_EVENTS` set, which is now gone). `STATUS_CHANGED` events (the single generic event every real transition — submit/start review/begin investigation/request actions/resolve/close — logs server-side) now map through a new `STATUS_CHANGE_LABELS` keyed by the real `newStatus`, giving each one its own specific label ("Incident submitted", "Review started", etc.) instead of the identical unhelpful "Status changed" line all 6 previously shared.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01E) — when a backend logs one generic event key for several distinct real transitions, don't try to fix that server-side just to improve wording; map the generic event's own discriminating field (here: `newStatus`) to a specific label at the display layer — same shape as `STATUS_CHANGE_LABELS` here, reusable anywhere else a module's activity log has the same "one key, many real meanings" shape.

- Purpose: All status transition buttons for the incident detail sidebar; shows inline panels for inputs (cancel reason, reopen reason, resolve summary, assign, severity change).
- Variants: Rendered buttons depend on `incident.status` + `permissions`; inline panel opened by `activePanel` state (one at a time).
- Key tokens/classes: `space-y-2` button list; inline panels `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-4 space-y-3`.
- Accessibility behavior: Buttons disable during pending transitions; confirm buttons also disabled if required inputs are empty.
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useTransition`. Props: `incident: Incident`, `currentUserId: string`, `permissions: string[]`, `people: UserRef[]`. Handles `INCIDENT_OPEN_ACTIONS` resolve confirmation flow with checkbox. **FMP-INC-01E** — every button/inline-panel-header label Title-Cased ("Submit" → "Submit Incident", "Start review" → "Start Review", "Begin investigation" → "Begin Investigation", "Request corrective actions" → "Request Corrective Actions", "Resolve incident" → "Resolve Incident", "Close incident" → "Close Incident", "Cancel incident" → "Cancel Incident", "Reopen"/"Reopen incident" → "Reopen Incident", "Assign investigator" → "Assign Investigator", "Change severity" → "Change Severity", "Edit draft" → "Edit Draft"); DRAFT's Submit Incident button gained a real helper line ("Submit this incident when the details and evidence are ready for review."); the previously-silent `if (buttons.length === 0) return <></>;` now returns a real message ("No actions available to you on this incident right now.").
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01E) — a multi-button transitions panel returning an empty fragment when no action applies reads as a rendering bug to anyone who can't tell "nothing to show" apart from "broken" — always render a real, honest message instead of silently nothing, matching the same fix already applied to this pattern in other modules.

### InvestigationPanel

- Accessibility behavior: Labels associated with textareas; error shown with `role="alert"`.
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useActionState(updateInvestigationAction.bind(null, incidentId), { error: null })`. Props: `incidentId`, `rootCause`, `investigationSummary`.

- Used by: `incidents/executive/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props: `items: IncidentNeedsAttentionItem[]`. Empty message: "No incidents currently need urgent attention." Deliberately scoped to critical+open only — Incidents have no due-date field, so no "overdue incident" concept was invented (unlike Safety's findings, which DO have `dueAt`).

### IncidentRecentList (new, FMP-UI-22)

- Used by: `incidents/executive/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props: `items: IncidentDashboardData['recent']`, `canCreate: boolean`. Slices to 3 regardless of the backend's own cap (8). Reuses the same `safetyApi`-style already-fetched dashboard data — no new request. Empty state differentiates by `canCreate`.

### IncidentModuleNav (new, FMP-UI-22)
