- Purpose: Back to Incident Dashboard / Back to Platform Dashboard / Previous / Next / Switch module, for the All Incidents list page. Modeled directly on `safety-compliance/_components/safety-module-nav.tsx` (FMP-UI-21D) — same reasoning for not reusing `ExecutiveModuleNav` wholesale, and the same both-prev-and-next shape (not `TaskModuleNav`'s prev-only shape), since `INCIDENT_REPORT` sits mid-array in `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`, not last.
- Used by: `incidents/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props: `permissions: string[]`.

### IncidentDetailNav (new, FMP-UI-22)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_components/incident-detail-nav.tsx`
- Purpose: 3 fixed buttons for the Incident Detail page — Back to Incident Dashboard (`/incidents/executive`), Back to All Incidents (`/incidents`), Back to Platform Dashboard (`/dashboard`). Mirrors `safety-compliance/_components/safety-detail-nav.tsx` (FMP-UI-21D) exactly — a dedicated 3-destination component, not `IncidentModuleNav` (built for the list page, which has no "back to all incidents" button since that would be circular there).
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Pure RSC, no props, no state.

### Incident Control Center (`incidents/executive/page.tsx`, redesigned FMP-UI-22)
- Purpose: same route (`/incidents/executive`), same access rule (`incidents.read`), same `incidentsApi.dashboard()` call as before — replaces the old generic Summary/Needs-Attention-counts/Recent-Activity/Quick-Links landing page with an operational hub, the same redesign shape as Task Management (FMP-UI-20) and Safety & Compliance (FMP-UI-21). `ExecutiveModuleTitle`'s `actions` slot holds "+ Report Incident" (gated `incidents.create`) and "All Incidents". 5 `MetricCard`s (Open Incidents / Critical Open / Under Investigation / Resolved This Month / Closed Total) — all 5 already existed on `getDashboard()`. `IncidentNeedsAttentionList` replaces the old counts-only panel. Recent Activity removed, replaced by `IncidentRecentList` ("Recent Incidents"). Quick Links replaced with exactly 2 non-duplicate filter chips (Open Incidents / Critical) placed next to the Recent Incidents heading — "+ Report Incident"/"All Incidents" are NOT repeated there since they're already the header's own actions.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-22) — when a brief's own "Quick Actions" list mostly repeats buttons already shown elsewhere on the page (here: 2 of 4 requested links duplicated the header actions), only add the non-duplicate remainder, placed near the section they actually relate to — never repeat a button just because a list names it.

### All Incidents list page (`incidents/page.tsx`, navigation/wording added FMP-UI-22)
- Purpose: same route, same data, same filters/search/table/pagination as before. Added `IncidentModuleNav` row + 3-level breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > Incident Report > All Incidents", was 1 level with no `href`). Title "Incident Report" → "All Incidents"; subtitle → "View, search, and open incident reports." Create button relabeled "+ Report Incident" (was "Report Incident"). Empty state differentiates "no create permission" ("No incidents found in your current scope.") from "create permission exists" ("No incidents found." + "Report the first incident or adjust your filters." + a real create button).

### Incident Detail page (`incidents/[id]/page.tsx`, navigation added FMP-UI-22; Evidence Attachments section added FMP-INC-01, moved higher FMP-INC-01B; full card-based polish FMP-INC-01E)
- Purpose: same route, same data, same permission/status/ownership logic as before — this page's core structure (description, investigation panel, corrective actions, real transitions via `IncidentTransitionsPanel`, human-readable `ActivityTimeline`) was ALREADY real and working before FMP-UI-22, confirmed by full-file audit (unlike Safety & Compliance's detail page before FMP-UI-21D, which had 2 real bugs). FMP-UI-22 added `IncidentDetailNav` + a 3-level breadcrumb. FMP-INC-01 added a real **Evidence Attachments** section (`IncidentEvidenceAttachments`), backed by the new attachment endpoints/model this unit built — never placeholder UI. **FMP-INC-01B** moved that section from after the conditionally-rendered Immediate Action block to directly after Description, so it's unconditionally the 2nd section on the page (previously 2nd or 3rd depending on whether `immediateAction` happened to be set) — per direct feedback that it needed to be visible without scrolling far.
- **FMP-INC-01E** — full visual polish pass, same "Control Center" card shape already used by Factory Tasks (FMP-UI-20G) and Safety & Compliance (FMP-UI-21D). New Incident Summary header card (eyebrow, reference, title + status/severity badges, a Status/Severity/Occurred/Reported By/Next Step grid, plus a separate highlighted Next Step callout box using `computeIncidentNextStepLabel()`/`computeIncidentNextStep()` from the new `_lib/incident-detail-helpers.ts`). Every section (Description, Evidence Attachments, Immediate Action, Investigation, Corrective Actions, Comments, Activity) now shares one `cardClass` constant (`rounded-xl border border-border bg-surface p-5 shadow-sm`) — Resolution kept its own distinct `border-success bg-success-light` styling deliberately, to flag a positive/terminal state. **Comments and Activity split into 2 separate cards** — previously interleaved inside `ActivityTimeline` itself (which took a `comments` prop); that prop is now gone, and the page renders its own Comments list + `AddCommentForm` directly. Available Actions panel renamed from "Actions"; Incident Details panel renamed from "Details" and expanded to always show Status/Severity/Department/Plant/"Location / Area" (with "Not specified"/"Not assigned" fallbacks instead of hiding the row when empty). The Evidence Attachments upload controls (Take Photo/Record Video/Upload Files) were confirmed ALREADY real and working before this unit (built FMP-INC-01/FMP-INC-01C) — this unit only made them visually unmistakable via the same card styling, not a functional fix.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-22) — "redesign the dashboard, improve the detail page" briefs should still start with a full read of the detail page before touching it — this module's detail page needed only navigation, while Safety & Compliance's needed a real rebuild; assuming the same amount of work every time wastes effort in one direction and risks under-delivering in the other.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01B) — placing a new section "after a conditionally-rendered block" silently makes its own position conditional too; anchor to an ALWAYS-rendered section (here: Description) when a brief asks for a fixed, predictable position "near the top."
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01E) — before "fixing" a reported visibility complaint, read the actual component first: the real upload controls already existed and worked here, so the fix was prominence (card styling, heading weight), not new functionality — conflating the two would have meant claiming credit for work already done in an earlier unit.

### incident-detail-helpers.ts (new, FMP-INC-01E)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_lib/incident-detail-helpers.ts`
- Purpose: pure functions computing the Incident Detail page's Next Step guidance — `computeIncidentNextStepLabel(status)` (short label for the header grid's "Next Step" row) and `computeIncidentNextStep(status)` (full-sentence explanatory callout), one pair per `IncidentStatus`. Mirrors the same dual-representation "Next Step" pattern already established for Factory Tasks (FMP-UI-20G) and Safety & Compliance (FMP-UI-21D).
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Fully unit-tested (`incident-detail-helpers.test.ts`, 5 tests) — exact wording checked for DRAFT/SUBMITTED/CLOSED, non-empty sentence checked for every other status, and label uniqueness checked across all 8 statuses. Each label/sentence names only a real action `IncidentTransitionsPanel` can actually perform for that status — never an invented workflow step.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01E) — this is the 3rd module (after Factory Tasks, Safety & Compliance) to need "short label in a summary grid + full sentence in a callout" for the same status — worth recognizing as a standing pattern rather than re-deriving its shape each time a new module's detail page gets this treatment.

### IncidentEvidenceAttachments (new, FMP-INC-01; empty-state wording updated FMP-INC-01B; refactored onto EvidenceFilePicker FMP-INC-01C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_components/incident-evidence-attachments.tsx`
- Purpose: the Incident Detail page's real Evidence Attachments section — list (filename, friendly type, size, uploaded-by, uploaded-date, "View"/"Delete") + `EvidenceFilePicker` (selection + staged-files preview) + a batch "Upload Evidence" submit button of its own.
- Key tokens/classes: staged-file rows `rounded-md border border-border bg-surface px-2.5 py-1.5`; upload area `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface-secondary p-3`.
- Used by: `incidents/[id]/page.tsx` only.
- **FMP-INC-01B** — empty state changed to "No evidence files attached yet." + (only when `canUpload` is true) "Use Take Photo, Record Video, or Upload Files to add supporting evidence." — the action hint is never shown to a viewer who doesn't actually have those buttons.
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useTransition` + `useRouter().refresh()` after each batch/delete (mirrors `contract-closeout-request-attachments.tsx`'s own established pattern — NOT `useActionState`, since a file batch needs to keep uploading after each individual file, which a single form-submit action can't express). Props: `incidentId`, `attachments: IncidentAttachment[]`, `currentUserId`, `canUpload` (`incidents.create`), `canManage` (`incidents.manage`). **FMP-INC-01C** — the selection buttons/staged-list markup moved into the new shared `EvidenceFilePicker`; this component now only owns the existing-attachments list, its own "Upload Evidence" button, and the actual upload/delete server-action calls. Delete button shown only when `canManage || attachment.uploadedByUserId === currentUserId`, matching the server's own real enforcement (never just a client-side hint).
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01) — uploading several files in one user action is easiest to express as "loop over File objects, call the upload server action once per file" rather than one multi-file request — the backend's `FileInterceptor('file')` (like every existing attachment endpoint in this codebase) only ever accepts one file per request, so the LOOP lives in the client component, not the server.

### EvidenceFilePicker (new, FMP-INC-01C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_components/evidence-file-picker.tsx`
- Purpose: the 3-button (Take Photo / Record Video / Upload Files) selection UI + staged-files preview (remove before upload, inline validation errors) — extracted out of `IncidentEvidenceAttachments` so the Report Incident create page's new optional evidence section and the Incident Detail page's existing upload area share ONE implementation of "select → validate → preview → remove."
- Key tokens/classes: same hidden-`<input>`-triggered-by-visible-button pattern as before extraction; staged-file rows `rounded-md border border-border bg-surface px-2.5 py-1.5`.
- Used by: `incident-form.tsx` (create page, no submit button of its own — staged files ride the outer form's own submit) and `incident-evidence-attachments.tsx` (detail page, which adds its OWN "Upload Evidence" button + upload logic around it).
- Notes: `'use client'`, purely presentational + local button refs — never uploads anything itself. Props: `staged: StagedEvidenceFile[]`, `onAddFiles(fileList)`, `onRemove(index)`, `disabled?`. "Take Photo"/"Record Video" use hidden `<input type="file" accept="image/*|video/*" capture="environment">` triggered by a visible button (never a raw file input styled as a button) — browsers/devices without camera support simply fall back to their own normal file picker, no code branch needed.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01C) — a "select files → validate → preview → remove" UI is genuinely reusable independent of WHAT happens to the files afterward (upload immediately vs. stage for a later outer-form submit) — separating the STAGING UI from the UPLOAD ACTION let 2 different call sites with different upload timing share the exact same component.

### evidence-validation.ts (new, FMP-INC-01C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/incidents/_lib/evidence-validation.ts`
- Purpose: single source of truth for evidence file rules — `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES`, `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` (25MB), `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_ACCEPT_ATTR`, `validateEvidenceFile()`, `stageEvidenceFiles()`, `formatEvidenceFileSize()`, `friendlyEvidenceFileType()`. Must match `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES`/`INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` in `apps/api/src/incidents/incident-attachment-storage.service.ts` exactly — the server independently enforces the same rule and remains the actual source of truth; this is a real UX convenience (fail fast), never the only check.
- Used by: `evidence-file-picker.tsx`, `incident-evidence-attachments.tsx`, `incident-form.tsx`.
- Notes: Fully unit-tested (`evidence-validation.test.ts`, 7 tests). Exact required messages: "File type is not supported." / "File is too large. Maximum size is 25MB." — applied consistently everywhere evidence validation happens (previously the detail page had its own slightly different "File size is too large." wording before this unit consolidated both places onto one shared implementation).

## API Patterns (Unit 08)

### IncidentsService — Concurrency-safe Status Transitions
- Pattern: `updateMany(where: { id, status: currentStatus })` + count check; throws `INCIDENT_CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION` if count === 0.
- Used by: All status transition methods in `IncidentsService`.
- Notes: Prevents TOCTOU races; never use findUnique + update pair for status transitions.

### IncidentsService — occurredAt Future-Date Validation (test coverage added FMP-INC-01D)
- Pattern: `validateOccurredAt(date)` compares real `Date` instants (`date.getTime() > now.getTime() + 60_000`, a 1-minute clock-skew tolerance) and throws `UnprocessableEntityException({ code: 'INCIDENT_OCCURRED_IN_FUTURE', message: 'occurredAt cannot be in the future' })`. Called first thing in `create()`/`updateDraft()`, before any department-access check or transaction.
- Used by: `IncidentsService.create()`, `IncidentsService.updateDraft()`; the frontend maps this exact `code` to a friendly `fieldErrors.occurredAt` message in `createIncidentAction`/`updateDraftAction` (`incidents/actions.ts`, FMP-INC-01D) rather than showing the raw string.
- Notes: **This comparison was already timezone-correct** — the real bug (fixed FMP-INC-01D) was upstream, in the FRONTEND sending a naive `datetime-local` string with no timezone marker, which `new Date(naiveString)` would parse as local time in the SERVER's own timezone rather than the user's. `incident-form.tsx` now converts to a real UTC ISO string (`.toISOString()`) before submission — see that component's own FMP-INC-01D note. 5 new tests in `incidents.service.test.ts` cover past/near-now/future/UTC-parsing — `create()` had zero test coverage before this unit.

### Atomic Sequence Upsert
- Pattern: `$queryRaw` with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (year) DO UPDATE SET last_seq = last_seq + 1 RETURNING last_seq`.
- Used by: `IncidentsRefService.nextRef()`.
- Notes: Prisma's standard `upsert` is not atomic across connections; `$queryRaw` is required for sequence safety.

### IncidentsService — Module-Scoped Departments/Plants/Locations Reference Endpoints (FMP-UI-22)
- Pattern: `GET /incidents/departments` / `/plants` / `/locations`, gated `incidents.read` (not the admin-only `org.departments.read`/`org.plants.read`/`org.locations.read`) — same precedent as `factory-tasks.controller.ts`'s `/departments`/`/plants` (FMP-UI-20D) and `safety.controller.ts`'s own (FMP-UI-21C).
- Used by: `IncidentsService.listDepartments()`/`listPlants()`/`listLocations()`; consumed by `incidents-api.ts`'s `incidentsApi.departments()`/`.plants()`/`.locations()`; used by `incidents/new/page.tsx` and `incidents/[id]/edit/page.tsx`.
- Notes: `{ id, name, code }[]`, `where: { isActive: true }`, ordered by name. No schema change.

### IncidentAttachmentStorageService + Evidence Attachment Endpoints (FMP-INC-01)
- Pattern: local-disk attachment storage, mirrors `ErectionMethodStatementAttachmentStorageService` exactly (random UUID on-disk filename per parent-entity subfolder, path-traversal-guarded `resolveAbsolutePath()`, best-effort `deleteFile()` that never throws). `GET/POST /incidents/:id/attachments`, `GET /incidents/:id/attachments/:attachmentId/download` (streams via `StreamableFile` + real `Content-Type`/`Content-Disposition`), `DELETE /incidents/:id/attachments/:attachmentId`.
- Used by: `IncidentsService.listAttachments()`/`createAttachment()`/`getAttachmentForDownload()`/`deleteAttachment()`; `IncidentsController`'s 4 new routes; consumed by `incidents-api.ts`'s `incidentsApi.listAttachments()` and `incidents/actions.ts`'s `uploadAttachmentAction()`/`deleteAttachmentAction()`/`createIncidentAction()` (FMP-INC-01C, via a shared private `uploadIncidentAttachment()` helper — same `POST /incidents/:id/attachments` endpoint, called once per staged file right after a new incident's id is known, no new backend code).
- Notes: `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES` (images: jpg/jpeg/png/webp; video: mp4/mov/webm; documents: pdf/doc/docx/xls/xlsx/csv/txt) and `INCIDENT_ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` (25MB, uniform — see this unit's own progress-tracker entry for why video isn't given a separate, larger limit) are defined fresh for this unit, not imported from an existing `*_ATTACHMENT_*` constant — every existing precedent's own allow-list (PDF/PNG/JPEG/XLSX/DOCX only) is narrower than incident evidence realistically needs. Delete is gated `incidents.create` at the route, with the finer uploader-or-`incidents.manage` ownership rule enforced inside `IncidentsService.deleteAttachment()` — mirrors that SAME service's own existing creator-or-manage convention (`submit()`/`cancel()`), not a new rule invented for attachments. `EVIDENCE_UPLOADED`/`EVIDENCE_REMOVED` activity rows are written on every upload/delete.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-INC-01) — when a module's real-world file-type needs are genuinely wider than every existing attachment precedent (here: video, webp, csv/txt — none of which any Contract attachment feature supports), define fresh constants rather than force-fitting an existing narrower allow-list; the STORAGE SERVICE SHAPE (save/resolveAbsolutePath/createReadStream/deleteFile) is what's worth reusing verbatim, not necessarily every existing feature's own specific MIME-type/size numbers.

## Factory Task Components (Unit 09)

### TaskStatusBadge
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/_components/task-status-badge.tsx`
- Purpose: Pill badge for the 8 factory-task lifecycle statuses.
- Variants: DRAFT (surface-secondary/muted), OPEN (secondary-accent), ASSIGNED (info), IN_PROGRESS (warning), BLOCKED (danger), COMPLETED (success), CLOSED (surface-secondary), CANCELLED (surface-secondary/muted).
- Key tokens/classes: `inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium`
- Accessibility behavior: Pure RSC; `aria-label` set to `Status: {label}`.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/my/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/assigned-by-me/page.tsx` (FMP-UI-20), `factory-tasks/_components/task-control-center-list.tsx` (FMP-UI-20)
- Notes: Props: `status: TaskStatus`. Exported as named export.

### TaskPriorityBadge
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/_components/task-priority-badge.tsx`
- Purpose: Pill badge for the 4 task priority levels (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH, URGENT).
- Variants: LOW (surface-secondary), MEDIUM (info), HIGH (warning), URGENT (danger).
- Key tokens/classes: `inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium`
- Accessibility behavior: Pure RSC; `aria-label` set to `Priority: {label}`.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/my/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/assigned-by-me/page.tsx` (FMP-UI-20), `factory-tasks/_components/task-control-center-list.tsx` (FMP-UI-20)
- Notes: Props: `priority: TaskPriority`. Priority is `LOW | MEDIUM | HIGH | URGENT` (never CRITICAL).

### TaskForm (regrouped into section cards + real "Assign To User" in FMP-UI-20B; single "Create Task" button in FMP-UI-20C; real dept/plant loading+error+empty states in FMP-UI-20D)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/_components/task-form.tsx`
- Purpose: Create / edit form for factory tasks. Create mode (`!isEdit`, computed from `Boolean(defaultValues?.id)`): title, description, **Assign To User** (real, only when `canAssign`; otherwise a plain sentence, never a fake field), responsibleDepartmentId (required), requestingDepartmentId, plantId ("Plant / Location"), priority, dueAt, optional incidentId, an honest note that attachments aren't supported yet and comments come after creation. Edit mode: same fields minus Assign To User and the Additional Details card (editing only ever touches a DRAFT's own fields, never assignment).
- Variants: Used for both new (create) and edit (DRAFT update) by passing different `action`/`defaultValues`/`canAssign`/`people`/`deptsFailed`/`plantsFailed`. **FMP-UI-20B** grouped every field into 4 `SectionCard`s (Task Details / Assignment / Priority & Schedule / Additional Details, create mode; Task Details / Assignment / Priority & Schedule, edit mode) instead of one flat `space-y-6` list. **FMP-UI-20C** collapsed the footer from 2 buttons (Save as Draft / Create & Assign Task) to ONE, always labelled via `submitLabel` ("Create Task" in create mode, "Save changes" in edit mode). **FMP-UI-20D** extracted the Responsible/Requesting Department + Plant/Location block (previously duplicated verbatim between the create and edit Assignment cards) into a shared `DepartmentAndPlantFields` sub-component with real loading/error/empty-state handling.
- Key tokens/classes: `SectionCard` = `rounded-xl border border-border bg-surface p-5 shadow-sm space-y-4`; form outer `space-y-5 max-w-2xl`; footer `flex items-center justify-between gap-3 border-t border-border pt-4` (Cancel left, one primary button right).
- Accessibility behavior: All inputs labelled; required fields marked with aria-hidden asterisk; field errors shown with `role="alert"`.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/new/page.tsx`, `factory-tasks/[id]/edit/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useActionState`. Props: `action`, `submitLabel`, `departments`, `deptsFailed?`, `plants`, `plantsFailed?`, `people?`, `canAssign?`, `canLinkIncident?`, `defaultValues?`. There is exactly ONE footer submit button; whether the task ends up assigned is decided entirely by whether `assignedToUserId` was filled in (read by `createTaskAction`), never by a second button. "Assign To User" only ever renders when `canAssign` is true. **FMP-UI-20D** — the submit button is also disabled when Responsible Department is required but genuinely unselectable (`deptsFailed || departments.length === 0`, AND no existing `defaultValues.responsibleDepartmentId` to fall back to), with a `title` tooltip explaining why.

### DepartmentAndPlantFields (new, FMP-UI-20D) — shared Responsible/Requesting Department + Plant/Location block
- Path: `factory-tasks/_components/task-form.tsx` (private to that file, not separately exported).
- Purpose: fixes the Responsible Department dropdown rendering with zero options for normal users. Root cause: `factory-tasks/new/page.tsx` (and identically `[id]/edit/page.tsx`) called the ADMIN-gated `organizations-api.ts`'s `departments.list()`/`plants.list()` (`GET /organizations/departments`, requires `org.departments.read`) — a permission a `tasks.create`-only user doesn't hold. The 403 was silently swallowed by `Promise.allSettled`, so the dropdown always rendered empty with no error. Fixed at the SOURCE (new `GET /factory-tasks/departments`/`/plants`, gated by `tasks.read` — see the backend entry below) and at the PRESENTATION layer here: 3 distinct states per field, never a silently empty `<select>` — (1) `deptsFailed`/`plantsFailed` (the fetch itself errored) → a real `role="alert"` error message; (2) fetch succeeded but zero active rows → "No active departments found. Please create departments in Administration first."; (3) real options → a normal `<select>`.
- Key reuse: same `OrgRef[]` shape (`{id, name, code}`) `organizations-api.ts` already used — only the DATA SOURCE changed, not the shape consumers render.
- Used by: both Assignment `SectionCard`s in `TaskForm` (create and edit mode) — previously 2 independently-duplicated copies of this same block, now one.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20D) — when a required `<select>` field can legitimately have zero real options right now (API error, or a genuinely empty reference table), NEVER leave it rendered with only its own blank placeholder option and call it done — that is indistinguishable from "broken" to the user. Show which of the 2 cases it is (error vs. genuinely empty) with different copy, and when there's also no existing value to fall back to, disable the submit button too — a disabled button with no explanation is still confusing, so pair it with the same message rather than relying on either alone.

### Backend: `/factory-tasks/departments` / `/factory-tasks/plants` reference endpoints (new, FMP-UI-20D)
- Path: `apps/api/src/factory-tasks/factory-tasks.service.ts`'s `listDepartments()`/`listPlants()`, `factory-tasks.controller.ts`'s `GET /factory-tasks/departments`/`/plants`.
- Purpose: active departments/plants for the New Task form's own dropdowns, gated by `tasks.read` (the same permission the whole factory-tasks module already requires) instead of the admin-only `org.departments.read`/`org.plants.read`. Mirrors `contracts.service.ts`'s own `listDepartments()`/`listPlants()` (and `production`'s `/production/departments`) EXACTLY — same `findMany({ where: { isActive: true } })` shape, same `{id, name, code}` projection, same `orderBy: name asc`. No schema change — same `Department`/`Plant` tables every admin-gated equivalent already reads from.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20D) — when a create/edit form in module A needs a reference list (departments, plants, people) that only an admin-tier permission currently exposes, check whether another module already solved this exact problem for itself (here: Contract Management's `/contracts/departments`, Production's `/production/departments`) before reaching for the admin endpoint or inventing something new. The right fix is almost always "give this module its own thin, `{module}.read`-gated reference endpoint over the same table," mirroring the precedent exactly rather than doing it differently a third way.

### Task creation assignment chain (FMP-UI-20B, simplified trigger in FMP-UI-20C) — create → open → assign
- Path: `factory-tasks/actions.ts`'s `createTaskAction`.
- Purpose: makes "Assign To User" on the create form genuinely work — not cosmetic — by calling the SAME 3 pre-existing, already-permission-gated endpoints `task-transitions.tsx`'s own Assign panel already uses (`POST /factory-tasks`, then `POST /:id/open`, then `POST /:id/assign`), in sequence, whenever the submitted form actually has an `assignedToUserId`. No new endpoint, no `CreateTaskDto` change, no schema change — the assignment column and both transition endpoints already existed for the post-creation "Assign" panel; this unit just lets the SAME calls happen immediately after creation when the viewer filled in that field.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20B) — "wire a UI-only field to a real capability" doesn't always mean adding a field to the primary create endpoint's own DTO. When 2 existing endpoints (created FOR a later step, `open`+`assign`) can be called back-to-back right after creation with no new validation/permission logic, chaining them from the frontend server-action layer is a real, honest feature — not a shortcut — as long as a failure partway through still lands the user somewhere they can see and finish the job (here: the task's own detail page, which already has the same real Open/Assign controls, per this action's own "always redirect once creation itself succeeds" behavior).
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20C) — the chain's trigger was originally a distinct button/`intent` value (FMP-UI-20B); replacing that with "does this optional field have a value" removed a whole validation branch ("Assign To User is required") for free, because there was no longer a user-visible CHOICE to validate — just data that either is or isn't there. When 2 buttons only ever differ by "did the user also fill in field X," collapsing to one button keyed off that field is usually both simpler AND removes error cases, not just visual clutter.

### TaskTransitionsPanel (honest empty state in FMP-UI-20E; DRAFT button wording in FMP-UI-20G)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/_components/task-transitions.tsx`
- Purpose: All status transition buttons for the task detail sidebar; inline panels for assign, block reason, complete summary, reopen reason, cancel reason, priority change.
- Variants: Rendered buttons depend on `task.status` + permissions + assignee ownership (`isAssignee`, `isCreator`, `has('tasks.manage')`); one panel open at a time.
- Key tokens/classes: `space-y-2` button list; inline panels `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-4 space-y-3`. DRAFT-state buttons keep 3 visually distinct variants (secondary/neutral for Edit Draft, primary/accent-red for Open for Work, outline/danger-light for Cancel Task) — already correct before FMP-UI-20G, only the LABELS changed that unit.
- Accessibility behavior: Buttons disable during pending transitions.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useState` + `useTransition`. Props: `task`, `currentUserId`, `permissions`, `people`. Assignee-only transitions: start, block, unblock, complete. Creator-or-manage transitions: cancel, reopen. **FMP-UI-20E** — when no transition applies to the current viewer, this used to `return <></>` (a silently empty Actions panel — very likely the real cause behind "detail page shows nothing"/"only Cancel task" complaints for OTHER viewers on OTHER states). Now returns `"No actions available to you on this task right now."` instead — the panel's own "Available Actions" heading (set by the page, not this component) never has to explain an empty gap underneath it. **FMP-UI-20G** — DRAFT button wording: "Open task" → "Open for Work" (same `openTaskAction` call, plus a new one-line helper "Moves this task from Draft to Open." underneath the button); "Cancel task" → "Cancel Task" (all 5 status branches + the Cancel panel's own header) and "Edit draft" → "Edit Draft" — Title Case throughout, label only, no logic change.

### TaskActivityTimeline (phrasing fixed in FMP-UI-20E)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/_components/task-activity-timeline.tsx`
- Purpose: Merged, chronological list of `FactoryTaskActivity` events and `FactoryTaskComment` entries.
- Variants: Activity events show a bullet dot; comments show author initial in accent avatar.
- Key tokens/classes: `space-y-4`; comment bubble `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-3`.
- Accessibility behavior: Rendered as `<ol aria-label="Task activity timeline">`.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props: `activities: FactoryTaskActivity[]`, `comments: FactoryTaskComment[]`. Sorts merged list by `createdAt` ascending. EVENT_LABELS map covers 14 event types (raw event keys were already converted before FMP-UI-20E — never shown raw). **FMP-UI-20E** — activity line phrasing changed from `"{actor} {label}"` (e.g. "manager Task created") to `"{label} by {actor}"` (e.g. "Task created by manager") per direct feedback — word order only, the label text/mapping itself is unchanged.

### Task Detail Page — Task Summary / Assignment Details / Dates (FMP-UI-20E, navigation added in FMP-UI-20F, Next Action/Next Step + card polish in FMP-UI-20G, nav-to-content spacing fixed in FMP-UI-20H)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`.
- Purpose: same route, same data, same permissions as before — reorganizes the same fields into the brief's required 2-column shape, per direct feedback that the page "felt too empty" and hard to scan. Left column: **Task Summary** (reference number under a small "Task Detail" eyebrow label (FMP-UI-20F), title, then a labeled `Status: / Priority: / Assigned to: / Responsible department: / Due: / Next Action:` grid (FMP-UI-20G added the Next Action row and switched Due to date+time), then a highlighted **Next Step** callout (FMP-UI-20G, new)) → Description (FMP-UI-20G: now always in its own card, "No description provided." when empty) → Blocked reason → Completion summary → Progress notes + Add progress → Comments & activity (FMP-UI-20G: now in its own card). Right column: **Available Actions** (was "Actions" — `TaskTransitionsPanel`) → **Assignment Details** (Assigned To, Created By, Requested By, Responsible/Requesting Department, Plant / Location) → **Dates** (Created, Due, Blocked, Completed, Closed, Linked Incident) — all 3 unified to the same `rounded-xl border border-border bg-surface p-5 shadow-sm` card style as the left column (FMP-UI-20G; was `rounded-lg`/`p-4`, no shadow).
- Key reuse: the old single "Details" `<dl>` panel's exact same fields, just split by QUESTION ("who/where" vs. "when") into 2 panels instead of one undifferentiated list. "Next Action:" reuses `computeTaskNextActionText()` — the SAME function/text the Task Control Center's list (`TaskControlCenterList`) already shows in its own "Next Action" column; never a second, independently-worded copy.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20E) — "Assigned to"/"Not assigned" now appears in 2 places on this one page (Task Summary's quick-glance grid, and Assignment Details' fuller panel) — both read from the exact same `task.assignedToUser?.displayName ?? 'Not assigned'` expression, never 2 independently-worded copies that could drift (one said "Unassigned" before this unit, the other didn't — now both say the same thing, everywhere in the module).
- **FMP-UI-20F** — a wrapper above the 2-column grid holds a 3-level `Breadcrumbs` ("Platform Dashboard > Task Management > {reference}", was "Factory Tasks Management > {reference}" linking to the OLD `/factory-tasks` list) plus the new `TaskModuleNav` row underneath it (see its own entry below) — per direct feedback that this page had no way back except the browser's own back button.
- **FMP-UI-20G** — `computeTaskNextStepGuidance(status, hasAssignee)` (`_lib/task-control-center-helpers.ts`) drives the Next Step callout: one honest, plain-English sentence per real status (e.g. Draft: "This task is still in draft. Open it for work when it is ready to be tracked and assigned."), never a second action button — this box is descriptive text only, distinct from the real buttons `TaskTransitionsPanel` renders in Available Actions.
- **FMP-UI-20H** — the nav-row wrapper's gap to `TaskModuleNav` changed from `space-y-2` (relying on `Breadcrumbs`' own default `mb-4` margin-collapsing with the wrapper's top-margin) to `space-y-3` with `Breadcrumbs` explicitly passed `className="mb-0"` — a deterministic gap, not dependent on CSS margin-collapse between 2 separately-styled elements. The grid's own `mt-6` (24px) → `mt-8` (32px) — deliberately above this unit's own suggested 16–24px range — per direct feedback that the nav row read as visually attached to the Task Summary card below it.
- **FMP-UI-20I** — the nav component this page uses was renamed `TaskDetailNav` → `TaskModuleNav` (see its own entry below); this page's own JSX only changed its import and tag name, no behavior change here.

### TaskModuleNav (new as TaskDetailNav in FMP-UI-20F; button order swapped in FMP-UI-20G; renamed + reused by the Task List page in FMP-UI-20I)
- Path: `factory-tasks/_components/task-module-nav.tsx` (was `task-detail-nav.tsx` before FMP-UI-20I).
- Purpose: Back to Task Management / Back to Platform Dashboard / Previous: Maintenance Management / Switch module. Originally built for the Task Detail page only — **FMP-UI-20G** put "Back to Task Management" FIRST (was second, after "Back to Platform Dashboard") since a viewer leaving one task's detail page is far more likely headed back to the task list than all the way out to the platform dashboard. Deliberately does NOT reuse `ExecutiveModuleNav` wholesale — that component bakes in its own 2-level breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > {module}"), which would have duplicated/conflicted with the 3-level breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > Task Management > {reference}" or "... > Task List") each call site renders separately via the existing `Breadcrumbs` component. Instead reuses the underlying PURE DATA functions `ExecutiveModuleNav` itself calls — `getModuleNeighbors`/`getVisibleModules` (`_lib/executive-modules.ts`) — plus the identical `NAV_BUTTON_CLASS`/Switch-module `<details>` markup, so the visual language matches every other Executive Module Landing Page's own nav exactly.
- Key tokens/classes: same `NAV_BUTTON_CLASS` (`inline-flex h-9 items-center gap-2 rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface px-3.5 text-sm font-semibold ...`) and Switch-module chip styling `ExecutiveModuleNav` already established — copied, not imported, since the surrounding markup (breadcrumb placement) differs per call site.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/[id]/page.tsx`; `factory-tasks/page.tsx` (Task List page, added FMP-UI-20I).
- Notes: Pure RSC, no `'use client'`, no state. Props: `permissions: string[]` (already computed by each page from the existing JWT payload — no new fetch). "Back to Task Management" (→ `/factory-tasks/executive`) is THIS component's own addition, not something `ExecutiveModuleNav` has — that component never needs a "back to this module's own landing page" button because it only ever renders ON that landing page; one level below it, that link is exactly what was missing. `FACTORY_TASKS` is last in `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`, so `getModuleNeighbors` only ever returns a `prev` (Maintenance Management) — no dead "Next" conditional was added for a case that can never fire.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20F) — when a component you'd like to reuse (`ExecutiveModuleNav`) bakes in a side effect (its own breadcrumb) that a new call site doesn't want, don't reuse the component "mostly" and fight the extra output — extract and reuse the underlying PURE functions it's built on instead. The component stays untouched for its 10 existing call sites; the new call site gets exactly the behavior it needs with zero risk of regressing the others.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20I) — this component was already fully generic (keyed only on `permissions`, never a task ID) despite its original `TaskDetailNav` name implying detail-page-only scope. When a second call site needs the identical nav shape, check whether the "specific-sounding" name is actually hiding a general-purpose component before writing a near-duplicate — here the rename was the entire change, no logic moved.

### Task List Page (FMP-UI-20I — navigation and wording added)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/factory-tasks/page.tsx`.
- Purpose: same route, same data, same filters/table/pagination as before — adds the same `TaskModuleNav` row (Back to Task Management / Back to Platform Dashboard / Previous / Switch module) the Task Detail page already had (FMP-UI-20F–20H), plus a 3-level breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > Task Management > Task List", was 1 level: "Factory Tasks Management" with no `href`). Title/subtitle changed to "Task List" / "View, filter, and open operational tasks." (was "Factory Tasks Management" repeated as both the breadcrumb label and the page title — direct feedback flagged this as confusing/redundant). "Assigned to" empty table cell now reads "Not assigned" (was a bare "—") — matches the wording already used everywhere else in this module (Task Control Center list, Task Detail page, per FMP-UI-20E's own reusable takeaway on that exact point).
- Key reuse: `TaskModuleNav` reused verbatim (no new props, no task-specific data needed) — see its own entry above. Nav-row wrapper uses the same `space-y-3` / `Breadcrumbs className="mb-0"` / `mt-8`-before-content pattern established for the Task Detail page in FMP-UI-20H, applied here for the first time to a second page.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20I) — no filter, search, pagination, table-action, or route logic was touched; this was a pure navigation + wording unit, confirmed by an unchanged 963/963 web test count (this page has no dedicated test file — its logic is untested display-only JSX, same as before).

## Task Control Center (`/factory-tasks/executive`, FMP-UI-20)

### TaskControlCenterList (columns rebuilt in FMP-UI-20E)
- Path: `factory-tasks/_components/task-control-center-list.tsx`, backed by `factory-tasks/_lib/task-control-center-helpers.ts`.
- Purpose: replaces the old Executive Module Landing Page for Task Management (a generic Summary KPI grid + Needs Attention + Recent Activity + Quick Links + one "View Tasks" button — direct user feedback: it never clearly guided anyone to create/assign/track/close a task) with a genuine Task Control Center. Same route, same `tasks.read` access rule, same underlying data (`tasksApi.dashboard()`/`.my()`/`.assignedByMe()`/`.list()`) as before.
- Structure: Header (`ExecutiveModuleNav` + a bordered card wrapping `ExecutiveModuleTitle`, with a "+ New Task" button in its own `actions` slot — the FMP-UI-16 slot, unused elsewhere in the app since Contract Management moved to its own bespoke header, reused here as originally designed) → 4 `MetricCard`s (My Open Tasks / Assigned by Me / Overdue / Due Today) → 5 tabs (My Tasks / Assigned by Me / All Tasks / Overdue / Completed, `?tab=` query param) each rendering a real top-8 preview via `TaskControlCenterList` + a "View all" link to that tab's own existing dedicated page.
- Key reuse: `TaskControlCenterList` reuses the exact table visual language, `TaskStatusBadge`, and `TaskPriorityBadge` already established by `factory-tasks/page.tsx`/`factory-tasks/my/page.tsx`. **FMP-UI-20E** — columns are now Task / Assignment / Department / Priority / Due Date / Status / Next Action / Actions (dropped "Created By" — not in this unit's own recommended column list, and the detail page's Assignment Details panel already covers it). "Unassigned" → "Not assigned". "Next Action" (`computeTaskNextActionText()`, renamed from `computeTaskActionHint()`) is now its own plain-text column, separate from "Actions," which shows "Open Task" (secondary, always) plus, when `computeTaskQuickAction()` returns one, ONE clearly-visible PRIMARY button for the specific next action — Assign/Complete still link to the task's real detail page (assigning needs a person picker, completing needs a summary — neither is a genuine single click); Close needs no extra input, so it's a REAL one-click `<form>` via a small inline Server Action (`closeTaskQuickAction`, a 3-line wrapper needed only because `closeTaskAction`'s `Promise<ActionResult>` return isn't assignable to a form action's `void | Promise<void>`) posting straight to the existing `closeTaskAction`.
- Used by: `factory-tasks/executive/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: `getVisibleTaskControlCenterTabs()` hides "All Tasks" from any viewer without `tasks.manage` (the existing manager-tier permission `task-transitions.tsx` already uses for cancel-any-task/reopen — reused, not a new permission); a deep-linked `?tab=all` without that permission falls back to `my`. `computeTaskNextActionText()`/`computeTaskQuickAction()` both mirror `task-transitions.tsx`'s own button-priority order exactly, so the list's text/buttons and the detail page's real buttons never disagree about what's "next."
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20E) — "Next Action" (an objective description of what the record needs) and "Actions" (the buttons a SPECIFIC viewer can actually click) are 2 separate concerns, same principle already established for Erection's `erectionNextActionLabel()` vs. its Action buttons (FMP-UI-19E) — conflating them into one cell (a button with hint text stacked underneath, as this list did before this unit) is what let "Assign task" read as an afterthought rather than a clear call to action.

### Backend: `createdByUserId` filter / `findAssignedByMe` / `assignedByMe` metric (FMP-UI-20)
- Path: `apps/api/src/factory-tasks/dto/task-list-query.dto.ts`, `factory-tasks.service.ts`, `factory-tasks.controller.ts`.
- Purpose: the ONE backend change this unit made — needed to make the "Assigned by Me" tab honest rather than a client-side filter over an already-paginated (and therefore possibly incomplete) list. Mirrors the existing `assignedToUserId`/`findMy()`/`assignedToMe` trio exactly, keyed on `createdByUserId` instead — same "me" resolution, same "not department-scoped, it's yours to see regardless" reasoning `findMy()` already established.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-20) — "UI-first" doesn't mean "never touch the backend": when a required tab/filter needs data the existing endpoints structurally cannot provide accurately (here: "everything I created," across however many pages that might span), client-side filtering over one fetched page is not a UI-only alternative — it's a different, less correct feature that happens to look similar. The right question isn't "can I avoid the backend" but "does an exact precedent already exist in this same file for the mirror-image case" — `assignedToUserId`/`findMy()` already existed for "assigned to me," so extending the identical shape for "created by me" was a same-day, low-risk addition, not schema drift.

## API Patterns (Unit 09)

### FactoryTasksService — Concurrency-safe Status Transitions
- Pattern: `updateMany(where: { id, status: currentStatus })` + count check; throws `TASK_CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION` if count === 0.
- Used by: All status transition methods in `FactoryTasksService`.
- Notes: Same TOCTOU-prevention pattern as `IncidentsService`. Never use findUnique + update for status transitions.

## API Patterns (Unit 10)

### MaintenanceService — WAITING_FOR_PARTS State Machine
- Pattern: IN_PROGRESS ↔ WAITING_FOR_PARTS via `waitingForParts(reason)` / `resume()`; both guarded by `maintenance.start` permission; assignee ownership enforced in service (not controller).
- Used by: `MaintenanceService.waitingForParts()`, `MaintenanceService.resume()`.
- Notes: Unique to maintenance module — not present in incidents or factory-tasks. `waitingForPartsReason` required and non-blank; cleared on resume.

### MaintenanceService — Reopen Logic
- Pattern: `reopen()` destination depends on source: REJECTED→SUBMITTED (re-enters review workflow, clears rejection fields); COMPLETED/CLOSED→IN_PROGRESS (continues execution, clears lifecycle timestamps). CANCELLED is never reopenable.
- Used by: `MaintenanceService.reopen()`.
- Notes: Differs from factory-tasks reopen (which always goes to IN_PROGRESS). Reason required and non-blank for all reopen calls.

### MaintenanceService — Cancel Ownership Rules
- Pattern: `cancel` endpoint uses `maintenance.create` at controller level (any authenticated user who can create can attempt cancel); service enforces: creator may cancel own DRAFT/SUBMITTED; `maintenance.manage` required for all other states or for cancelling others' requests.
- Used by: `MaintenanceService.cancel()`, `MaintenanceController.cancel()`.
- Notes: Prevents non-owners from cancelling others' early-stage requests while allowing `manage` override at any non-terminal state.

- Variants: OPEN (info-light/text-info), ACTION_REQUIRED (warning-light/text-warning), RESOLVED (accent-light/text-accent), VERIFIED (success-light/text-success), CLOSED (surface-secondary/text-secondary).
- Key tokens/classes: `inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2.5 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium`
- Used by: `safety-compliance/[id]/page.tsx`; `safety-compliance/_components/safety-needs-attention-list.tsx` (FMP-UI-21).
- Notes: Props: `status: FindingStatus`.

### SafetyNeedsAttentionList (new, FMP-UI-21)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/safety-compliance/_components/safety-needs-attention-list.tsx`
- Purpose: Itemized "Needs Attention" list for the Safety Control Center — replaces the old bare-count-only panel. Each row: parent inspection's reference number + `FindingSeverityBadge` + `FindingStatusBadge`, finding title, due date if set, and an explicit "Open Record" button (not just a clickable row, per this unit's own accessibility/older-users requirement).
- Key tokens/classes: `rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-3`; "Open Record" button reuses the same `NAV_BUTTON_CLASS`-style outline button as the module nav components.
- Used by: `safety-compliance/executive/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: Pure RSC. Props: `items: SafetyNeedsAttentionItem[]` (from `safetyApi.dashboard()`'s new `needsAttention` field). Empty message: "No critical or overdue safety items right now." (exact wording from the brief). "Open Record" always links to `/safety-compliance/{inspectionId}` — findings have no reference number or detail page of their own; the parent inspection's detail page is the only real place to open/action one.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21) — a dashboard's "Needs Attention" section is only honest if it shows real per-record data (reference, title, status, due date) with a real action, not just an aggregate count with a generic link. When a module's dashboard only had counts computed, the fix was a small additive query in the service layer (`getDashboard()`), never front-end fabrication.

### SafetyModuleNav (new, FMP-UI-21)

- Used by: `safety-compliance/page.tsx` only.

Props: `permissions: string[]`. **Difference from `TaskModuleNav`**: renders BOTH `prev` and `next` (like `ExecutiveModuleNav` does), because `SAFETY_COMPLIANCE` sits in the middle of `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`, not last — `TaskModuleNav` only ever needed `prev` since `FACTORY_TASKS` is the last entry. Any future per-module "list page nav" component should check its own module's position in `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` before copying either one verbatim.

### Safety Control Center (`safety-compliance/executive/page.tsx`, redesigned FMP-UI-21)
- Purpose: same route (`/safety-compliance/executive`), same access rule (`safety.read`), same `safetyApi.dashboard()` call as before — replaces the old generic Summary/Needs-Attention-counts/Recent-Activity/Quick-Links landing page with an operational hub. `ExecutiveModuleTitle`'s `actions` slot holds "+ New Safety Inspection" (gated `safety.create`, → `/safety-compliance/new`) and "View Safety Records" (→ `/safety-compliance`) — no "Record Finding" button (see FMP-UI-21's own progress-tracker "Create action audit result": no standalone route exists). 6 `MetricCard`s (Scheduled Inspections / In Progress / Open Findings / Critical Findings / Overdue Findings / Completed Inspections) from `data.metrics` — no new metrics needed, all 6 already existed. `SafetyNeedsAttentionList` (see above) replaces the old counts-only panel. Recent Activity removed entirely. Quick Links replaced with Quick Filters (Scheduled Inspections / In Progress / Completed / Open Findings) styled as filter chips.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21) — this is the SAME redesign shape as Task Management's Task Control Center (FMP-UI-20): keep `ExecutiveModuleNav` at the top (it IS the landing page, so its own 2-level breadcrumb is correct here — unlike a one-level-below page, which needs the dedicated `*ModuleNav` pattern instead), put real create actions in `ExecutiveModuleTitle`'s `actions` slot, and replace "counts + generic recent + link list" with real itemized data wherever the ticket asks for it.

### Safety Records list page (`safety-compliance/page.tsx`, navigation/wording added FMP-UI-21)
- Purpose: same route, same data, same filters/search/table/pagination as before. Added `SafetyModuleNav` row + 3-level breadcrumb ("Platform Dashboard > Safety & Compliance > Safety Records", was 1 level with no `href`). Title "Safety & Compliance" → "Safety Records"; subtitle → "View, search, and open safety inspections and findings." Create button relabeled "+ New Safety Inspection" (was "New Inspection"/"Create first inspection" — same route). Empty state: now differentiates "no create permission" ("No safety inspections in your current scope.") from "create permission exists, truly empty" ("No safety inspections yet." + "Create the first safety inspection to start tracking compliance." + a real create button) — the filtered-empty case is unchanged.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21) — an empty state is only "helpful" (per this unit's own brief) if its message and its action both depend on what the CURRENT viewer can actually do next — a viewer with no create permission gets a scope explanation, not a button they can't use; a viewer who can create gets the button, immediately, in the same place the message told them what to do.

### SafetyInspectionForm (new, FMP-UI-21B; dropdown fetch states added FMP-UI-21C)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/safety-compliance/_components/safety-inspection-form.tsx`
- Purpose: Create/edit form for a safety inspection (title/summary/departmentId/plantId). Extracted from `new/page.tsx`'s own inline, BROKEN copy — that version rendered a plain `<form action={action as unknown as string}>` with no `useActionState`, which is the confirmed root cause of the "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'get')" crash on submit FMP-UI-21B fixed. See this component's own doc comment for the full mechanism.
- Key tokens/classes: same field/button styling as the old inline version; `role="alert"` error banner (`rounded-lg border border-danger bg-danger-light`) and a disabled/"Creating…" submit button while `pending` (FMP-UI-21B, neither existed before). Department/Plant fields (FMP-UI-21C): 3 states each — `role="alert"` danger text when the fetch itself failed, muted text when the fetch succeeded but returned zero rows, or the real `<select>`.
- Used by: `safety-compliance/new/page.tsx` only (as of FMP-UI-21C).
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useActionState(action, { error: null })`; `<form action={dispatch}>` — `dispatch` is what correctly supplies BOTH `(prevState, formData)` to the action on every submit; a plain `<form action={someTwoArgFn}>` never does. Props: `action`, `submitLabel`, `cancelHref`, `departments`, `deptsFailed?`, `plants`, `plantsFailed?`, `defaultValues?`. Department/Plant are BOTH optional for a safety inspection (`CreateInspectionDto`'s own `@IsOptional()`), so — unlike `task-form.tsx`'s REQUIRED Responsible Department — nothing here ever blocks submission; "— None —" is a valid real choice, not a fallback for missing data.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21B) — `action={fn as unknown as string}` on a plain `<form>` is a red flag by itself: if a form action needs a type cast to compile, that's TypeScript correctly detecting a signature mismatch between a `(prevState, formData)`-shaped action and a calling convention that only ever supplies one argument. The fix is always `useActionState` + `<form action={dispatch}>`, never a wider cast.
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21C) — "the dropdown is empty" and "the dropdown's data source is broken" are 2 different states that read identically to a user unless the code distinguishes them explicitly (`Failed !== zero rows`). Confirmed via a direct read-only query against the dev database (1 active department, 0 plants total) that Department really was a loading bug (real data existed, wasn't shown) while Plant really is just empty (no rows exist yet) — the fix always starts with checking the DATA, not just the code.
- **Known related issue, NOT fixed in FMP-UI-21B/21C** (out of scope — a different page): `safety-compliance/[id]/edit/page.tsx` has the exact same `action={boundAction as unknown as string}` crash pattern AND the same admin-gated `organizations-api.ts` dropdown bug, on its own still-inline form. Candidate for an immediate follow-up unit that adopts this same `SafetyInspectionForm` component for the Edit page too (once its `action` prop type is widened to accept a bound 2-arg action) and switches it to `safetyApi.departments()`/`.plants()`.

### SafetyService — Module-Scoped Departments/Plants Reference Endpoints (FMP-UI-21C)
- Pattern: `GET /safety-compliance/departments` / `GET /safety-compliance/plants`, gated `safety.read` (not the admin-only `org.departments.read`/`org.plants.read`) — same precedent as `factory-tasks.controller.ts`'s own `/departments`/`/plants` (FMP-UI-20D), `contracts.service.ts`'s `listDepartments()`/`listPlants()`, and `production`'s `/production/departments`.
- Used by: `SafetyService.listDepartments()`, `SafetyService.listPlants()`; consumed by `apps/web/src/lib/safety-api.ts`'s `safetyApi.departments()`/`.plants()`; used by `safety-compliance/new/page.tsx`.
- Notes: `{ id, name, code }[]`, `where: { isActive: true }`, ordered by name. No schema change — same `Department`/`Plant` tables every other module-scoped reference endpoint already reads.

### SafetyInspectionTransitions (new, FMP-UI-21D)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/safety-compliance/_components/safety-inspection-transitions.tsx`
- Purpose: the Inspection Detail page's real "Available Actions" panel — replaces a section that (a) wired Start/Close to a raw `<form action="/safety-compliance/{id}/start" method="POST">`, which posts to the WEB APP's own origin, not the API, so it never actually worked, and (b) showed "…use the API or this page will be extended with forms." for Schedule/Complete/Reopen/Cancel, explicitly forbidden by this unit's own brief. All 6 transitions plus Edit (moved in from a floating header button) are wired to the real, already-implemented `actions.ts` functions.
- Key tokens/classes: same `Btn` component shape/variants (primary/secondary/danger) as `task-transitions.tsx`'s own `Btn`; same inline-panel styling (`rounded-lg border border-border bg-surface p-4 space-y-3`).
- Used by: `safety-compliance/[id]/page.tsx` only.
- Notes: `'use client'`; `useTransition` + manually-constructed `FormData` per panel (Schedule needs `scheduledAt`+`inspectorUserId`; Complete needs `conclusion`; Reopen/Cancel need `reason`) — NOT `useActionState`, matching the established multi-panel convention (`task-transitions.tsx`, `incident-transitions.tsx`), neither of which uses `useActionState` either. Props: `inspectionId`, `canEdit`, `canDoSchedule`, `canDoStart`, `canDoComplete`, `canDoClose`, `canDoReopen`, `canDoCancel`, `people: UserRef[]` (all computed by the parent page from the same permission/status/ownership logic that was already there). Empty-buttons fallback: "More actions will appear when available for this inspection status." — the brief's own required wording, never "use the API".
- Reusable takeaway (FMP-UI-21D) — a Server Action called directly from a client component (via `useTransition`, not a `<form>` submit) gives the client NO signal that data changed on success. `actions.ts`'s 6 transition actions this component calls were all extended to `redirect()` back to the SAME detail page on success (previously they only `revalidatePath` and returned `{error: null}`), forcing a fresh server render — the identical mechanism `factory-tasks/actions.ts`'s own transition actions already use for the exact same reason.

## API Patterns (Unit 11)

### SafetyService — Inspector Ownership Rule
- Pattern: `start()` and `complete()` require `inspectorUserId === actor.id` OR actor has `safety.manage`. Both check via private `requireInspectorOwnership()` helper before the `updateMany` transition.
- Used by: `SafetyService.start()`, `SafetyService.complete()`.
- Notes: Inspector set at schedule time; null inspector means no-one can start without `safety.manage`.

### SafetyService — Verifier Separation of Duties
- Pattern: `verifyFinding()` rejects if `finding.resolvedByUserId === actor.id` unless actor has `safety.manage`. Prevents resolver from self-verifying.
- Used by: `SafetyService.verifyFinding()`.
- Notes: Throws `SAFETY_VERIFIER_SAME_AS_RESOLVER` (ForbiddenException) when violated.

### SafetyService — Finding Reopen Target
- Pattern: Reopening a RESOLVED/VERIFIED/CLOSED finding goes to `ACTION_REQUIRED` (not OPEN). Clears resolvedAt/verifiedAt/closedAt and their userId fields. Preserves `resolutionSummary`. Sets `reopenedAt`, `reopenedByUserId`, `reopenReason`.
- Used by: `SafetyService.reopenFinding()`.
- Notes: Same concurrency guard as other transitions.

### SafetyService — Inspection Reopen
- Pattern: CLOSED → IN_PROGRESS. Clears `completedAt`, `completedByUserId`, `closedAt`, `closedByUserId`. Preserves `conclusion`. Mandatory `reason` (non-blank). Requires `safety.manage`.
- Used by: `SafetyService.reopen()`.

## Contract Components (Unit 12)

### ContractLifecycleBadge
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_components/contract-lifecycle-badge.tsx`
- Purpose: Pill badge for the 6 derived contract lifecycle display states.
- Variants: DRAFT (surface-secondary/muted), ACTIVE (success-light/text-success), EXPIRING (warning-light/text-warning), EXPIRED (danger-light/text-danger), TERMINATED (surface-secondary/text-secondary), CLOSED (surface-secondary/text-secondary).
- Key tokens/classes: `inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2.5 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium`
- Accessibility behavior: Pure RSC; no additional aria attributes.
- Used by: `contracts/page.tsx`, `contracts/[id]/page.tsx`
- Notes: Props: `status: DerivedLifecycleStatus`. Note: displays derived lifecycle status (6 states), not stored `ContractStatus` (4 states). EXPIRING = ACTIVE with `renewalNoticeDate ≤ today AND endDate not passed`. EXPIRED = ACTIVE with `endDate < today`. Color mapping is explicit (no dynamic Tailwind key construction).

## API Patterns (Unit 12)

### ContractsService — Optimistic Concurrency via Version Field
- Pattern: `updateMany(where: { id, status: currentStatus, version: dto.version })` for ALL mutations (update/activate/terminate/close); `version` field incremented on every mutation. After count=0: `findUnique` inside transaction distinguishes not-found (→ 404) from conflict (→ 409 `CONTRACT_VERSION_CONFLICT`).
- Used by: `ContractsService.update()`, `activate()`, `terminate()`, `close()`.
- Notes: `UpdateContractDto` requires `version: number` (Int, min 1). The WHERE clause always uses the client-submitted `dto.version`, never a DB-refetched version. A preliminary read may be used for existence, status, and permission checks, but the concurrency gate is always `dto.version`. Frontend receives "Contract was changed by another user; please refresh and retry" on 409.

### ContractsService — Derived Lifecycle Status
- Pattern: `getDerivedLifecycleStatus(contract, today)` is a named exported function (not a method). Computes: EXPIRING = stored ACTIVE + `renewalNoticeDate ≤ today AND (endDate IS NULL OR endDate ≥ today)`; EXPIRED = stored ACTIVE + `endDate < today`. All other stored statuses pass through. `utcToday()` = `new Date(Date.UTC(now.getUTCFullYear(), now.getUTCMonth(), now.getUTCDate()))`.
- Used by: `ContractsService.withLifecycle()` (all response builders), and importable in tests.
- Notes: EXPIRING and EXPIRED are derived at read time — no background job, no stored field. `buildListWhere()` also exported for testability; handles `lifecycleStatus=EXPIRING/EXPIRED` by translating to date-range WHERE clauses.

### Inline Server Action Wrappers — Next.js 15 Pattern
- Pattern: Server component declares `async function handleAction(formData?: FormData): Promise<void> { 'use server'; ... }` inline, closing over component-scope variables (`id`, `version`). This avoids `.bind()` whose return types are incompatible with React form `action` prop, and correctly passes `formData` as the last argument to `useActionState`-style actions.
- Used by: `contracts/[id]/page.tsx` (`handleActivate`, `handleClose`, `handleTerminate`, `handleComment`).
- Notes: Actions with `useActionState` signature (`(prev, formData) => Promise<State>`) require `{ error: null }` passed as `_prev` when called from inline wrappers: `await terminateContractAction(id, contract.version, { error: null }, formData)`.

## Contract Register Shared Form Fields (CM-24)

### contract-form-fields.tsx (shared field-group components)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_components/contract-form-fields.tsx`
- Purpose: Field-group components (`ClientContactFields`, `ContractDatesFields`, `ContractValueFields`, `ProjectSiteFields`, `ScopeOfWorkFieldset`, `CraneFields`) plus shared style tokens (`inputCls`, `labelCls`, `gridCls3`, `InfoBox`), consumed identically by both New Contract Register and Edit Contract so the two screens never diverge in field set or labeling.
- Key tokens/classes: `inputCls`/`labelCls`/`gridCls3` reused from prior contract form styling; textareas use `${inputCls} resize-y`.
- Accessibility behavior: All inputs have associated `<label htmlFor>`; required fields (`Other Description`) marked with `<span className="text-danger">*</span>` and `required`.
- Used by: `contracts/new/_components/new-contract-form.tsx`, `contracts/[id]/edit/_components/edit-contract-form.tsx`.
- Notes: `ScopeOfWorkFieldset` is fully controlled by the parent (`scope`, `onScopeChange`, `exFactory`, `onExFactoryChange`, `otherDescription`, `onOtherDescriptionChange`) so the parent can also derive `erectionSelected` for conditional Erection/Crane section visibility and read the same state at submit time. Mutual-exclusivity rules enforced client-side (mirrored server-side in `ContractsService`): checking "Not Applicable" clears every other option; checking "Ex-Factory" disables/clears Delivery+Erection; checking "Other" reveals a required `scope_otherDescription` text input (plain field, not a `scope_<key>` checkbox) whose value is merged into the `scopeOfWork.otherDescription` JSON key by the server action, not stored as its own DB column. When building a `{ ...scope, key: value }` object from a `Record<string, boolean>` state, annotate the result type explicitly (`const next: Record<string, boolean> = ...`) — TS narrows the inferred type to just the literal key otherwise and later `next['x'] = ...` index writes fail typecheck.

### ContractScopeDetailsCard / ContractCraneDetailsCard
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_components/contract-scope-details-card.tsx`, `contract-crane-details-card.tsx`
- Purpose: Read-only Contract Detail Overview cards for the CM-24 long-text scope/schedule fields and the Erection/Crane fields, respectively.
- Key tokens/classes: Same `Field`/`dt`/`dd` pattern as `contract-info-card.tsx` / `contract-register-details-card.tsx`; long-text values use `whitespace-pre-wrap`.
- Used by: `contracts/[id]/(workspace)/page.tsx`.
- Notes: `ContractCraneDetailsCard` is rendered conditionally — only when `contract.scopeOfWork?.['erection'] === true` — matching the same "only when relevant" rule used for the create/edit form's crane section. Missing values render `—`, never blank/undefined, so old (pre-CM-24) contracts without these columns display cleanly.

### optionLabel() helper
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_lib/contract-ui-helpers.ts`
- Purpose: `optionLabel(options: OptionDef[], key: string | undefined): string` — looks up a coded value's display label from an `OptionDef[]` list (e.g. `CRANE_REQUIRED_OPTIONS`), returning `—` for empty and the raw key as a fallback if unmapped.
- Used by: `ContractCraneDetailsCard`.

## Contract Management Sidebar Navigation (CM-26)

### ContractPlaceholderPage
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_components/contract-placeholder-page.tsx`
- Purpose: Shared shell for module-level Contract Management pages with no backend yet (Schedule, Payments, Issue Log, Claim Log) — breadcrumb + title/subtitle + a single bordered card with a "Not started" pill and one line of plain-language body copy. Never renders fake/sample data.
- Props: `breadcrumbLabel`, `title`, `subtitle`, `body` (all plain strings).
- Key tokens/classes: Same `px-6 lg:px-8 py-6 max-w-[1920px] mx-auto space-y-6` page container as `contracts/page.tsx` / `contracts/dashboard/page.tsx`, so it feels like a real (if empty) module page rather than an error state.
- Used by: `contracts/schedule/page.tsx`, `contracts/issues/page.tsx`, `contracts/claims/page.tsx`. (`contracts/payments/page.tsx` moved off this shell in CM-28 — it's now a real register, see below.)
- Notes: each caller page is itself gated by `getUserPermissions().includes('contracts.read')` → `notFound()` otherwise, since these pages make no API call of their own and would otherwise have no permission gate at all (existing contract pages are indirectly gated by their API calls failing for unauthorized users).

### Sidebar — Contract-Management-only flattening
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/sidebar.tsx`
- Pattern: `isContractManagementOnlyAccess(user.permissions)` (from `module-visibility.ts`) drives two changes at once — the top-level "Dashboard" link (`href: '/'`, `MAIN_GROUPS[0]`) is filtered out, and the "Contract Management" group renders as a flat top-level section (own `<p>` label, plain `<Link>`s, no toggle button/chevron) instead of nested under "Operations" behind a dropdown. Any user with contracts access *plus* at least one other module (including Admin/Super Admin) keeps the original nested-dropdown-under-Operations rendering — same `CONTRACT_ITEMS` array and `visibleContractItems` filter feed both render paths, so the two never drift out of sync on which items are shown.
- Notes: `isContractItemActive(href, pathname)` must special-case each fixed module-level slug (`dashboard`, `schedule`, `payments`, `issues`, `claims`) individually — a route added to `CONTRACT_ITEMS` without a matching branch will silently fall through to Contract List's catch-all and highlight the wrong link. `CONTRACT_TOP_LEVEL_SLUGS` is the single list both the catch-all check and future additions need to stay in sync with.

## Contract Payments Register (CM-28)

### PaymentFormModal
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/payments/_components/payment-form-modal.tsx`
- Purpose: Single modal for both Add and Edit payment, sharing `contract-form-fields.tsx`'s `inputCls`/`labelCls`/`gridCls3` tokens for visual consistency with the Contract Register forms.
- Pattern: Footer submit button lives outside the `<form>` element (so it can sit in a sticky footer bar) and is wired via the standard HTML `form="payment-form"` attribute rather than DOM traversal — the robust way to submit a form from a sibling element. Closes itself and calls `router.refresh()` on successful submit via a `submittedRef` flag checked in a `useEffect` watching `useActionState`'s state/pending — `useActionState`'s initial state shape (`{error: null}`) is indistinguishable from a "just succeeded" state, so a ref tracking "a submit actually happened" is required to avoid closing on first render.
- Used by: `payment-register-table.tsx` (both `mode="add"` and `mode="edit"`).

### PaymentRegisterTable / PaymentStatusBadge / PaymentSummaryCards / PaymentFilterBar
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/payments/_components/`
- Purpose: The module-level Payments Register's table (client component — owns Add/Edit modal state and the Cancel action), status badge (7-state `ContractPaymentStatus` → color+label maps), 5-card `MetricCard` summary row, and GET-form filter bar (URL-search-params driven, same pattern as `contract-filter-bar.tsx`).
- Notes: Table and filter bar both mark their interactive chrome (buttons, Action column, filter form) `print:hidden`; the page adds a `hidden print:block` header with a generated timestamp so the printed/PDF output has context the on-screen title doesn't need. Reused as-is for the per-contract read-only tab (`contracts/[id]/(workspace)/payments/page.tsx`) via `PaymentStatusBadge` — the table itself is not reused there since that view is deliberately simpler (no Add/Edit/Cancel).

### contract-payment-csv.ts
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_lib/contract-payment-csv.ts`
- Purpose: `csvField()` (RFC-4180-style escaping — quotes wrap any value containing a comma/quote/newline, embedded quotes doubled), `buildPaymentsCsv()`. Extracted from the `payments/export/route.ts` Route Handler specifically so the escaping logic has unit test coverage — Route Handlers aren't otherwise tested in this codebase.
- Used by: `contracts/payments/export/route.ts` (first Route Handler in the web app — Next.js Route Handlers run outside page layouts, so this route re-checks `contracts.read` itself via `getUserPermissions()` rather than relying on any page-level guard).

## Contract Workflow & Team Tasks Register (CM-29)

### WorkflowBoard / WorkflowUpdateTaskModal
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/workflow/_components/workflow-board.tsx`, `workflow-update-task-modal.tsx`
- Purpose: `WorkflowBoard` renders the 4 team lanes (Technical/Production/Erection/QS-Commercial), each only rendered when it has at least one task, with a compact task card (name, status badge, responsible, due date, Edit button). Shows "No workflow tasks configured." when a contract has zero tasks. `WorkflowUpdateTaskModal` follows the exact same footer-submit-button-via-`form=` attribute and `submittedRef`-gated close-on-success pattern as `PaymentFormModal` (CM-28) — see that entry for why the ref is needed.
- Used by: both the module-level `contracts/workflow/page.tsx` (via `?contractId=`) and the per-contract `contracts/[id]/(workspace)/workflow/page.tsx` tab — the **same** `WorkflowBoard` component instance is reused in both places (not just the same data shape), so board behavior/styling can never drift between the two surfaces.

### WorkflowStatusBadge vs WorkflowTaskStatusBadge
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/workflow/_components/`
- Purpose: Two distinct badges that are easy to confuse — `WorkflowStatusBadge` renders the **derived, contract-level** summary (`NOT_GENERATED`/`NOT_STARTED`/`IN_PROGRESS`/`COMPLETED`, computed server-side from all of a contract's tasks, never stored), while `WorkflowTaskStatusBadge` renders the **stored, per-task** enum (8 states: `NOT_STARTED` through `COMPLETED`/`ON_HOLD`). Do not merge these — they have different value sets and different meanings.

### formatScopeSummary()
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_lib/contract-ui-helpers.ts`
- Purpose: `formatScopeSummary(scope)` — compact "Shop Drawing, Production, Erection" comma-joined label list from a `scopeOfWork` object, filtering to `=== true` boolean keys only (ignores the `otherDescription` string value that lives in the same object). Returns `—` for empty/null.
- Used by: `workflow-contract-table.tsx` (Scope column), `workflow-contract-header.tsx` (Selected Contract summary) — reusable anywhere else a compact scope summary is needed instead of the full `ContractBadgeGroupCard` badge list.

### Task generation is backend-owned, not a frontend concern
- Path: `apps/api/src/contracts/contract-workflow-templates.ts`
- Note for future frontend work: the frontend never decides which default tasks to show — `GET /contracts/:id/workflow` lazily generates and persists them server-side on first view (idempotent via a DB unique constraint), and the frontend only ever renders whatever tasks the API returns. Do not port the template list or scope rules into a frontend `_lib` file — that would create a second source of truth that can drift from the backend's.

## Contract Issue Log Register (CM-30)

### IssueRegisterTable
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/issues/_components/issue-register-table.tsx`
- Purpose: `'use client'` table reused for both the module-level register and the per-contract tab. Owns Add/Edit modal state and the Close-issue action (`closeIssueAction` + `router.refresh()`).
- Props: `fixedContractId?: string` — when set (per-contract tab), the Contract selector is skipped in the Add modal and the Contract ID/Name/Company columns are hidden (`compact = Boolean(fixedContractId)`). Same "one component, two contexts" pattern as `WorkflowBoard` (CM-29) and the payments table (CM-28) — never fork a second table for the per-contract view.

### IssueFormModal / IssueStatusBadge / IssuePriorityBadge / IssueFilterBar / IssueSummaryCards
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/issues/_components/`
- Purpose: `IssueFormModal` — same footer-submit-via-`form="issue-form"` + `submittedRef`-gated close-on-success pattern as `PaymentFormModal`/`WorkflowUpdateTaskModal`; conditionally shows Resolution + Closed Date fields only when status is `RESOLVED`/`CLOSED`. `IssueStatusBadge` (6 states) / `IssuePriorityBadge` (4 states) — plain color+label maps. `IssueFilterBar` — GET-form filter bar, same URL-search-params pattern as the payments/workflow filter bars. `IssueSummaryCards` — 6 `MetricCard`s computed server-side over the full filtered result set (not just the current page).

### contract-issue-csv.ts
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_lib/contract-issue-csv.ts`
- Purpose: `buildIssuesCsv()` — imports `csvField()` directly from `contract-payment-csv.ts` (CM-28) rather than duplicating the RFC-4180 escaping logic. Reuse the shared `csvField` for any future CSV export in this module instead of re-implementing escaping.
- Used by: `contracts/issues/export/route.ts` (filtered CSV download, re-checks `contracts.read` itself since Route Handlers run outside page-level guards).

### Gotcha: runtime constants in `contracts-api.ts` break client-component builds
- `apps/web/src/lib/contracts-api.ts` imports `next/headers` at module scope (server-only). Any **runtime value** — not a type — exported from that file and imported into a `'use client'` component pulls the entire module, including `next/headers`, into the client bundle and fails the Turbopack build with a misleading Pages-Router-flavored error even though this project only uses the App Router.
- Rule: client-facing constants/enums that would naturally belong in `contracts-api.ts` (it's otherwise the right home for API-shaped types/constants) must instead live in the dependency-free `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_lib/contract-ui-helpers.ts`. `CONTRACT_ISSUE_CATEGORIES` was moved there for exactly this reason. `import type {...}` is safe (stripped at compile time) — only runtime `import {...}` triggers the failure.
