### A collapsible section defaults to open/closed based on viewer tier, not a fixed default, and the toggle control itself only renders for the tier that needs it (CM-71H.6)
- `ErectionStepGuidance` initializes its `open` state as `!isStaffTier` — manager-tier starts open with no toggle shown at all (matching the original always-visible section exactly); staff-tier starts collapsed with a small "Show guidance" button. Rather than one fixed default plus a toggle everyone sees, the initial state itself is tier-aware, so a manager-tier viewer's very first render is pixel-identical to the pre-CM-71H.6 markup — no button appears where none existed before, avoiding any risk of a manager mistaking a new UI element for a functional change.

## Erection Manager UX Cleanup: Remove Locks, Remove Guidance, Fix Read-only Fields (CM-71H.7)

### Before building a new "friendly inline notice" component, check whether the panel already has one doing the exact same check a level up (page.tsx) wants to duplicate (CM-71H.7)
- CM-71H.3's page-level `ErectionStepLockedNotice` and each later panel's own internal `if (!prereqRecord || prereqRecord.status !== 'X') return <InfoBox>...` block turned out to test the IDENTICAL condition (Step 4/5/6) or a deliberately weaker one (Step 2/3, where the panel's own floor is looser than the page's stricter UI-visibility rule) — the page-level check was a second, harsher gate sitting in front of a friendlier one that already existed and that every manager-tier viewer had been seeing unmodified the whole time. Before adding new "show a friendly message instead of blocking" UI anywhere, grep the target component for an existing early-return that already handles the same missing-prerequisite case — a redundant harsher duplicate is a deletion, not a design problem.

### A UI-only "harsh lock, this button is disabled" affordance and a real backend authorization/business-rule check are two different things — removing the first must never touch the second (CM-71H.7)
- Every fix in this unit — deleting the page-level lock block, greying-out/opacity styling, and "Locked" button copy — was strictly about VISUAL/UX severity: not one backend guard (`assertCanWriteErectionDepartmentStep`, the Step 5 hard `UnprocessableEntityException` on missing Delivery Start, the Step 3-4-6 prerequisite `ConflictException`/`UnprocessableEntityException` checks) was touched, weakened, or bypassed. Separately, widening `canUpdate` to include `contracts.workflow_update` for Steps 1/3/5 was safe to do ONLY because it made the frontend match a permission the backend ALREADY accepted (`AnyPermission('contracts.update', 'contracts.workflow_update')`, from CM-71H.1) — never the other way around (never widen a frontend gate to something the backend doesn't already independently enforce). When a task says "remove the harsh lock but keep it safe," the backend's own authorization/validation layer is the one thing that must be read and left completely alone while every visual severity cue around it changes freely.

### The same "guidedStepLocked" derived UI state is computed once but rendered by THREE separate task-list surfaces — a styling fix has to be applied to all three, found by grepping the shared field name, not by fixing the one screen the request happened to name (CM-71H.7)
- CM-71H.4 already established that `StaffTaskRow`/`StaffFlatTask` (the two sibling generic-task shapes) get rendered by more than one screen; this unit reconfirmed that lesson for STYLING (not just labeling/routing): `guidedStepLocked === true` drives the exact same grey-out + "Locked" badge/button pattern independently copy-pasted into `staff-task-card.tsx` (My Tasks), `staff-task-table.tsx` (Contract Staff Dashboard's "My Assigned Tasks"), and `staff-todays-work-panel.tsx` ("Today's Work" — currently dead code there since `pickNextTask()` already filters locked tasks out, but fixed anyway for consistency/robustness against that filter ever changing). Grepping for the shared boolean field name (`guidedStepLocked`) across the whole app, not just the one component the task text named ("My Tasks / Erection Dashboard"), is what surfaced all three; fixing only the named one would have left two visually-inconsistent duplicates of the exact same now-fixed bug.

### A component's own "how it looks when passed a boolean" contract can be simplified back to presentational-only once every caller decides not to pass that boolean anymore — don't leave dead conditional branches "just in case" (CM-71H.7)
- CM-71H.6 gave `ErectionStepGuidance` an `isStaffTier` prop that changed ITS OWN internal collapse/toggle behavior. This unit's task asked to go further — never render the card at all for staff-tier — which moves that decision to every CALL SITE (`{!isStaffTier && <ErectionStepGuidance>...}`) instead of into the component. Once every one of the 6 call sites was updated to the new pattern, the component's own `isStaffTier` prop and its `useState`/toggle-button logic became genuinely unreachable, not just unused for one case — so it was deleted back to a pure presentational component rather than left in place "in case a future caller wants the old collapse behavior." A prop that no live call site can any longer pass a meaningfully different value for is dead code, not a preserved extension point — delete it in the same change that removes the last caller, rather than accumulating unreachable branches.

## Guided Erection Workflow Layout Polish for Staff View (CM-71H.8)

### Removing a card from a shared grid row isn't the same as removing the ROW — check what's left behind before calling a "hide this for one tier" change complete (CM-71H.8)
- CM-71H.7's `{!isStaffTier && <ErectionStepGuidance>...}` correctly stopped Guidance from rendering, but the surrounding `grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-3` row it shared with Contract Summary was left in place and still rendered for staff-tier — just now with one card in it instead of two, `max-w-sm`-capped so it wouldn't stretch, but still occupying its own full grid row above the form. The visual result (a lone narrow card, then a gap, then the form starting a full row later) was exactly the "large empty space" this unit's task reported — a real regression introduced by a fix that was correct about WHICH ELEMENT to hide but hadn't checked whether the CONTAINER it lived in needed to go too. When a task says "remove X for this case," check every ancestor element X was the only meaningful content of — a wrapper `<div>`/grid row left behind with one child remaining is not the same as no wrapper at all.

### Moving a card from a "first-row, own-container" position into an EXISTING later sidebar is safer than inventing a new layout for one viewer tier — reuse the container, add an optional prop (CM-71H.8)
- The task asked for a "clean two-column layout" with Contract Summary in the sidebar for staff-tier, which could have meant building a whole new staff-tier-specific grid structure parallel to the manager-tier one. Instead, the EXISTING form+sidebar grid row (unchanged since CM-71A, already responsive, already `lg:col-span-2`/1-column-sidebar) was reused as-is, and each panel's own `*Sidebar` function gained one new optional `contract` prop rendering the exact same Contract Summary markup as its first card, passed only by the staff-tier branch. This meant zero new grid/breakpoint code, zero risk of the new staff-tier layout drifting out of sync with the manager-tier one over time (same container, same responsive rules, just a different set of children), and the "sidebar starts aligned with the form top" requirement fell out automatically from both living in the same grid row — never something that had to be separately coded or verified. When a task asks to relocate a UI element into a section that already exists elsewhere on the page, prefer adding an optional prop to the EXISTING section's component over building parallel layout structure for the tier that needs the change.

### `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true` requires `T | undefined` on the type, not just `?`, when a call site can explicitly pass `undefined` — a real strictness feature, not a lint quirk to silence (CM-71H.8)
- All 6 new sidebar `contract` props were typed as `contract?: {...}` and called as `contract={isStaffTier ? contract : undefined}` — correct-looking TypeScript that failed `tsc --noEmit` under this repo's `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true`, because `?:` alone means "the property may be omitted," not "the property may be omitted OR explicitly set to `undefined`" (two different things under this flag). The fix was `contract: {...} | undefined` (no `?`), matching how an explicit `undefined` value is actually being passed. Caught immediately by the typecheck step this unit's own workflow already runs before considering any change done — a reminder that "add `undefined` to the target's properties," when `tsc` suggests it under this flag, is the correct fix to make, not a warning to route around with a type assertion.

## Manager Preview Mode for Guided Erection Workflow Pages (CM-71H.9)

### An early-return that blocks a screen "for everyone when a prerequisite is missing" is a SINGLE gate doing two different jobs — separate them before adding a bypass, or the bypass leaks to the wrong audience (CM-71H.9)
- Before this unit, each later guided panel's `if (!prereqRecord) return <InfoBox>...</InfoBox>` served both "protect against invalid/crashing renders on null data" and "enforce that this step isn't ready yet" in one boolean check. Adding a preview bypass required splitting those concerns explicitly: `prerequisiteMissing` (the data-shape fact) stays computed exactly as before, but the RETURN now only fires `if (prerequisiteMissing && !isPreviewMode)` — the render-safety half (every downstream `statement.foo` access) still had to be independently guarded with optional chaining, because bypassing the early return does not make the underlying data any less null. Treat "block for data reasons" and "block for workflow-gating reasons" as two separate conditions from the start, even when today they happen to share one boolean — a future exception (preview mode, an admin override, a different tier) will need to split them anyway.

### A `?query=1` feature flag that only manager-tier can activate must check the RAW permission list, never a broadened per-step permission variable a nearby part of the same file already computes for an unrelated reason (CM-71H.9)
- Steps 1/3/5's own `page.tsx` files compute a widened `canUpdate = permissions.includes('contracts.update') || permissions.includes('contracts.workflow_update')` (CM-71H.7, so the Erection Manager can save their own owned steps). Preview-mode eligibility could easily have been implemented as "if `canUpdate` and `?preview=1`" by reusing that same nearby variable — which would have silently let an Erection Manager (staff-tier) trigger manager-only preview mode on exactly the 3 steps where `canUpdate` happens to be broadened. `resolveErectionPreviewMode()` instead re-checks `permissions.includes('contracts.update')` directly, independent of any local widening. When gating a new manager-only feature by permission, grep for whether a DIFFERENT local variable in the same file has already been intentionally broadened for a different purpose before reusing it — same name, different meaning, is exactly the kind of bug static analysis won't catch.

## Erection Workflow Manager Presentation Navigation (CM-71H.10)

### A feature can be fully correct and still fail on usability grounds — "the mechanism works" and "there's one obvious place to find it" are two separate acceptance bars, and a follow-up unit can exist purely to satisfy the second (CM-71H.10)
- CM-71H.9's `?preview=1` mechanism was complete and correct on every technical axis (gating, null-safety, disabled actions) — the ONLY gap this unit closed was discoverability: a manager had to already know the query param existed, or stumble onto a per-step "Preview Screen" fallback that only ever appeared for the one step that happened to be not-ready at that moment, never all six at once. The fix added zero new logic — a static array of the 6 steps' labels/paths, one `canManage`-gated section, 6 links reusing the exact existing `?preview=1` contract. When a task describes a UX/discoverability complaint about a feature that already technically works, resist the urge to touch the underlying mechanism — audit what's actually missing is usually just an ENTRY POINT, not new behavior.

## Executive Module Landing Pages and Senior-Friendly Navigation (FMP-UI-07)

### A "senior-friendly simplified overview" requirement for pages that already have a rich, working, heavily-iterated operational dashboard is an ADDITIVE new page, not a redesign of the existing one (FMP-UI-07)
- Every one of the 8 modules already had (or, for Erection, had a particularly complex) operational dashboard serving Managers/Staff/Admins today — some, like Contract Management's Manager/Staff-branched dashboard and Erection's work-queue tables, had survived many prior units of careful iteration (CM-37, CM-71B, the whole CM-71H.x series). Rather than simplify those in place (which would have regressed every existing persona's workflow to satisfy a persona-specific request), a brand-new landing route was added per module (`/module/executive`), and the operational route was left byte-for-byte untouched. The two are linked one-way only: the landing page's own "View Records" button points at the operational page/list; nothing on the operational side ever points at or depends on the landing page. Reusable takeaway: when a request asks for a simpler view of something that already has a battle-tested complex view serving other personas, add a new page addressed to the new persona rather than touching the existing one — the "don't break the existing workflow" constraint is trivially satisfied by construction, not by careful editing.

### A shared metric-rendering component that reads real API response fields generically (via `Object.entries`/key-name heuristics) can serve 6 differently-shaped dashboards without per-module custom code — but only stays honest if it never guesses a value, only formats one already computed elsewhere (FMP-UI-07)
- `ExecutiveKpiGrid` takes whatever `Record<string, number | null>` a module's own dashboard API already returns and renders one `MetricCard` per field, humanizing the key name and color-coding by substring heuristics on the KEY NAME (`overdue`/`critical`→error, `pending`/`waiting`→warning, `completed`/`resolved`→success) — never by inspecting or judging the VALUE. This let one component serve Incident Report's 5-field, Safety's 6-field, Erection's 7-field (after excluding one non-numeric flag), and Contract Management's 7-field metrics objects with zero per-module branching inside the component itself — each page just decides which real object to hand it. The one piece of real judgment (excluding `ErectionDashboardKpis.paymentPendingAfterErectionAvailable`, a fixed `false` boolean, not a metric) was made once, explicitly, in the one page that has that field — never inside the generic component, which would otherwise have had to special-case a non-numeric type it can't safely render as a count.

### When two dashboard endpoints that "do the same job" (recent activity) return differently-shaped rows, don't force the odd one through the shared component — render it inline and say why (FMP-UI-07)
- 6 of the module dashboard APIs (contracts/incidents/safety/production/maintenance/factory-tasks) share a byte-for-byte identical `recent: {id, referenceNumber, title, status, updatedAt}[]` shape, already served by the existing `DashboardRecentTable` component — reused as-is on 6 of the 8 new landing pages with zero new list-rendering code. Erection's `recentActivity` is a genuinely different shape (`{id, contractId, contractReference, event, actorName, createdAt}` — an event log, not a status-tracked record list), so `contracts/erection-executive/page.tsx` renders it with a small bespoke inline list instead of coercing it to fit `DashboardRecentTable`'s props. Reusable takeaway: "6 out of 8 modules share a shape" is a strong reason to reuse a component for those 6, and an equally strong reason NOT to force-fit the 7th and 8th into the same component via lossy field mapping — a few lines of page-local JSX for the genuine outlier is cheaper and more honest than widening a shared component's contract to paper over a shape mismatch.

### A route rename that only changes a config-level `route`/`href` string can still silently break an active-state highlighting rule that assumed the OLD string's exact suffix — grep every helper that pattern-matches on the string being renamed, not just its declared usages (FMP-UI-07)
- Changing `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS`'s Contract Management href from `/contracts/dashboard` to `/contracts/executive` looked like a pure data change (both are just strings passed to `<Link>`), but `sidebar.tsx`'s `isActive()` has a generic rule that treats any `/dashboard`-SUFFIXED href as "also active for its bare module base path" — and after generalizing that same rule to cover `/executive`-suffixed hrefs too (needed so the other 6 single-owner modules' landing-page sidebar items keep highlighting correctly while viewing a record reached from them), `/contracts/executive` would have inherited the exact same "also matches `/contracts` and everything under it" behavior that `isExecutiveItemActive()` already had a dedicated override to PREVENT for `/contracts/dashboard` — because Contract Management, uniquely among the 8 modules, shares its `/contracts/*` prefix with sibling sidebar entries (Technical, Erection). The override was found by reading `isExecutiveItemActive()`'s own doc comment (which named the exact old href it guarded) before assuming the rename was purely cosmetic, and moved to guard the new href instead. Reusable takeaway: before renaming a route/href string that already exists as a literal inside a pattern-matching helper (an `if (href === '...')`, a prefix list, a suffix check), grep for that literal string across the whole file/module, not just the declaration site — a helper written to special-case the OLD value doesn't automatically follow the rename.

## Executive Module Navigation Access Filtering, Quick Links, and Whole-Card Click (FMP-UI-07B)

### When a follow-up spec re-describes an already-shipped feature with a conflicting suggested architecture, surface the conflict and ask rather than silently redo or silently ignore it (FMP-UI-07B)
- The follow-up task read as if FMP-UI-07 (dedicated `/module/executive` landing pages, all 8 operational dashboards untouched) had never happened — it re-specified the same navigation goal but suggested embedding the nav banner directly into the existing operational dashboards and routing dashboard cards back to them, which would have meant editing Contract Management's and Erection's dashboards despite the task's own "do not break Contract Management/Erection" constraint. Rather than guessing which the user actually wanted (redo the architecture, or just treat the new spec as additional polish on the existing one), the two options were named explicitly and the user picked directly. Reusable takeaway: a request that appears to describe a feature as if it doesn't exist yet, when it demonstrably already shipped, is a signal to check for an architecture conflict before writing any code — not a signal to assume the new wording always wins.

### A permission-filtered nav list needs its OWN filter function, not just a reused "all modules" constant, because the wrong default (show everything) fails silently as dead links, not as an error (FMP-UI-07B)
- `ExecutiveModuleNav`'s chip row and Previous/Next originally iterated the full fixed `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` list unconditionally — correct for the one persona (Executive Manager) who holds all 6 underlying permissions, but wrong for any single-module viewer who could reach their own landing page directly (their own module's permission check passes) yet would then see chips for 7 modules a click would 404 on. This class of bug is easy to miss in testing because the primary persona (Executive Manager, all permissions) never triggers it — it only shows up for a narrower-access viewer, exactly the "other users should see only allowed modules" case the task called out by name. `getVisibleModules(permissions)` filters `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` down to modules whose `requiredPermission` the viewer actually holds — the SAME permission code each module's own controller and `PlatformDashboardService` already gate on, so this can never diverge from what's actually reachable. Reusable takeaway: any nav list built from a fixed "all items" constant needs to be explicitly re-derived per viewer before being tested only against the most-privileged persona.

### A "rename X everywhere" instruction still needs a whole-repo grep checked for false positives before applying — a short internal module name can coincide with real, unrelated, pre-existing terminology (FMP-UI-10C)
- Renaming "QA/QC" → "Quality Assurance & Control" surfaced, via a whole-repo grep run specifically to confirm completeness, that "QA/QC" already existed as genuine construction-industry terminology inside the unrelated Erection Checklist and Method Statement Approval workflows ("Reviewed By (QA/QC)", "QA/QC Engineer", "Submitted for QA/QC verification") — pre-existing, real business text that happens to share a short name with the Executive dashboard's new placeholder module. A "storage" grep hit the same way against unrelated file-attachment-storage services. Reusable takeaway: when a rename's OLD name is short/generic enough that it could plausibly appear elsewhere for an unrelated reason (an acronym, a common word), the completeness-check grep is exactly the moment to also actively look for and exclude genuine false positives — don't assume every match belongs to the feature being renamed just because the string matches.

### When "the frontend shows my change but the API-sourced data doesn't," check whether the RUNNING API process predates the source edit — `ts-node` without a watch flag never reloads (FMP-UI-10B)
- FMP-UI-10 added QA/QC and Storage & Delivery to `PlatformDashboardService.getDashboard()`, and the sidebar (a pure frontend list, `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS`) picked up the new items immediately via Next's dev fast-refresh. The dashboard cards did not, and stayed at 8 — because they come from a live HTTP call to the API, and the specific API process the browser was hitting had been running since before the `platform-dashboard.service.ts` edit existed. `apps/api/package.json`'s `dev` script is a plain `node -r ts-node/register src/main.ts` — no `--watch`/`nodemon`/`ts-node-dev` — so `ts-node` compiles the TypeScript to JS exactly once, at process startup, and never looks at the file again. The fix required zero code changes (the source, build, and unit tests were already correct) — only stopping the stale process and starting a fresh one from the same command. Reusable takeaway: when ONE half of a full-stack feature (frontend-rendered, fast-refreshed) works live but the OTHER half (API-sourced data) doesn't, and the source code for both looks correct on inspection, check the RUNNING API process's start time against the edit's timestamp before assuming the code itself is still wrong — `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process | Select ProcessId,CreationDate,CommandLine` (or the Unix equivalent) answers this in one command. This class of bug will recur for every future backend-only edit until the API's dev script gains an actual watch mode.

### A new route added under an existing shared prefix must be checked against every OTHER file that pattern-matches on that prefix, not just the files the new route's own feature touched (FMP-UI-07D)
- FMP-UI-07 added `/contracts/executive`, `/contracts/technical`, and `/contracts/erection-executive` — three new pages, all under the `/contracts/*` prefix. `TopHeader`'s own breadcrumb logic has a fallback classifier, `isContractWorkspaceDetailPath()`, that treats ANY `/contracts/{segment}` not in its own hardcoded `CONTRACT_MODULE_SEGMENTS` allowlist as if `{segment}` were a contract's UUID (i.e., "this must be a contract detail page") — a reasonable design for a route family that rarely grows, but it silently miscategorized all 3 new routes as contract-detail pages, rendering a wrong second breadcrumb above each landing page's own correct one. Nothing about building the landing pages themselves would have surfaced this — the bug lives entirely in a DIFFERENT file's pattern-matching logic that nobody re-checked when the new routes were added. Reusable takeaway: before (or immediately after) adding a new route under a prefix other code already pattern-matches on (`/contracts/*`, `/administration/*`, any shared prefix with its own classifier/allowlist), grep for every function that inspects that prefix's path segments — an allowlist-style classifier fails silently (wrong branch, not a crash) exactly the way this one did, so it won't be caught by typecheck, lint, or even most manual testing that doesn't specifically look at the header.

### "Fit content to the available height" has two very different fixes, and only one of them actually removes the empty space instead of relocating it (FMP-UI-09B)
- The Executive Dashboard's 2-row card grid had a large empty gap below it on a typical 1080p screen because each card's natural content height (~240px) was far shorter than the ~450px/row a tall `<main>` area could offer. The tempting fix — `grid-auto-rows: 1fr` (Tailwind's `auto-rows-fr`) so each row stretches to fill the available height — does remove the gap BELOW the grid, but only by creating a new one INSIDE every card, between the metrics and the `mt-auto` button, since the card's own content still only needs ~240px of whatever taller row it's now stretched into. The actual fix was to grow the content itself (every icon/text/padding/gap size, one step each) so the card's natural height is genuinely bigger, and separately center the modest resulting grid within the page's own available height so leftover slack (which will still exist on very tall screens) is split evenly above and below rather than concentrated in one place. Reusable takeaway: when a "make X fill the available space" request surfaces as an empty-space complaint, check whether the empty space would just move inside a child element instead of disappearing before reaching for a stretch/`1fr` mechanism — sizing the content itself, plus centering the leftover slack, is often the fix that actually satisfies "no lopsided empty space" rather than "no empty space at this one exact anchor point."

### Making a whole card clickable without breaking a nested interactive element inside it is an HTML constraint, not a styling one — move the interactive element up, don't nest a second one (FMP-UI-07B)
- The instinct for "make the whole card clickable, keep the visible button" might be to wrap the existing `<div>` card in an outer `<Link>` while leaving the inner "Open {title}" `<Link>` exactly as it was — but that nests an `<a>` inside an `<a>`, which is invalid HTML (browsers auto-close the outer one, breaking hydration/click targets unpredictably). The correct fix moves the ONE real interactive element to the outermost wrapper (the card root itself became the `<Link>`) and demotes the inner "button" to a non-interactive `<span>` styled identically — `group`/`group-hover:*` on the span reproduces whatever hover feedback the button alone used to give, and the focus ring moves to the new outer anchor so keyboard users still get exactly one focus stop, not a broken nested pair. Reusable takeaway: "make a card fully clickable while it already contains a link/button" is solved by relocating the interactivity outward one level, never by adding a second interactive wrapper around the first.

### When a follow-up task's stated symptom ("clicking X still opens the old page") is the premise for a whole unit, verify that symptom against current source FIRST — the real bug may be adjacent to, not identical to, what was reported (FMP-UI-07C)
- FMP-UI-07C opened with "live testing shows clicking Contract Management still opens /contracts/dashboard directly." Grepping `PlatformDashboardService`'s `route:` fields and `sidebar.tsx`'s `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS` before touching anything showed both already pointed at `/contracts/executive`, set by FMP-UI-07 and untouched since — the reported symptom did not match current source, most likely because whoever tested it was against a stale dev server/build predating that work (this whole feature has only ever existed as uncommitted, undeployed local changes). Blindly "fixing" the routing again would have been a no-op edit that looked productive but fixed nothing. Reading the unit's OWN detailed content spec for the Contract Management landing page against what FMP-UI-07 had actually shipped there surfaced the real, adjacent gap instead: that landing page never linked to `/contracts/dashboard` at all, and its KPIs/Needs Attention didn't match the specific fields this new spec named. Reusable takeaway: a bug report's stated symptom is a starting hypothesis, not a given — check it against the actual current code before spending the unit's effort re-doing work that direct inspection would show is already correct, and let that same inspection point at what's genuinely still missing.

## Add Estimation Module and Reorder Executive Modules (FMP-UI-23)

### A single ordered-array "source of truth" pattern (established FMP-UI-07) makes a full module reorder a one-file, zero-risk change — but only when every consumer genuinely reads from it rather than hardcoding its own copy (FMP-UI-23)
- Reordering the Platform Dashboard cards, the sidebar, the module switcher, and every page's Previous/Next to a brand-new 11-item sequence (inserting Estimation 2nd, moving Storage Yard & Delivery and Quality Control from positions 4–5 to 9–10) touched exactly 3 arrays: `PlatformDashboardService.getDashboard()`'s card-push order (backend), `_lib/executive-modules.ts`'s `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` (frontend switcher/Previous-Next), and `sidebar.tsx`'s `EXECUTIVE_SIDEBAR_ITEMS` (frontend sidebar) — because `ExecutiveModuleNav`, `IncidentModuleNav`, `SafetyModuleNav`, and `TaskModuleNav` all call `getModuleNeighbors()`/`getVisibleModules()` against the ONE `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` array rather than each hardcoding its own copy of the module list or order. Renaming 3 existing titles ("Incident Report" → "Incident Management", etc.) inside that same array propagated to every Previous/Next label and every switcher chip with no per-page edits. Reusable takeaway: the payoff of a single-source-of-truth ordered list isn't just avoiding duplication at write time — it's that a full reorder/rename months later becomes a small, provably-complete diff instead of a repo-wide hunt for every place the old order/labels were silently baked in.

### A new item's required position relative to an existing permission-gated GROUP of items may not align with that group's own code structure — restructure the block, don't just reorder items around it (FMP-UI-23)
- Estimation's required position (2nd, between Contract Management and Technical/Erection) fell INSIDE what was previously a single `if (actor.permissions.includes('contracts.read'))` block that fetched and pushed Contract Management, Technical, and Erection together from one `Promise.all`, all gated on the SAME permission. Estimation is gated on a different rule (`isExecutiveManagerOrAdminAccess`, no dedicated permission), so it couldn't just be inserted as a 4th array element in that block. The fix split the push (not the fetch — `Promise.all` still runs all 3 queries together) into `cards.push(contractCard)`, then a conditional `if (showPlaceholders) cards.push(buildEstimationCard())`, then `cards.push(technicalCard, erectionCard)` — plus a parallel `else if (showPlaceholders)` branch for the edge case of an Admin actor who lacks `contracts.read` itself. Reusable takeaway: when a new item's required position falls between two members of an existing group that share one gate/one fetch, check whether the new item's OWN gate matches that group's gate before assuming a simple array insert works — a mismatched gate means restructuring the push order (or gate check) itself, not just where in a list the new item's data appears.

### A long-running session's local Turborepo build cache is unbounded by default and will eventually fill the disk — treat `ENOSPC` mid-session as a cache-pruning signal, not a code bug (FMP-UI-23)
- Mid-unit, a routine `Edit` call failed with `ENOSPC` (no space left on device) — the C: drive had reached 0 bytes free. Investigation (`Get-ChildItem -Recurse | Measure-Object -Sum` over the usual suspects: `.next`, `node_modules/.cache`, pnpm store, npm cache) found the actual cause: `.turbo/cache` had grown to 180GB across this session's many `pnpm build` runs, since Turborepo never prunes its local cache automatically and this repo's `.turbo/` had apparently never been cleared. `rm -rf .turbo/cache` (not `.turbo` itself, which also holds config) freed 181GB immediately, with zero effect on source code, git history, or the next build's correctness (Turborepo just re-populates cache entries as tasks re-run, at the cost of a one-time full rebuild instead of cache hits). Reusable takeaway: an `ENOSPC` failure partway through a long session is a strong first hypothesis for "the local build-tool cache has grown unbounded," not a sign of a code/disk-hardware problem — checking `.turbo/cache`, `.next/cache`, and the pnpm/npm store sizes takes under a minute and is a safe, reversible fix before escalating.

## Fix Missing Estimation Dashboard Card, Dashboard Labels, and No-Scroll Layout (FMP-UI-23B)

### A follow-up bug report describing symptoms that exactly match the OLD pre-fix behavior, filed immediately after a unit that already fixed them in source, is the single strongest signal to check the RUNNING process's age before touching any code (FMP-UI-23B, recurrence of FMP-UI-10B)
- This ticket's reported symptoms — missing Estimation card, old titles ("Incident Report", "Production Planning", "Quality Assurance & Control"), and specifically "Storage Yard & Delivery before Safety & Compliance" (the EXACT pre-FMP-UI-23 push order, not some other wrong order) — were reproduced by re-reading `platform-dashboard.service.ts`, `EXECUTIVE_MODULES`, and `sidebar.tsx` and finding all three byte-for-byte already correct, already matching every acceptance criterion. That combination (symptom == old behavior, source == already fixed) is not ambiguous the way a generic "it's broken" report is — it is the fingerprint of a stale long-running process serving pre-edit compiled code, and `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process | Select ProcessId,CreationDate,CommandLine` confirmed it in one command: the API dev process's `CreationDate` predated the fixing edit's own file-modified timestamp. Reusable takeaway: when a bug report's symptom set matches a PREVIOUS, already-fixed state of the code rather than describing something genuinely new or different, check the serving process's age before writing a single line — this is the second time in this project this exact shape of bug has occurred (FMP-UI-10B first), and it will keep recurring for any `apps/api` edit made while an older `ts-node`-without-watch process is still running.

### "Do not allow the backend and frontend to have a different order — prefer one single source of truth" is sound intent but can collide with a real package boundary; a hand-duplicated array with a mutual doc-comment cross-reference is the correct compromise, not a forced merge (FMP-UI-23B)
- `apps/web`'s `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` (icons, JSX-adjacent `isVisible` closures) cannot be imported by `apps/api` (a separate NestJS package, no shared package hosts this data today) — so a literal single array was not achievable without introducing a new shared package purely to host an 11-row order/title list, which would have been disproportionate work for a divergence that, on inspection, never actually existed (both were already in sync; the bug was the stale process, not a data mismatch). The compromise already established elsewhere in this exact file for `isExecutiveManagerOrAdminAccess` (duplicated literally, with each copy's doc comment naming the other) was applied here too — added explicit "if this ever changes, update both together" comments to both `EXECUTIVE_MODULES` and `getDashboard()`'s push order. Reusable takeaway: "one single source of truth" is the right end-state to want, but when the two candidate locations sit on opposite sides of a real language/framework/package boundary, a clearly cross-referenced duplicate is an honest, working compromise — reach for a new shared package only when the duplication has actually caused a real divergence, not preemptively.

### Compacting a card grid for "fits without scrolling" is a uniform proportional size pass across every dimension, with one deliberate exception: a placeholder block must stay shorter than the thing it might sit next to, not just scaled down with everything else (FMP-UI-23B)
- `ExecutiveModuleCard` needed every dimension (padding, icon, title, description, metric tiles/numbers, button) eased down one more notch to fit an 11-card, 3-row grid into a normal 1920×1080 viewport — a straightforward proportional pass, same category as FMP-UI-10C's own earlier "narrower grid → smaller card" easing. The one place that got asymmetric treatment: the placeholder ("Setup Pending") block was kept deliberately SHORTER than the 2-row metrics grid it visually replaces, not scaled down to the same relative size — because a placeholder card (Estimation, Storage Yard & Delivery, Quality Control) can land in the same grid ROW as a live metrics card, and the grid's default `align-items: stretch` forces every card in a row to match its tallest member. If the placeholder block were sized to roughly match a 2×2 metrics grid, it would risk becoming that tallest member on some viewport width, stretching its live neighbors taller than THEIR OWN content needs — silently undoing the whole compaction. Reusable takeaway: when compacting a card that has two visually different content variants (live data vs. placeholder) sharing one grid, check whether they can ever land in the same row before assuming a uniform proportional shrink is sufficient — a variant that's structurally simpler than the other should usually end up shorter, not just equally-scaled.

## Live MMS Maintenance Dashboard (FMP-MAINT-01)

### External-system live dashboard pattern
- `/maintenance/dashboard` is the first FMP page whose data comes from another system (MMS). Pattern: a server component calls one FMP API endpoint that returns an explicit `status` (`ONLINE | OFFLINE | NOT_CONFIGURED | SCOPE_RESTRICTED`) plus `syncedAt`. The page renders exactly one state banner, KPI cards show "—" with an "Unavailable" chip when there's no data, and the browser never contacts the external system except through "Open in …" links (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`). Reuse this for future SAP/other read-only integrations.
- Components (local to `maintenance/dashboard/_components/`):
  - `MmsKpiCard`: TechnicalKpiCard's visual language, plus `value: number | null` and `unavailableText` for a per-metric "can't compute this" state. Never show 0 for unknown.
  - `MmsLiveControls`: Refresh button plus 60 s `router.refresh()` polling that pauses while `document.visibilityState` is hidden.
  - `MmsNeedsAttention`: warning-tinted rows with all applicable reasons joined by "·".
  - `MmsRecentTable`: 7 columns with a text-link "Open in MMS".
  - `MmsSyncStatus`: source, online pill, last synced, read-only badge, plain-language metric definitions.
- Maintenance accent is teal (`bg-teal-light text-teal`, matching `module-accent.ts` `maintenance`).
- Status badge colors by group, in `_lib/mms-format.ts` `MMS_STATUS_GROUP_CLASSES`:
  - Submitted / Closure Requested → warning
  - Active → info
  - Waiting parts → secondary-accent
  - In Progress → teal
  - Closed → success

## Live MMS Maintenance Dashboard via MMS API (FMP-MAINT-02)

- The external-system live dashboard pattern (FMP-MAINT-01) now has 6 states: `ONLINE | OFFLINE | NOT_CONFIGURED | AUTH_ERROR | NOT_ENABLED | SCOPE_RESTRICTED`. Each non-ONLINE state has exactly one banner. Only OFFLINE uses the error-red "unavailable" wording. AUTH_ERROR is red, NOT_ENABLED and SCOPE_RESTRICTED are amber, and NOT_CONFIGURED is info-blue.
- Polling is only for states that can recover on their own (ONLINE, OFFLINE, transient API failure). States that need an admin (config, auth, scope) don't poll.
- `MmsLiveControls` takes its interval from the server (`refreshSeconds`) with a client-side floor of 15 s. It stops the interval while the tab is hidden, rather than just skipping ticks, and refreshes immediately when the tab becomes visible.
- The overdue metric is labelled by its actual rule: card "Overdue / Past Start Time" with helper "Based on MMS start time rule". The MMS reason "Overdue" is shown as a "Past start time" chip. Don't call a rule "overdue" without qualifying it when the source system has no due date.
- Needs Attention reasons are colored chips (`reasonClasses`), and status badges are colored by the raw MMS status (`statusClasses`), with the raw value in the tooltip and MMS's `statusLabel` as the text.
- External links come from the source system (`openUrl`) but are origin-checked server-side (`safeOpenUrl`) before they reach an `href`.

## Maintenance Control Center (FMP-MAINT-03)

- **One component, two routes.** `MaintenanceControlCenter` (async Server Component in `maintenance/_components/mms/`) is rendered by both `/maintenance/executive` and `/maintenance/dashboard`. When a module has an executive landing route and a department route, check which one the Platform Dashboard card and sidebar actually open before redesigning — MAINT-01/02 changed the route most users never reach.
- **`MmsSyncStrip`:** a single-row provenance strip under the header (source, status dot + word, last synced, refresh interval, cache TTL, read-only, link out), with an optional second line for the specific reason when not online. Prefer this to a side panel for external-data pages.
- **`MmsUnavailableState`:** dashed-border card, muted icon in a circle, one sentence. The sentence says why there's no data for the current state; don't reuse "not deployed yet" wording for offline or restricted states.
- **`MmsQuickActions`:** vertical list of compact action rows (icon chip, label, one-line description, arrow). Replaces "Quick Links" chips plus an `h-12` primary button. External rows are teal, the FMP-local row is neutral and says "FMP local … (not MMS)".
- **Only real links.** If the source system has no view that matches a metric's rule, omit the link. Don't point at a near-match filter or at local data.
- **Reason summary chips** above a ranked list must say what they count ("in the N most urgent job cards from MMS") when the list is a server-limited sample.
- **Avoid duplicate nav.** A shared component with header actions should take a prop to drop an action the host page already provides (`showBackLink`).
- Header pill labels: "Live from MMS", "MMS not configured" (both not-configured and not-deployed), "MMS offline", "MMS auth error", "Restricted".

## Standardized Platform Dashboard Module Card (FMP-UI-24)

- **`ExecutiveModuleCard` is three fixed zones:** header (icon, title max 2 lines, badge; subtitle below with 2 lines reserved), content (`flex-1`), button (`h-9`). Any new card variant must fill the same content zone, never add a zone.
- **Equal height across the whole grid** needs `auto-rows-fr` on the grid. Row stretch alone only equalizes cards within a row. It is safe only when a zone inside the card is `flex-1` and its children stretch (`auto-rows-fr` tiles, or a `flex-1` block), otherwise the extra height becomes a gap above the button.
- **Reserve space instead of letting text push content.** A fixed row min-height for a 1-or-2-line title and a `min-h` + `line-clamp-2` subtitle keep the content zone at the same offset on every card.
- **Setup-pending block:** dashed border and tint in the module color, centered text, same minimum height as the 2x2 metric grid. This supersedes FMP-UI-23B's "keep the placeholder shorter" rule.
- **Grid columns by minimum card width, not viewport breakpoints:** `grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(min(100%,14rem),1fr))] lg:grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(17.5rem,1fr))]`. With a persistent sidebar, viewport breakpoints overestimate the content width. Inside `max-w-7xl` this yields at most 4 columns.
- **Container query for narrow cards:** the card is `@container`; under a 15.5rem content box the title moves to its own row (`@max-[15.5rem]:order-3 @max-[15.5rem]:basis-full`) and the badge sits beside the icon. Prefer this to truncating a long module title.
- **Theme-safe tints:** use the module base color with an alpha suffix (`${base}12`) for tile backgrounds. A fixed light pastel hex only works on a light surface.
- **Verify layout numerically.** A Playwright script that reports the set of distinct card heights, button offsets, and content offsets across all cards catches 2px drift that a screenshot review misses.

## MMS Executive Summary (FMP-MAINT-04)

- **Optional external sections:** each MMS section arrives as `Section | null`. A panel takes the section plus an `unavailableText`; `null` renders that text ("Not available from MMS live API yet" when online, the offline/config reason otherwise). Never default a missing section to zeros.
- `MmsKpiCard`: `display` shows a formatted value (e.g. "12.5 h") while `value` still decides availability; `compact` is the secondary-row variant (icon, value, label on one line).
- `MmsManagerAttention`: a six-tile count row (tinted only when the count is above 0, each tile linking to the MMS page that lists it) above a divided list of typed items (`attentionTypeLabel` / `attentionTypeClasses`).
- ~~`mms-panels.tsx`~~ **Removed 2026-10-03.** Its five expanded detail panels were replaced by `MmsModulesSummary` (FMP-MAINT-05) and the file was deleted. The `Section | null` rule above still applies to the summary rows.
- Dates from another system that are date-only (`YYYY-MM-DD`) are formatted with `formatDateOnly` (string-based) so server and browser locale data cannot disagree.

## One-Screen Executive Control Center (FMP-MAINT-05)

- **Budget the height first.** A dashboard that must fit 1366x768 has about 700px under the app header. Maintenance uses: header 56, KPI tiles 56, mini-stats 32, one two-column grid about 400, gaps of 10.
- **`MmsCard`** (`maintenance/_components/mms/mms-card.tsx`): slim uppercase header row with an optional right-side `aside`, body fills the rest. Two cards in a column both take `flex-1` so the column matches its neighbour. `MmsCardNote` is the one-line muted empty/unavailable text.
- **KPI tiles are horizontal** (icon, number, label on one row; accent on the left edge, not the top). A mini-stat is label-left / value-right. Helper text moves to the tooltip.
- **Lists are single-line rows** where the whole row is the link out; detail goes in the tooltip. Cap at 5 and say so in the card header ("top 5", "Latest 5 · all …").
- **Module summary rows** instead of panels: icon, title, three inline `value label` pairs, arrow. Keep labels to one short word; long labels truncate first on a 1366px screen.
- **Say why data is missing once.** One banner. Everywhere else: "—" for a value, one short line for a section. Never repeat the explanation per card.
- **Drop columns, not readability.** The recent table hides Assigned To below `2xl` and Asset / Location below `xl` rather than scrolling sideways.
- **Low-priority chrome can be height-gated:** `hidden [@media(min-height:900px)]:block` for the source line.
- **Verifying a state the test accounts can't reach:** a throwaway local proxy that overrides one API response with sample data lets the real page be screenshotted. Label it as sample data in the report and keep it out of the repo.

## Public links and user-facing wording for an external system (FMP-MAINT-06)

- **Two addresses, two jobs.** An integrated system has an internal address (server-to-server calls) and a public one (links users click). Only the public one may appear in an API response or the page. Normalize every external link server-side in one function (`safeMmsUrl`): relative and known-host links go onto the public base; unknown hosts are dropped for a known route.
- **Spell out system names on buttons.** "Open Maintenance Management System" in the header, "Open Maintenance System" where space is tight. Abbreviations like "MMS" are fine in status/source text ("Live from MMS").
- **Repeated row tags: dot + quiet label, not filled pills.** In a list where most rows share a category, a filled colored pill per row outweighs the content. Use a `size-1.5` colored dot (`bg-current` inside the tag's text color, see `tagDotColor`) and a muted 10px label, in a fixed-width column.
- **Fixed-width leading columns** (tag, ref) make single-line list rows scannable.
- **An empty section should not stretch.** Give a card `flex-1` only when it has rows; its empty state is one short line.
- **Compact button labels wrap, not truncate:** `line-clamp-2 leading-[1.1]` inside a fixed-height button.
- **Header with a long primary button:** give the title block `flex-1 basis-80 min-w-0` so the title area wraps internally before the action buttons drop to a second row.


### FMP-TASK-01 — Task Control Center tabs and list
`TaskControlCenterList` rows show "Assigned by / Assigned to" in one column; tab order and empty states live in `TASK_CONTROL_CENTER_TABS`; pure helpers `isTaskUrgent`, `sortCompletedTasks`, `getAssignedByLabel`, `PENDING_STATUSES` in `task-control-center-helpers.ts`.

### FMP-TASK-02 — Task work-queue rows and compact overview card
`TaskControlCenterList` is now a row list (From:/To: labels, no table, no horizontal scroll). `TaskOverviewCard` (`factory-tasks/_components/`) is the compact icon+count+label card used for the task overview row.

### FMP-TASK-03 — Task View popup
`TaskViewModal` (`factory-tasks/_components/`) is the simple "Task Details" dialog opened by View on `TaskControlCenterList` rows (dashboard + Task List page). Button rules come from `computeTaskViewActions()`; the list now needs a `people` prop for its Assign form.

### FMP-TASK-04 — Task List tabs
The Task List page reuses `TASK_CONTROL_CENTER_TABS` (pill tabs via `?tab=`) and one plain-word filter row; no component changes.

### FMP-TASK-05 — Create Task popup
`NewTaskButton` (popup + success message) and `NewTaskForm` (Task Name, Task Details, searchable `PersonPicker`, Priority, Due Date, file list with Remove) in `factory-tasks/_components/`; `NewTaskPageForm` wraps the same form for `/factory-tasks/new`. `TaskForm` is now edit-draft only.

### FMP-TASK-06 — Form field states
Create Task fields use `fieldClass(hasError)`: neutral border + soft accent ring on focus; solid `error` border only with a visible message. Use `error` tokens (there is no `danger` token). Popup layout: fixed header, scrolling body, fixed footer, `max-h-[85vh]`.

### FMP-TASK-07 — Attachment list in popups
In `NewTaskForm` the upload box collapses to a one-line "Add more files" strip once files exist, selected files are cards (icon / truncated name / size / Remove), and the popup body scrolls the list into view above a fixed footer.

### FMP-TASK-08 — Dashboard "Recent Tasks" header
Task dashboard list is capped at 5 rows with a header (Recent Tasks / "Showing latest 5 of N tasks" / View All Tasks button).

### FMP-TASK-09 — "Department: Not set"
Task rows, the View popup and the Assign To picker show "Not set" when a task/person has no department; it is never an error.

### FMP-TASK-10 — Add Files on task page
`TaskAttachmentUpload` (task detail "Attachments" section): "Add Files" button with the same type/size rules as Create Task and friendly error text.

### FMP-TASK-11 — Task Detail page layout
Two-column work page: header card with a 4-item summary strip (`SummaryItem`), section cards (What needs to be done / Files / Update progress / Comments & history) and a side column (Actions / Task summary (`SummaryRow`) / Next step). `AddProgressForm` and `AddTaskCommentForm` now submit via client handlers and refresh the page; `TaskActivityTimeline` shows plain-language history only.

### FMP-CONTRACT-01 — Contract Parties and Basic Contract Details
- `PartyFormModal` (`contracts/parties/_components/`) — Add/Edit party popup. `compact` + `fixedType` gives the quick "Add new customer" (Company Name + Contact No only); the New Contract Register renders it through a portal so its own `<form>` is never nested in the register form.
- `PartyList` — Contract Parties table (Company Name / Type / Contact No / Status / Actions) with Add Party, Edit, Deactivate/Activate. Parties are never deleted.
- `BasicContractDetailsFields` (`contracts/new/_components/`) — Customer (First Party) + Second Party dropdowns (Second Party starts on RECAFCO), Job Order, Quotation No, Project Number, Project Name; field errors under each field.
- Wording lives in `_lib/contract-party-helpers.ts` (`PARTY_TYPE_LABELS`, `validateBasicDetails`, `displayFirstParty`, `displaySecondParty`); do not reintroduce "Company Name" / "Counterparty" in this form.

### FMP-CONTRACT-02 — Contract Parties two-panel layout
`PartyList` now renders two `PartyPanel`s (Customer (First Party) | Second Party) of list rows instead of one table: count in each panel header ("Customer (First Party) — 1"), per-panel "+ Add Customer" / "+ Add Second Party" (preselects the Type via `PartyFormModal`'s `defaultType`, still editable), plain empty states, and one search box (`filterParties`) over both panels. Stacks on screens below `lg`, customers first.

### FMP-BOQ-01 — Simple BOQ input on New Contract Register
`ContractBoqRegisterTable` is now a list of card rows (Item Description | Unit | Qty | Unit Price | calculated Total | Remove) with one column header on desktop and per-field labels on small screens — no horizontal scroll. "+ Add Item" and a Total Amount box sit below. Validation is `validateRegisterBoqRows` (plain messages); unit names come from `unitLabel`. Edit Contract keeps its own `ContractBoqTable`.

### FMP-BOQ-02A — BOQ columns on New Contract Register
`ContractBoqRegisterTable` columns: S/N, Item Description, Unit (only Nos / M² / M³ / LM via `REGISTER_UNIT_OPTIONS`), **Contract Qty** (never plain "Qty"), Unit Price, Total, Remove. Note under the table: "Final piece quantity will be confirmed from Technical drawings."

### FMP-BOQ-02B — BOQ unit order and note
`REGISTER_UNIT_OPTIONS` order is now M², M³, Nos, LM. The note under the BOQ reads "Contract Qty is for contract value. Piece quantity will be confirmed later from Technical drawings." (replaces the 02A note).

### FMP-BOQ-03 — BOQ Drawing Confirmation (Technical job page)
`BoqDrawingConfirmation` (`technical/jobs/[contractId]/_components/`) — section on the Technical job page: one card per BOQ item (Contract Qty + unit, Confirmed Pieces in Nos or "Not confirmed yet"), "Add Drawing Confirmation", "View Confirmations (n)" expanding per-drawing rows with a status badge (Draft / Confirmed / Revised / Cancelled) and Edit (drafts) / Revise (confirmed) / Cancel. Its popup has Save Draft and Confirm. Wording/validation in `technical/_lib/boq-confirmation-helpers.ts`. Contract detail's BOQ card gained a read-only "Drawing Confirmed Pieces" column.

### FMP-BOQ-04 — Pieces in BOQ Drawing Confirmation
`BoqDrawingConfirmation` now also has: a **Generate Pieces** button in the section header (shown only while a Confirmed drawing still has pieces to create — `shouldShowGeneratePieces`), a per-item summary (Pieces Generated, status chips from `pieceStatusSummary`, a "Needs Attention" badge when pieces ≠ Drawing Confirmed Pieces) and a **View Pieces** popup (`PiecesModal`: Piece Code, Drawing No, Status badge, Size / Specification, Location, Updated; filter chips All / Drawing Ready / In Production / Produced / In Store / Delivered / Erected / Hold / Rejected / Cancelled). Labels and colours: `BOQ_PIECE_STATUS_LABELS` / `BOQ_PIECE_STATUS_CLASSES` in `technical/_lib/boq-confirmation-helpers.ts`. Read-only: nothing here changes a piece status.

### FMP-BOQ-05 — View Pieces: select, update status, history
`PiecesModal` (in `boq-drawing-confirmation.tsx`) now has, for users who can write: a tick box per piece + "Select all shown", an "Update selected to" status dropdown (`BOQ_PIECE_UPDATE_OPTIONS`, friendly labels only), an optional Note and an **Update Status** button, with a plain result message ("Pieces updated." / "8 pieces updated. 2 pieces skipped."). Every row has a **History** button that expands "Piece History" (date/time, from → to, note, who). Read-only users see the list and History but no tick boxes or update bar. Filters keep working after an update and the item's status chips refresh.

### FMP-BOQ-06 — Piece update dropdown follows the user's department
The "Update selected to" list in View Pieces now shows only the statuses the API allows for the current user (`allowedPieceStatuses` prop → `pieceUpdateOptionsFor`): normal Technical users get Drawing Ready, Hold, Rejected, Cancelled; manager/admin override users get all ten. If the list is empty or could not be loaded the tick boxes and update bar are hidden (read-only list). Status chips and filters always show every status.

### FMP-BOQ-07 — Piece Production screen (`/production/pieces`)
Page "Piece Production" (Production & Planning): four summary cards (`SUMMARY_CARDS`: Ready for Production / In Production / Produced / On Hold / Rejected), a plain GET filter form (Search, Status — default "Ready + In Production" —, Contract, Drawing No, BOQ Item; Apply / Clear filters), `PieceProductionBoard` (tick boxes + Select all shown, "Update selected to" limited to what the API allows, Note, Update Status, History per piece = same "Piece History" block as the Technical popup) and Previous/Next paging. Empty state: "No pieces ready for production." + helper text. Entry point: a "Piece Production" button on the Production dashboard header (the sidebar has no Production submenu, so it is unchanged). Reuses the Technical piece wording/classes from `technical/_lib/boq-confirmation-helpers.ts`.

### FMP-BOQ-08 — Piece Delivery screen (`/storage-delivery/pieces`)
Same layout as Piece Production, for Storage Yard & Delivery: summary cards (Ready for Store = Produced / In Store / Delivered / On Hold / Rejected), GET filter form (Search, Status — default "Produced + In Store" —, Contract, Drawing No, BOQ Item), `PieceDeliveryBoard` (tick boxes, "Update selected to" limited to In Store / Delivered / Hold / Rejected, Note, an optional **Location** box that only appears when In Store or Delivered is chosen, Update Status, History per piece, a Location text on every row) and paging. Empty state: "No pieces ready for storage or delivery." Entry points: sidebar item "Storage Yard & Delivery" (needs `storage_delivery.read`) and a "Piece Delivery" button on the executive Storage Yard & Delivery page. Wording shared with the other piece screens from `technical/_lib/boq-confirmation-helpers.ts`.

### FMP-BOQ-09 — Piece Erection screen (`/erection/pieces`)
Same layout as Piece Delivery, for Erection: summary cards (Ready for Erection = Delivered / Erected / Completed / On Hold / Rejected), GET filter form (Search, Status — default "Delivered + Erected" —, Contract, Drawing No, BOQ Item), `PieceErectionBoard` (tick boxes, "Update selected to" limited to Erected / Completed / Hold / Rejected, Note, an optional "Location / site note" box that only appears for Erected or Completed, Update Status, History per piece, Location on every row) and paging. Empty state: "No pieces ready for erection." Entry points: sidebar item "Piece Erection" (needs `erection.read`), a "Piece Erection" button on the Erection dashboard and the executive Erection page. The existing Erection dashboard link/page is unchanged.

### FMP-BOQ-10 — BOQ Progress tab (Contract Detail, read-only)
New workspace tab "BOQ Progress" (`/contracts/[id]/boq-progress`, after Production Status). `BoqProgressView`: seven total cards (Confirmed Pieces, Pieces Generated, Produced, Delivered, Erected, Completed, Needs Attention); one card per BOQ item with Contract Qty + unit, Drawing Confirmed Pieces, Pieces Generated, a "N of M completed" bar (Completed / Pieces Generated), eight status tiles (Drawing Ready, In Production, Produced, In Store, Delivered, Erected, Completed, Hold / Rejected), a "Needs Attention" badge with "Some pieces need review." and the reasons, and a read-only **View Pieces** list with the status filters (incl. Cancelled). Plain empty states: "No BOQ items found." / "Technical has not confirmed drawing pieces yet." / "Pieces have not been generated yet." / "No pieces found." There are no tick boxes, status dropdowns or buttons that change data anywhere on this tab. Logic: `contracts/_lib/boq-progress-helpers.ts`.

## Redesigned Contract Management Dashboard with Contract/Project Selector (FMP-UI-29)

### ContractDashboardLeftColumn (the dashboard's one client component)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/executive/_components/contract-dashboard-left-column.tsx`
- Purpose: owns the single piece of client state the redesigned `/contracts/executive` page's left column shares across 3 otherwise-separate-looking sections — a search box, a "Selected Contract Progress" card, and the Recent Contracts table all read/write the SAME `selected`/`boqItems` state. A search pick or a Recent Contracts row click both call the same internal `selectBasics()`; only the explicit "View Contract"/"View BOQ Progress"/per-row "View" `<Link>`s navigate away.
- Reusable pattern: when a page needs ONE piece of shared interactive state (here: "which record is selected") consumed by several visually-separate sections that a plain Server Component can't coordinate on its own, put ALL of those sections inside ONE client component rather than several small ones each needing the state passed/lifted awkwardly between siblings — everything else on the page that doesn't need that state (KPI row, Needs Attention, Today's Focus, Overall BOQ Piece Progress) stays a plain server-rendered server component, keeping the client bundle to exactly the piece that needs it.
- Data flow: receives `initialContract`/`initialBoqItems` (the page's own default pick, already fetched server-side — no redundant fetch on first paint) and `recentContracts`/`recentContractsBoqItems` (5 rows, already fetched server-side in parallel). Live search calls `searchContractsForDashboardAction` (debounced 300ms); selecting a NEW contract (one not in the initial 5) calls `getContractBoqProgressForDashboardAction`; selecting a Recent Contracts row reuses its already-fetched `recentContractsBoqItems[i]` instead of re-fetching.
- Notes: both server actions it calls are read-only, additive wrappers in `contracts/actions.ts` around EXISTING functions (`contractsApi.list()`'s own `search` filter; `fetchBoqConfirmations()`, already used by the per-contract BOQ Progress tab) — they exist only because those underlying functions read the access token via `next/headers`, which requires a server context a client component doesn't have.

### Contract Management dashboard selector/progress helpers (FMP-UI-29)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/executive/_lib/dashboard-selector-helpers.ts`
- Purpose: every pure computation the redesigned dashboard needs that isn't already covered by `contracts/_lib/boq-progress-helpers.ts`'s existing `contractTotals()` — `pickDefaultContractId` (most recently updated ACTIVE contract), `formatBoqProgressForRow` (Recent Contracts' compact BOQ Progress column text), `selectedContractProgressState` (the Selected Contract Progress card's 3-state empty message), `buildTodaysFocus`/`buildNeedsAttentionRows` (filter/shape real figures already computed elsewhere on the page into the two required lists), `buildSelectedContractIssues`.
- Reusable takeaway: `selectedContractProgressState()` deliberately reuses `MESSAGES.noConfirmations`/`MESSAGES.notGenerated` from the EXISTING per-contract BOQ Progress tab's own helper file verbatim (both happened to be the exact wording this unit's own ticket specified too) rather than redefining equivalent strings locally — when a new page needs a message an existing page already shows for the same underlying state, importing the constant (not just happening to retype the same words) keeps them from silently drifting apart later.

### ContractBoqPieceOverview — cross-contract BOQ piece aggregate (FMP-UI-29)
- Path: `apps/api/src/contracts/contract-dashboard.service.ts` (`buildBoqPieceOverview()`, `ContractBoqPieceOverview` interface); mirrored type in `apps/web/src/lib/contracts-api.ts`.
- Purpose: the ONE place in this codebase that computes BOQ piece progress ACROSS MULTIPLE contracts at once (every other piece/confirmation query in this app, including the per-contract BOQ Progress tab, is scoped to a single `contractId`). Reused the exact business rules `technical/boq-piece-generation.ts`'s `summarizeItemPieces()` already defined per-contract (confirmed = sum of CONFIRMED rows; generated excludes cancelled pieces; produced/delivered/erected/completed are CURRENT-status snapshots, not a cumulative funnel) rather than inventing new semantics for the aggregate case.
- Reusable takeaway 1 — a cross-record aggregate of a value that's normally computed per-record needs the SAME record's own already-chosen scope filter threaded through, not a fresh one: `buildBoqPieceOverview(contractWhere)` takes the literal same `where` object `getDashboard()` already built for the contract list itself (non-cancelled, department-scoped) as a nested Prisma relation filter (`contract: contractWhere`) on the piece/confirmation tables — this is the only way the aggregate can never show data outside what that same actor's contract list already shows.
- Reusable takeaway 2 — "how many records disagree between two aggregates" needs item-level `groupBy`, even when neither headline total does: `itemsNeedingReview` (BOQ items whose generated-piece count doesn't match confirmed-piece count) couldn't be derived from the 2 straightforward aggregate queries (total confirmed sum, piece-status-count snapshot) because both of those collapse away the `boqItemId` grouping — two MORE `groupBy(['boqItemId'])` queries were needed specifically to compare per-item, even though the final answer is just one number.
- Placed inside the Contract Management domain's own dashboard service, never inside any file under `apps/api/src/technical/`, even though it queries tables that module's services also query — when a ticket explicitly puts a module's files off-limits, a read-only cross-cutting aggregate over that module's tables can still live entirely in the CALLING domain's own service.

## Redesigned Technical Dashboard for One-Screen Workflow Control (FMP-UI-31)

### TechnicalJobSelector (the dashboard's one client component)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/technical-job-selector.tsx`
- Purpose: same role as `ContractDashboardLeftColumn` (FMP-UI-29) one unit earlier — owns the one shared piece of client state (which job is selected) a search box and a Selected Job Progress card both read/write.
- Reusable pattern, extended from FMP-UI-29: search here runs entirely against the already-loaded `jobs` array (`searchJobsLocally()`, pure, no network call) rather than a server action — because the Technical dashboard's own `jobs` list already holds up to 50 rows (effectively every job in scope), unlike Contract Management's dashboard, which only pre-fetches 5 "recent" rows and genuinely needs a live search action for anything beyond those. When the data a selector needs to search is already fully loaded on the page, searching it client-side is simpler and faster than reaching for a server action by default — reach for the server round-trip only when the full dataset genuinely isn't already on the page.
- Reuses, unchanged, the exact read-only server action FMP-UI-29 added for Contract Management's own selector (`getContractBoqProgressForDashboardAction` in `contracts/actions.ts`) for the per-job BOQ summary — it's generic (any contract id), so a second module needing the same per-contract BOQ data required zero new backend code.
- A real build break and its fix are worth remembering for any FUTURE client component added to this module: see `STAGE_LABELS`'s own entry below.

### STAGE_LABELS — a client-safe local copy, and why it had to exist (FMP-UI-31)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_lib/technical-format.ts`
- `TechnicalJobSelector` (a `'use client'` component) first imported `TECHNICAL_STAGE_LABELS` as a VALUE directly from `@/lib/technical-api` — that file does a module-level `import { cookies } from 'next/headers'`, and any value import from it (not a `type` import, which is erased at compile time) pulls that server-only dependency into the client bundle, breaking the build ("Ecmascript file had an error"). This exact problem already has a precedent and a fix pattern in this same file: `STAGE_ORDER` is already documented (this file's own top-of-file comment) as a deliberate local copy of `TECHNICAL_STAGE_ORDER` for the identical reason. `STAGE_LABELS` (a local copy of `TECHNICAL_STAGE_LABELS`) was added alongside it.
- Reusable takeaway: before a new `'use client'` component in this module imports ANYTHING from `technical-api.ts` (or any sibling `*-api.ts` file that reads cookies), check whether the import is `import type` (always safe, erased at compile time) or a real value import (only safe inside a Server Component). `_lib/technical-format.ts` is the established place to add a client-safe local copy of a technical-api.ts constant — check there first before importing the original.

### Next Action Focus and Needs Attention panels — rebuilt in opposite directions (FMP-UI-31)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_components/next-action-panel.tsx`, `needs-attention-panel.tsx`
- `NextActionPanel` (FMP-TECH-05/05P) used to render ONE aggregate sentence ("3 jobs need attention. Most urgent: ..."); rebuilt into a real top-5 list of individual Needs Attention items (Job Order/Contract No., Project, Next Action, Current Stage, Open button) — the aggregate-sentence design couldn't show more than one job at a time, which a direct "show the top 3 to 5 items" requirement made impossible to satisfy by tweaking it in place.
- `NeedsAttentionPanel` (FMP-TECH-01C/05) used to render an UNCAPPED per-record list (one row per flagged job, no limit) — a real contributor to "dashboard too tall" on any contract/department with many flagged jobs. Rebuilt into the SAME "per-record list → fixed named counts" shape Contract Management's own Needs Attention panel went through one unit earlier (FMP-UI-29): 5 fixed rows (Overdue Jobs, Waiting Approval, Missing BOQ Drawing Confirmation, Confirmed Pieces Not Generated, Rejected/Hold Pieces), always all 5, neutral at 0.
- Reusable takeaway: these 2 sibling panels needed opposite treatments for the SAME underlying complaint ("make the page shorter, keep the information"). One was too narrow (1 sentence hiding N real items — widen it to a capped list) and the other was too wide (N real items with no cap — narrow it to a fixed set of counts). Don't assume every panel on a "too tall" dashboard needs the same kind of fix; check whether each one is currently UNDER- or OVER-showing its own data before picking a direction.

### TechnicalBoqAttentionSummary — a second cross-contract BOQ aggregate, scoped tighter than the first (FMP-UI-31)
- Path: `apps/api/src/technical/technical.service.ts` (`buildBoqAttentionSummary()`, `TechnicalBoqAttentionSummary`); mirrored type in `apps/web/src/lib/technical-api.ts`.
- Same shape as `ContractBoqPieceOverview`/`buildBoqPieceOverview()` (FMP-UI-29, `contract-dashboard.service.ts`) — read-only `groupBy`/`findMany` queries against the same `ContractBoqDrawingConfirmation`/`ContractBoqPiece` tables, mirroring the same per-item rules `boq-piece-generation.ts` already defined. The one real difference: scoped not just to department access but to `contractId: { in: startedContractIds }` — only jobs whose Technical workflow has actually started — rather than every contract in department scope. A not-yet-started job trivially has zero BOQ confirmation, so including it would inflate "missing confirmation" with jobs nobody is working on yet, making the number technically real but practically meaningless.
- Reusable takeaway: "real data, correctly scoped" has two separate dimensions — WHO can see it (department/permission scope, the dimension FMP-UI-29's own aggregate got right first) and WHICH records are even relevant to the question being asked (here: only workflow-started jobs). A number can pass the first test and still mislead if the second one is skipped — always ask "does every record in this scope actually have a chance to be the thing I'm counting toward/against" before finalizing an aggregate's `where` clause.

## Redesigned Production & Planning Dashboard for Piece Production Flow (FMP-UI-32)

### ProductionContractSelector — the simplest of the 3 dashboard selectors (FMP-UI-32)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/production/executive/_components/production-contract-selector.tsx`
- Same role as `ContractDashboardLeftColumn` (FMP-UI-29) and `TechnicalJobSelector` (FMP-UI-31) — the one client component on its dashboard, owning the selected-record state. The simplest of the 3: `ProductionPiecesService.contractProgress()` already returns every contract's COMPLETE piece breakdown (ready/inProduction/produced/onHold/rejected) in one array, so selecting a different contract needs no fetch, no loading state, and no server action at all — a plain `useState` swap. Neither of the other 2 dashboards' selectors could do this, because their own per-record detail (BOQ confirmation items, a job's Technical workflow stage) genuinely isn't part of the list they search.
- Reusable takeaway: before reaching for the "search locally, fetch on select" pattern FMP-UI-29/31 established, check whether the record list already search is built from ALREADY CONTAINS everything the selected-detail view needs — if the aggregate query backing the list is a per-record breakdown (not just identity fields), the "fetch on select" half of that pattern may be unnecessary entirely.

### ProductionContractProgress — a 5-section aggregate from one query (FMP-UI-32)
- Path: `apps/api/src/production/production-pieces.service.ts` (`contractProgress()`, `buildProductionContractProgress()`); mirrored type in `apps/web/src/lib/production-pieces-api.ts`.
- One `contractBoqPiece.findMany` (same non-cancelled population every other method on this service already reads), reduced in JS into one row per contract. Feeds 5 separate dashboard sections — KPI row, Overall Production Flow, the Contract/Project selector's search, Selected Project Production, and the Production Work Queue's ranking — from the single array, no per-section query.
- Deliberately a `findMany` + JS reduce, not a `groupBy(['contractId','currentStatus'])`: a groupBy can't carry a contract's own `referenceNumber`/`jobOrder`/`title` (not a groupable scalar) or a per-contract MAX(updatedAt) in the same call, both of which this page needs — a second query would have been required either way, so one larger read beat two smaller-but-incomplete ones.
- Added to the EXISTING Piece Production screen's own service/controller (`production-pieces.service.ts`/`.controller.ts`), not a new file or module — when a ticket asks for a dashboard built on a module's existing read-only data, and that module already has the right service file, a new method there is simpler than inventing a new home for it.
- Neither this nor the sibling `recentUpdates()` (latest piece status changes, reusing the existing `contractBoqPieceStatusHistory` table the per-piece `history()` method already reads) applies department scoping — this matches every OTHER method on this same service (`summary()`, `contractOptions()`, `list()`), none of which scope by department either (the whole Piece Production engine is permission-only, `production.read`/`production.update`/`production.manage`, not department-partitioned) — so these 2 additions extend the screen's existing visibility rule rather than inventing a new one.

### A link with a real, already-supported filter param beats inventing a new one (FMP-UI-32)
- "Production Work Queue"'s per-row "Open" button links to `/production/pieces?contractId={id}` — checked, before using it, that `contractId` is a real URL param the Piece Production screen's own `readFilterValues()`/`buildPieceQuery()` already reads and applies, not a guess. Reusable takeaway, continuing FMP-UI-29/31's own "don't add a broken button" precedent one step further: when a ticket says "open with a filter if possible," check the TARGET page's own existing query-param handling before either adding a plain unfiltered link (gives up on a feature that already exists) or inventing a new param the target page doesn't read (looks like it works, silently does nothing).

## Redesigned Storage Yard & Delivery Dashboard for Piece Delivery Flow (FMP-UI-33)

### A structural copy of the PREVIOUS unit's own dashboard, not a fresh design — because the 2 modules share one engine (FMP-UI-33)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/executive/storage-delivery/` (`_lib/storage-dashboard-helpers.ts`, `_components/storage-contract-selector.tsx`, `_components/storage-needs-attention-panel.tsx`, `page.tsx`); backend `apps/api/src/storage-delivery/storage-delivery-pieces.service.ts` (`contractProgress()`, `recentUpdates()`).
- This dashboard is close to a field-renamed copy of the Production & Planning dashboard built one unit earlier (FMP-UI-32) — same helper function names/shapes (just Ready-for-Store/In-Store/Delivered instead of Ready/In-Production/Produced), same selector architecture (no fetch on select, full per-contract breakdown already in hand), same Needs Attention panel shape (4 fixed rows, neutral at 0), same Work Queue ranking logic.
- Reusable takeaway: when 2 modules already share one backend engine (here: `technical/boq-piece-screen.ts`/`boq-piece-generation.ts` — `ProductionPiecesService` and `StorageDeliveryPiecesService` already mirror each other method-for-method, just with different visible-status sets and write permissions), a dashboard-redesign ticket for the SECOND module is less "design something new" and more "port the first module's own dashboard shape, renamed." Checking the sibling module's most recent equivalent redesign FIRST (before designing from scratch) turns a multi-hour exploration into a mechanical rename pass, and keeps the 2 dashboards' behavior genuinely consistent for users who work in both.
