- One real difference from Production's version, worth remembering: `ContractBoqPieceStatusHistory` has no location column, so "Location if available" in Recent Updates can only ever show the piece's CURRENT location (from the piece row itself), never a historical snapshot of where it was at that exact status change — documented on `RecentStoragePieceUpdate.currentLocation`'s own doc comment rather than left as a silent, easy-to-miss approximation.
- Page-level access intentionally kept the EXISTING `isExecutiveManagerOrAdminAccess` gate (not switched to `storage_delivery.read`) — the pre-existing placeholder page already had a two-tier shape (page-level gate, plus an inner `storage_delivery.read` check that only gated its one real feature, the "Piece Delivery" link). The redesign extended that same two-tier shape to the newly-real data sections rather than collapsing it to one gate, honoring "do not change permissions unless only read-only dashboard access requires existing permission usage."

## Redesigned Erection Dashboard for Piece Erection Flow (FMP-UI-34)

### The 3rd sibling dashboard, plus one genuinely new requirement: demote, don't remove (FMP-UI-34)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/erection-executive/` (`_lib/erection-dashboard-helpers.ts`, `_components/erection-contract-selector.tsx`, `_components/erection-needs-attention-panel.tsx`, `_components/erection-workflow-summary-card.tsx`, `page.tsx`); backend `apps/api/src/erection/erection-pieces.service.ts` (`contractProgress()`, `recentUpdates()`).
- Follows the exact same port from Production (FMP-UI-32) → Storage Yard & Delivery (FMP-UI-33) → Erection, field-renamed each time for Ready-for-Erection/Erected/Completed. Same selector architecture (no fetch on select), same Needs Attention shape (4 fixed rows, neutral at 0), same Work Queue ranking, same `?contractId=` link pattern (confirmed real before use, same as the other 2).
- The one genuinely new piece this module needed and the other 2 didn't: Erection already had an OLD, separate method-statement/checklist workflow dashboard (`ErectionWorkflowStatusDashboard`, shared with the full `/contracts/erection-dashboard` page) that this landing page used to render as its ENTIRE body. The ticket's own explicit "do not remove, but reduce it if it takes too much space" required a genuinely new component, `ErectionWorkflowSummaryCard` — 3 real figures (Contracts in workflow / Overdue workflow items / Waiting approval) read from the SAME `contractsApi.erectionDashboard()` call the old full component already consumed, no new query, plus one button to the full, completely untouched dashboard route.
- Reusable takeaway: when a dashboard redesign says "replace X but don't remove the thing X used to show," check whether the OLD thing's own data is already being fetched somewhere nearby before reaching for a new query — `ErectionDashboardKpis` already had exactly the 3 figures the ticket asked for, named almost identically (`totalErectionContracts`/`delayedAttentionRequired`/`submittedForApproval`), because the shared component this page used to render had already computed them.
- A real access-scoping bug, caught before finishing: `ErectionWorkflowSummaryCard` was first nested inside the page's `erection.read`-gated block, which would have hidden "old workflow remains accessible" behind a DIFFERENT permission than the one that data has always been gated on (`contracts.read`, the page's own top-level check). Reusable takeaway: when a page already branches on 2 different permissions for 2 different pieces of content, adding a NEW section needs to be placed in the branch matching ITS OWN data's real gate, not wherever it happens to sit visually in the design — check which fetch/permission check actually produced the data a new component renders, not just which section of the page it visually belongs in.

## Piece Flow Dashboard Consistency Polish (FMP-UI-35)

### DashboardKpiCard — the one KPI card for all 5 piece-flow dashboards (FMP-UI-35)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/dashboard-kpi-card.tsx`
- Replaces 5 near-identical inline `kpi.icon`/`toneClasses`/`valueToneClasses` blocks (Contract Management, Technical — which additionally had its own `technical-kpi-card.tsx` wrapper — Production, Storage, Erection) with one component: `{ label, value, icon, tone? }`, 4-tone palette (`neutral`/`warning`/`error`/`success`), `value: number | null | undefined` renders as "—" (so a permission-gated page can pass `null` instead of inventing its own ternary, as Storage's page previously did inline).
- Reusable takeaway: when the SAME ticket author describes 5 dashboards built unit-by-unit over several tickets (FMP-UI-29/31/32/33/34 here), each one's own small UI choices drift apart even when every ticket explicitly says "match the others' style" — because each dashboard was built by porting the PREVIOUS one's shape (see FMP-UI-33/34's own registry entries), not by sharing a component, so each copy could silently diverge after the fact. A later, explicit "make these consistent" ticket is the right time to promote the copied shape into one real shared component — don't promote on the first or second repeat, since the shape is often still settling; by the 4th-5th copy it has stabilized enough to extract safely.

### DashboardNeedsAttentionPanel — one row-list component, with a real empty state the per-dashboard copies never had (FMP-UI-35)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_components/dashboard-needs-attention-panel.tsx`
- Replaces 5 separate Needs Attention panel components (`technical/_components/needs-attention-panel.tsx`, `production/executive/_components/production-needs-attention-panel.tsx`, `executive/storage-delivery/_components/storage-needs-attention-panel.tsx`, `contracts/erection-executive/_components/erection-needs-attention-panel.tsx`, plus Contract Management's own inline markup) with one component: `{ rows: { label, value, tone: 'error'|'warning'|'neutral', href? }[], emptyMessage? }`. When every row's value is 0 it collapses to a single "No urgent items." success banner instead of listing 4 rows that all read "0" — none of the 5 original copies did this; each always rendered its full fixed row list regardless of whether anything actually needed attention, which is the exact kind of "always-visible noise" a Needs Attention panel shouldn't produce.
- `href` is optional per row, not per panel: Production/Storage/Erection's rows all link to their own Piece screen pre-filtered by `?statuses=`; Technical's rows render through the same component with no `href` at all, because its attention figures are BOQ-item counts rather than a single piece status — there is no one status filter a click could land on, so the honest choice is a plain non-clickable row, not a link to somewhere that wouldn't actually show the right thing.
- Do NOT confuse with `contracts/erection-dashboard/_components/erection-needs-attention-panel.tsx` — same-sounding name, but it belongs to the older, separate method-statement/checklist workflow dashboard at `/contracts/erection-dashboard` (outside this unit's scope, and outside all of FMP-UI-29/31/32/33/34's scope too) and was correctly left untouched.

### Each sibling module's own real status filter, not a shared guess (FMP-UI-35)
- Production's Needs Attention rows already linked to `/production/pieces?statuses=…` as of FMP-UI-32. This unit added the same `href` field to Storage's (`buildStorageNeedsAttentionRows` → `/storage-delivery/pieces?statuses=…`) and Erection's (`buildErectionNeedsAttentionRows` → `/erection/pieces?statuses=…`) own helpers — checked each target screen's own `_lib/*-pieces-helpers.ts`/`actions.ts` first to confirm `statuses` is a real, already-read query param on THAT screen (not assumed just because Production's screen reads it), continuing the "don't add a broken button" precedent FMP-UI-29/31/32 already established.
- Reusable takeaway: 3 sibling modules sharing one backend engine (`technical/boq-piece-screen.ts`) still each have their OWN piece screen/route/query-param implementation — confirm the param name on the actual target route per module, even when the pattern is already proven on a sibling, rather than copy-pasting the href shape blind.

### FMP-BOQ-11 — Drawing / Calculation Groups (Technical job page)
`DrawingGroupsSection` (`technical/jobs/[contractId]/_components/`), shown under BOQ Drawing Confirmation: one card per BOQ item with Pieces Generated / Assigned to Groups / Not Assigned / Approved Pieces / Released to Production, **Add Drawing Group** and **View Groups**. Each group row shows Drawing No, Calc ref, title, piece count, a status badge (Draft / Submitted / Approved / Released to Production / Revised / Cancelled), approved and released dates, **View Pieces**, and only the actions the status allows (Edit, Submit, Approve, Release to Production, Cancel). The add/edit form has BOQ Item, Drawing No, Calculation Ref, Group Title, Remarks and a piece picker (search by piece code, "Select all visible", selected count; pieces already in another group are greyed out with the group name). Technical's View Pieces list now shows "Group: HC-001 · CALC-001" or "Not assigned" for every piece. Wording/validation: `technical/_lib/drawing-group-helpers.ts`.

### FMP-BOQ-12 — Files on Drawing / Calculation Groups
Each group row in `DrawingGroupsSection` now shows its file count ("Files: 3 files", or an amber "No files attached." warning), **View Files (n)** and — for Technical writers while the group is Draft / Submitted / Approved — **Add Files**. Both open `DrawingGroupFilesModal`: an "Add File" box (Category: Drawing / Calculation / Approval Document / Other, File, optional Remarks, Upload; "PDF, Word, Excel or image (PNG, JPG). Maximum 25 MB."), then the file list (name, category · size · date · uploaded by, **Download**, **Remove**). Released groups show "Released groups cannot be changed." and no upload/remove. Technical's View Pieces list adds "· Files: n" next to each piece's group. Download goes through `/technical/jobs/[contractId]/drawing-groups/[groupId]/attachments/[attachmentId]/download`. Helpers in `technical/_lib/drawing-group-helpers.ts`.

### FMP-BOQ-13 — Released drawing files on the Piece Production screen
Each piece row now has a drawing line: a badge **Released to Production** (green) / **Not released to Production** (amber) / **No drawing group** (grey); for a released group also "HC-001 · CALC-001 · 2 files" and a **View Files** button (next to History). `ProductionDrawingFilesModal` ("Drawing / Calculation Files", read-only): piece code, contract / project, drawing no, calculation ref, group title, then the file list (name, category · size · date) with **Download**; empty = "No files attached."; there is no upload / edit / remove control. If a user ticks pieces that are not released and picks In Production or Produced, a non-blocking note appears: "Some selected pieces are not released to Production." Helpers: `production/pieces/_lib/production-drawing-helpers.ts`; download via `/production/pieces/[pieceId]/drawing-files/[attachmentId]/download`.

### FMP-BOQ-14 — Release requirement on the Piece Production update bar
When "Update selected to" is In Production or Produced, the bar shows the helper "Only released pieces can be marked In Production or Produced."; if any ticked piece is not released it adds the amber "Some selected pieces are not released to Production and will be skipped." Hold / Rejected show neither. The result message after Update Status comes from the API (e.g. "1 piece updated. 2 pieces skipped.").

### FMP-BOQ-15 — Technical release in BOQ Progress (Contract Detail)
Each BOQ item card now shows a flow strip **Confirmed → Generated → Released → Produced → Delivered → Completed** with a count per step, and a compact **Technical Release** box (Assigned to Groups / Not Assigned / Released to Production / Not Released, plus "No drawing groups created yet." or "No pieces released to Production yet." and "Production can start only after Technical release."). Totals row: Confirmed Pieces, Pieces Generated, Released to Production, Produced, Delivered, Completed, Needs Attention (Erected card removed; Erected still shows in the item tiles). Needs Attention reasons now include "N pieces not assigned" and "N pieces not released". New read-only **View Drawing Groups** popup (drawing no, calc ref, title, pieces, files, status, released date). View Pieces rows add Group status and Release (Released to Production / Not released / Not assigned) and use the group's drawing no and calc ref. No update, upload or remove controls.

### FMP-BOQ-16 — Technical release status on the Technical Dashboard
Selected Job Progress gets a compact **Technical Release** box: flow **Confirmed → Generated → Grouped → Files Attached → Released** (piece counts; Files Attached = pieces in groups that have at least one file), six counts (Pieces Generated, Assigned to Groups, Not Assigned, Released to Production, Not Released, Groups with No Files) and a plain note ("No pieces generated yet." / "No drawing groups created yet." / "No pieces released to Production yet."). Needs Attention adds **Pieces Not Assigned**, **Groups Not Released**, **Groups With No Files**. Next Action Focus (max 5) lists Technical release work first — confirmed pieces not generated, pieces not assigned to groups, groups with no files, submitted groups waiting approval, approved groups waiting release — then the existing workflow actions; empty state "All Technical release work is up to date." The jobs table adds a small line under the job name ("10 not assigned" / "No groups" / "50 generated / 25 released"). Read-only: every row is an Open link; no new buttons that change data.


### New User form + created-user screen (FMP-AUTH-01)
- New User wizard step 1 asks for **Full Name \***, **Email \*** (`name@recafco.com`, required, validated) and **Employee Number** (`EMP-001`). No Username field or username validation text; the API generates the internal username from the email.
- Created-user screen shows **Login Email** and **Temporary Password** (no username). "Copy Credentials" copies Login Email, Temporary Password, Role and Module Access via `buildCredentialsText` (`_components/credentials-text.ts`).
- Users list columns: Full Name, Email, Role, Status, Last login, Actions. Search matches name, email, username and employee number.

## Attachment View link + required-field markers (FMP-TECH-06)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/technical/_lib/attachment-helpers.ts`; route handler `.../drawing-received/attachments/[attachmentId]/open/route.ts`
- Purpose: "View" opens the inline file-serving route in a new tab (no frontend page — replaces the FMP-TECH-05 viewer page); TIFF rows show a small preview-may-not-work hint next to Download. `Field` in `drawing-received-form.tsx` takes `required` to render a `*`; missing-field errors render friendly labels via `friendlyMissingLabels()`.

## Edit Contract Details modal (FMP-CONTRACT-03)
- Path: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/contracts/_components/contract-edit-details-action.tsx` (+ `_lib/contract-basic-details-helpers.ts`)
- Purpose: secondary-style button + modal on Contract Overview (same overlay shape as `ContractCancelAction`) for safe basic fields only; helper text states BOQ/workflow/payments/progress are unchanged. Required `*`: Job Order No, Project / Contract Name, Client / Employer.
- FMP-CONTRACT-04: the Edit Contract Details modal also has an optional Schedule Status select (`SCHEDULE_STATUS_OPTIONS`, placeholder "Select schedule status").

## Access Mode, sidebar grouping and Module Access (FMP-ACCESS-01)
- Paths: `apps/web/src/app/(protected)/_lib/access-mode.ts` (derivation, landing path, sidebar layout, shared module names, Related Workflows links); `administration/users/_components/access-mode-config.ts` (template suggestions, wizard visible modules, step validation, Edit User grouping).
- Access Mode (Single Module / Multi-Module / Full Platform) is **presentation only**, derived from permission codes plus the one stored flag `User.fullPlatformAccess`. It never grants a permission and is not Super Admin.
- New User wizard step 3: template cards show Suggested Access Mode / Module Access / Role; an **Access Mode** selector (locked for Executive Manager, Viewer, Platform Admin, Erection Manager); Single → one **Primary Module**; Multi → **Modules** checkboxes (nothing pre-ticked); Full Platform → explicit, needs `access_scope.manage_all_departments`. Step 4 shows scope rows only for the modules the mode covers. Review and Copy Credentials show the Access Mode.
- Edit User → Module Access: **Access Mode** card (+ Grant/Remove Full Platform Access button for admins who may grant All Departments), then **Primary Module**, **Related Workflow Access**, **Modules**, **Other Module Access**, **Administration Access** (only if admin permission or a non-default stored row), and a collapsed "Modules this role does not grant". Every stored row is still shown somewhere.
- Sidebar: Contract Management Single Module users get a **Contract Management** group and a **Related Workflows** group (in execution order: Technical → Production & Planning → Storage Yard & Delivery → Erection → Schedule Planning; Production, Storage and Piece Erection show only with production.read, storage_delivery.read, erection.read). Other single-module users get only their module. Full Platform / Multi-Module / Admin users get the main sidebar whose first link is **Factory Operations Control Center**. Executive sidebar unchanged.
- `/dashboard` redirects a Single Module user to their module dashboard (`MODULE_LANDING_PATH`); everyone else stays on the Control Center.
- Module names used everywhere: Task Management, Incident Management, Maintenance Management, Safety & Compliance, Contract Management, Production & Planning, Administration (breadcrumb/page titles in factory-tasks, maintenance, production updated).

## Full Platform Access = view all modules (FMP-ACCESS-01C)
- A Full Platform Access user gets the executive-style full sidebar (Control Center landing, Contract Management, Estimation, Technical, Production & Planning, Storage Yard & Delivery, Erection, Quality Control, Safety & Compliance, Incident Management, Maintenance Management, Task Management, Schedule Planning). Administration appears only with real admin permissions.
- Single Module Contract Manager Related Workflows now also include **Task Management** (`tasks.read`) before Schedule Planning; every gated link still needs its own read permission.
- Read-only banner (`shouldShowReadOnlyBanner`, shown in `app-shell.tsx`): "You have full platform view access. Some actions are hidden because your role does not include update permission for this module." Only for Full Platform users, on a module page, when the role has no write-type permission in that module.

## Technical as its own module (FMP-ACCESS-02)
- Technical has its own permissions (technical.read / update / manage) and roles (Technical Staff, Technical Manager). Contract Management codes still open the same screens, so Contract Managers and Full Platform Access users are unaffected (`technical/_lib/technical-permissions.ts` on the web, `technical/technical-permissions.ts` on the API - keep in sync).
- A Technical-only user is Single Module Access with primary module **Technical**: /dashboard sends them to /technical, the sidebar shows a **Technical** section with one **Dashboard** link (no Contract Management workspace, no Schedule Planning).
- User creation: **Technical** is a Primary Module / Modules choice (Module Staff suggests Technical Staff, Module Manager suggests Technical Manager). Its department scope is stored on the Contract Management scope row, shown under the name **Technical**; the Edit User tab shows the same.
- Technical write buttons need technical.update (or the older contracts.update / contracts.workflow_update); deleting others files needs technical.manage / contracts.manage.
